    从架构的角度来看，批量归一化层独特而有趣，因为它是我们讨论过的第一个明确将不同样本耦合在一起的模型组件。以前，在标准的密集层或卷积层中，对样本 1 的操作与样本 2 完全独立。但在这里，标准化步骤需要计算整个小批量的均值和标准差，这意味着一张图像的处理输出直接取决于同一批次中碰巧存在的其他图像。在将其集成到深度神经网络中时，通常的惯例是将批量归一化层直接放在线性变换（如矩阵乘法或卷积）之后，但在非线性激活函数（如 ReLU）之前。我们在将其放入 ReLU 之前将其应用于 $XW + b$。这里有一个微小但精巧的细节：如果你将批量归一化放在线性层之后，该线性层中的偏置项 $b$ 就变得完全多余了。批量归一化步骤会简单地计算包含该恒定偏置的均值，并将其直接减去。因此，在使用批量归一化时，你可以省略前面线性层中的偏置项，从而节省参数，因为偏置的作用完全被批量归一化层本身内部的可训练 $\beta$ 参数接管了。现在，我们讨论的所有内容都适用于训练阶段，在训练阶段我们对小批量进行操作。但是在推理或测试期间会发生什么呢？在测试期间，我们通常一次处理一个数据点。不可能从单个样本中计算出有意义的批次均值或标准差。为了解决这个问题，在训练阶段，批量归一化层会默默地保留其遇到的批次均值 $\mu$ 和批次标准差 $\sigma$ 的滑动平均值。然后，当模型切换到推理模式时，它会停止即时计算批次统计信息。相反，它使用那些预先计算的滑动平均值来执行标准化步骤。这确保了模型在评估期间具有确定性行为，完全独立于可能同时通过网络的任何其他内容来处理测试样本。

  - [15:14:32 - 15:20:05] **Applying Batch Norm to Images and Alternatives**

    While analyzing exactly why Batch Normalization works is mathematically complex, intuitively, it normalizes the internal representations and breaks the strong interdependence of statistics across different layers. The training procedure itself is influenced because gradients actively flow back through the normalization step. For practical implementation, there is a specific nuance when applying Batch Normalization to images within Convolutional Neural Networks. For CNNs, you do not calculate a single mean and variance for the entire flat feature map, nor do you calculate them per pixel location. Instead, a common $\beta$ (shift) and $\gamma$ (scale) parameter is learned for each individual channel of the feature maps. So, if your feature map output has a shape of [Batch Size, Height, Width, Channels], the $\beta$ and $\gamma$ parameters will be vectors whose length equals the number of channels. The normalization is calculated across the batch dimension, the height, and the width, simultaneously. Why do we do this? It's fundamentally about what an image represents. We want to treat the entire image as a cohesive spatial unit. The relative scale between adjacent pixels horizontally or vertically is an important inherent feature of the image that we don't want to destroy. It does not make sense to independently rescale the top-left pixel differently from the center pixel. However, it is perfectly acceptable to rescale different feature channels—like an edge-detector channel versus a color-detector channel—independently of one another. As an aside on modern architectures, while Batch Normalization works exceptionally well for Convolutional Neural Networks, it has been less successful for sequence models like Transformers. For those architectures, maintaining scale across the batch dimension doesn't seem to be the primary concern. Instead, maintaining scale across the layer outputs for a single sample is more critical, which led to the popularity of Layer Normalization. There are other variants too, like Group Normalization, but they all share the fundamental concept of explicitly controlling the scale and mean of intermediate activations across some specific dimension of the tensor. For CNNs, Batch Norm remains the standard.

    虽然准确分析批量归一化为什么有效在数学上很复杂，但直观地说，它规范化了内部表示，并打破了跨不同层的统计数据的强相互依赖性。训练过程本身也会受到影响，因为梯度会积极地反向传播穿过归一化步骤。在实际实现中，当将批量归一化应用于卷积神经网络中的图像时，有一个特定的细微差别。对于 CNN，您既不计算整个扁平特征图的单个均值和方差，也不计算每个像素位置的均值和方差。相反，为特征图的每个单独通道学习一个公共的 $\beta$（平移）和 $\gamma$（缩放）参数。因此，如果您的特征图输出的形状为 [批量大小，高度，宽度，通道数]，则 $\beta$ 和 $\gamma$ 参数将是长度等于通道数的向量。同时在批次维度、高度和宽度上计算归一化。我们为什么要这样做？这从根本上关系到图像代表什么。我们希望将整个图像视为一个有凝聚力的空间单元。水平或垂直相邻像素之间的相对比例是图像的一个重要固有特征，我们不想破坏它。将左上角像素的缩放比例独立于中心像素的缩放比例是没有意义的。然而，独立地重新缩放不同的特征通道（例如边缘检测器通道与颜色检测器通道）是完全可以接受的。顺便提一下现代架构，虽然批量归一化对于卷积神经网络效果异常好，但它在 Transformer 等序列模型中的应用却不太成功。对于这些架构，维持批次维度的尺度似乎不是首要关注点。相反，维持单个样本跨层输出的尺度更为关键，这导致了层归一化 (Layer Normalization) 的流行。还有其他变体，比如组归一化 (Group Normalization)，但它们都共享在张量的某个特定维度上显式控制中间激活的尺度和均值的基本概念。对于 CNN 来说，批量归一化仍然是标准做法。

  - [15:20:05 - 15:26:35] **Data Augmentation and Invariance**

    Now let's switch gears and talk about data augmentation. You can think of data augmentation as another form of regularization technique aimed at reducing the generalization gap. Recall from statistical learning theory that the generalization gap—the difference between the empirical risk on your training data and the true population risk on unseen data—is fundamentally bounded by a term proportional to the complexity of the model divided by the number of training samples, $N$. To shrink this gap, you have two main levers: reduce model complexity (using techniques like L1/L2 regularization, dropout, or early stopping), or increase $N$, the size of your dataset. In reality, collecting significantly more raw data is often expensive or impossible. Data augmentation provides a clever workaround to artificially increase your dataset size. The core idea is to apply small, label-preserving perturbations to your existing training points to synthesize new, valid training examples. For image data, this might involve taking an image of a '7' and applying a slight rotation, a small horizontal translation, or a minor zoom. Because these perturbations are small, the label clearly remains a '7'. By feeding these modified versions to the network, we effectively expand the dataset and improve generalization. Beyond simply providing more data, augmentation serves another profound purpose: it is a way of injecting prior knowledge into the model. By applying specific transformations, we are explicitly forcing the model to learn that the true underlying function is invariant to those transformations. Creating a neural network architecture that is inherently and perfectly rotation or scale invariant is mathematically very difficult and computationally inefficient. The far easier approach is to use standard CNNs but augment the training data with rotated and scaled images. This forces the model to figure out the invariance on its own. However, you must apply augmentation carefully and thoughtfully. You cannot just apply arbitrary transformations blindly. For example, in the MNIST digit dataset, slight rotations are fine. But if you take an image of a '6' and rotate it 180 degrees, you create an image that looks exactly like a '9'. This completely changes the semantic label. If you do this without changing the training target, you will confuse the model. Data augmentation is a powerful and straightforward tool to introduce desired invariances, but the transformations must strictly preserve the true label of the data.

    现在让我们转换一下思路，谈谈数据增强。你可以把数据增强看作是另一种旨在减少泛化差距的正则化技术。回想一下统计学习理论，泛化差距——即训练数据上的经验风险与未见数据上的真实总体风险之间的差异——从根本上受到一个与模型复杂度成正比除以训练样本数 $N$ 的项的限制。为了缩小这个差距，你主要有两个杠杆：降低模型复杂度（使用诸如 L1/L2 正则化、Dropout 或提前停止等技术），或者增加 $N$，即数据集的大小。在现实中，收集大量更多的原始数据通常是昂贵甚至不可能的。数据增强提供了一种巧妙的变通方法来人为地增加数据集的大小。核心思想是对现有的训练数据点应用小的、保留标签的扰动，以合成新的、有效的训练示例。对于图像数据，这可能涉及获取数字“7”的图像并应用轻微旋转、小的水平平移或轻微缩放。因为这些扰动很小，所以标签显然仍然是“7”。通过将这些修改后的版本输入网络，我们有效地扩展了数据集并改善了泛化。除了仅仅提供更多数据之外，增强还有另一个深刻的目的：它是一种将先验知识注入模型的方法。通过应用特定的转换，我们明确地迫使模型去学习真实的潜在函数对这些转换是不变的。创建一个天生完美具有旋转或尺度不变性的神经网络架构在数学上非常困难，而且在计算上效率低下。更简单的方法是使用标准的 CNN，但用旋转和缩放的图像来增强训练数据。这迫使模型自己找出不变性。然而，您必须仔细而周到地应用增强。您不能盲目地应用任意转换。例如，在 MNIST 数字数据集中，轻微的旋转是可以的。但是，如果你拿一张“6”的图像并将其旋转 180 度，您创建的图像看起来就完全像一个“9”。这完全改变了语义标签。如果您在不改变训练目标的情况下这样做，您会使模型感到困惑。数据增强是一种引入所需不变性的强大而直接的工具，但这些转换必须严格保留数据的真实标签。

  - [15:26:35 - 15:31:13] **Learning Rate Decay and the Exponential Schedule**

    The final training technique we'll discuss today is learning rate decay. If you recall our analysis of stochastic gradient descent on a simple quadratic problem many lectures ago, we saw that SGD converges towards the minimum, but due to the stochastic noise introduced by mini-batches, it doesn't converge exactly to the optimal point. Instead, it bounces around the minimum, resulting in an error term proportional to the learning rate, epsilon ($\epsilon$). If epsilon is large, the initial convergence is very fast, but you end up with large, noisy fluctuations around the minimum. If epsilon is small, the convergence is slow, but the final fluctuations are tighter. The obvious solution is to dynamically adjust the learning rate over time: start with a high learning rate initially to move quickly towards the general area of the solution, and then progressively decay the learning rate to smaller values to reduce the fluctuations and settle precisely into the minimum. The theoretical conditions for convergence suggest that the sequence of learning rates $\epsilon_k$ should satisfy two rules: the sum of the learning rates from $k=0$ to infinity must diverge, but the sum of their squares must converge. A harmonic schedule, like $\epsilon_k = \frac{a}{1+bk}$, mathematically satisfies these conditions and is useful in pure optimization theory. However, in deep learning practice, the harmonic decay schedule is generally far too slow. We are not just trying to aggressively minimize the empirical training risk; we also care about training in a reasonable amount of time and finding solutions that generalize well. Instead, the most popular approach in deep learning is the exponential schedule. In an exponential decay schedule, you start with an initial learning rate $\epsilon_0$. You maintain this constant learning rate for a specific number of steps or epochs, denoted as $k_0$. After $k_0$ iterations, you decay the learning rate by multiplying it by a fraction, $\gamma$ (gamma), which is typically a value like 0.1 or 0.5. You keep it at this new level for another $k_0$ steps, then multiply by $\gamma$ again. Mathematically, it's defined as $\epsilon_k = \epsilon_0 \cdot \gamma^{\lfloor k / k_0 \rfloor}$. If you plot this schedule on a log scale, it creates a distinct step-function pattern that drops by a factor of $\gamma$ at regular intervals. While it doesn't strictly satisfy the theoretical summation bounds we just mentioned, the exponential step schedule is empirically highly effective and is the standard practice for training deep networks.

    今天我们要讨论的最后一个训练技术是学习率衰减。如果您回忆起我们在许多讲之前对简单二次问题上的随机梯度下降的分析，我们看到 SGD 收敛于最小值，但是由于小批量引入的随机噪声，它并没有准确地收敛到最佳点。相反，它在最小值周围反弹，导致与学习率 epsilon ($\epsilon$) 成正比的误差项。如果 epsilon 很大，初始收敛非常快，但你最终会在最小值附近产生巨大而嘈杂的波动。如果 epsilon 比较小，收敛会很慢，但是最终的波动会比较紧密。显而易见的解决方案是随时间动态调整学习率：最初以较高的学习率开始，以快速向解的大致区域移动，然后逐渐将学习率衰减到较小的值，以减少波动并精确地稳定在最小值上。收敛的理论条件表明，学习率序列 $\epsilon_k$ 应满足两条规则：从 $k=0$ 到无穷大的学习率总和必须发散，但它们的平方和必须收敛。像 $\epsilon_k = \frac{a}{1+bk}$ 这样的谐波调度在数学上满足这些条件，并且在纯优化理论中很有用。然而，在深度学习实践中，谐波衰减调度通常太慢了。我们不只是试图积极地最小化经验训练风险；我们还关心在合理的时间内进行训练并找到泛化良好的解决方案。相反，在深度学习中最流行的方法是指数调度。在指数衰减调度中，你从初始学习率 $\epsilon_0$ 开始。你将此恒定学习率保持特定的步数或时期（记为 $k_0$）。经过 $k_0$ 次迭代后，您通过将学习率乘以一个分数 $\gamma$ (gamma)（通常是 0.1 或 0.5 之类的值）来衰减学习率。你将它在这个新水平上再保持 $k_0$ 步，然后再乘以 $\gamma$。在数学上，它被定义为 $\epsilon_k = \epsilon_0 \cdot \gamma^{\lfloor k / k_0 \rfloor}$。如果在一个对数坐标下画出这个计划表，它会产生一个明显的阶梯函数模式，定期下降 $\gamma$ 倍。虽然它并不严格满足我们刚才提到的理论求和界限，但指数步进调度在经验上是非常有效的，并且是训练深度网络的标准做法。

  - [15:31:13 - 15:41:07] **Implementing Dropout, Batch Norm, and Decay in Keras**

    Let's transition to the practical demonstration and see how to implement all these techniques using Keras. First, let's finish the notebook from last week regarding the heart disease dataset. We established a baseline fully connected network that clearly overfit the data. We then tested L1 and L2 weight decay, which helped smooth the learning curves. To implement dropout, Keras provides a specific `Dropout` layer. You simply interleave these `Dropout` layers within your model's sequential definition. It acts purely as a structural modification. You must specify the dropout rate—the probability that a node is dropped—which I've somewhat aggressively set to 0.95 in this demo, though 0.5 is far more typical. Training with this high dropout rate drastically reduces the generalization gap, proving its strong regularizing effect, though the curves look a bit noisier due to the intense stochastic masking. Moving on to a more complex problem, we load the CIFAR-10 dataset, which consists of 50,000 colored RGB images across 10 categories. It's significantly harder than MNIST. After normalizing the pixel values to the [0,1] range, we build a baseline Convolutional Neural Network with three conv layers and three dense layers. Unsurprisingly, training this baseline model with no regularization results in severe overfitting, achieving only around 72% validation accuracy while the training accuracy approaches 100%. To fix this, we apply the suite of techniques we've learned. We already saw how to add `Dropout` layers. To implement Batch Normalization, we import the `BatchNormalization` layer. Following the standard convention, we want to insert the batch norm operation *between* the linear convolution and the ReLU activation. To do this in Keras, you must define the `Conv2D` layer without an activation function, then add the `BatchNormalization` layer, and finally explicitly add an `Activation('relu')` layer. We apply this pattern across all convolutional blocks. Finally, to implement the exponential learning rate decay schedule, we don't change the model architecture; we use a Keras Callback. We import the `LearningRateScheduler` and define a Python function that implements the exponential step logic: keeping the rate constant for 20 epochs, then halving it periodically. We pass this scheduler object to the `callbacks` list within the model's `fit` method. When we run this heavily modified model combining batch norm, dropout (specifically in the dense layers), and learning rate decay, the results are dramatic: the overfitting is suppressed, and the test accuracy jumps significantly to 84%, up from the baseline's 72%.

    让我们转到实践演示，看看如何使用 Keras 实现所有这些技术。首先，让我们完成上周关于心脏病数据集的笔记本。我们建立了一个基线全连接网络，该网络显然过度拟合了数据。然后我们测试了 L1 和 L2 权重衰减，这有助于平滑学习曲线。为了实现 Dropout，Keras 提供了一个特定的 `Dropout` 层。您只需将这些 `Dropout` 层交错放置在模型的顺序定义中即可。它纯粹作为结构修改起作用。您必须指定 Dropout 率——即节点被丢弃的概率——在这个演示中，我有些激进地将其设置为 0.95，尽管 0.5 要常见得多。以如此高的 Dropout 率进行训练大大缩小了泛化差距，证明了其强大的正则化效果，尽管由于强烈的随机掩蔽，曲线看起来有些嘈杂。转到一个更复杂的问题，我们加载 CIFAR-10 数据集，它包含 10 个类别的 50,000 张彩色 RGB 图像。它比 MNIST 难得多。在将像素值归一化到 [0,1] 范围后，我们构建了一个包含三个卷积层和三个密集层的基线卷积神经网络。毫不奇怪，在没有正则化的情况下训练这个基线模型会导致严重的过拟合，验证准确率仅达到 72% 左右，而训练准确率接近 100%。为了解决这个问题，我们应用了我们学到的一系列技术。我们已经看到了如何添加 `Dropout` 层。为了实现批量归一化，我们导入了 `BatchNormalization` 层。遵循标准惯例，我们希望将批量归一化操作插入到线性卷积和 ReLU 激活**之间**。为了在 Keras 中做到这一点，您必须定义一个没有激活函数的 `Conv2D` 层，然后添加 `BatchNormalization` 层，最后明确添加一个 `Activation('relu')` 层。我们将此模式应用于所有卷积块。最后，为了实现指数学习率衰减计划，我们不更改模型架构；我们使用 Keras 回调 (Callback)。我们导入 `LearningRateScheduler` 并定义一个实现指数步进逻辑的 Python 函数：将速率保持恒定 20 个时期，然后定期将其减半。我们将这个调度器对象传递给模型 `fit` 方法中的 `callbacks` 列表。当我们运行结合了批量归一化、Dropout（专门针对密集层）和学习率衰减的这个经过大量修改的模型时，结果是惊人的：过拟合被抑制了，测试准确率从基线的 72% 显著跃升至 84%。

  - [15:41:07 - 15:51:00] **Implementing Data Augmentation with ImageDataGenerator**

    Lastly, let's explore how to implement data augmentation, which is incredibly powerful. Let's step back and apply *only* data augmentation to our weak baseline model to see its isolated effect. The critical implementation detail here is that you do not want to pre-compute augmented images and save them to disk or memory. Generating and storing thousands of rotated and shifted copies of the dataset is incredibly inefficient and will quickly exhaust your RAM. Instead, we perform data augmentation "on the fly" during training using Keras's `ImageDataGenerator` class. We configure an instance of `ImageDataGenerator` with the transformations we want to allow: random rotations up to 15 degrees, horizontal and vertical shifts up to 10%, random scaling (zooming) up to 10%, and allowing horizontal mirroring. The generator takes these instructions and randomly applies these perturbations dynamically as it feeds mini-batches to the GPU. Because our CIFAR-10 dataset is small enough to fit in memory, we use the generator's `.flow()` method, passing it the `x_train` and `y_train` arrays directly. (If the dataset were massive, like ImageNet, we would use `.flow_from_directory()` to stream batches directly from the hard drive, as we've seen in previous examples). When we train the model, instead of passing the raw static arrays to the `model.fit()` function, we pass the data generator object. Even applying data augmentation completely on its own pushes the baseline model's test accuracy up to a very respectable 81%. Finally, we combine the ultimate model. We use the architecture that includes Batch Normalization throughout, but following empirical best practices for CNNs, we restrict Dropout solely to the final dense layers, as dropping features early in the spatial convolution layers often leads to underfitting. We train this robust architecture using the `ImageDataGenerator` for on-the-fly augmentation and apply the `LearningRateScheduler` callback to slowly step down the learning rate. Combining all these state-of-the-art techniques—Batch Norm, localized Dropout, Augmentation, and LR decay—yields our best result yet, pushing the validation accuracy to 86%. This demonstrates the additive power of these regularization and optimization strategies. We will take a break here until 4:00 PM, and when we return, we will discuss adversarial training and see how to completely break the model we just spent so much effort perfecting.

    最后，让我们探讨如何实现数据增强，这非常强大。让我们退一步，**仅**将数据增强应用于我们薄弱的基线模型，以查看其独立效果。这里关键的实现细节是，您不想预先计算增强的图像并将它们保存到磁盘或内存中。生成并存储成千上万个数据集的旋转和移位副本效率极低，并且会很快耗尽您的 RAM。相反，我们在训练期间使用 Keras 的 `ImageDataGenerator` 类“即时”执行数据增强。我们使用我们想要允许的变换来配置 `ImageDataGenerator` 的实例：高达 15 度的随机旋转，高达 10% 的水平和垂直移动，高达 10% 的随机缩放（放大），并允许水平镜像。生成器获取这些指令，并在向 GPU 馈送小批量时动态随机地应用这些扰动。由于我们的 CIFAR-10 数据集足够小，可以放入内存中，因此我们使用生成器的 `.flow()` 方法，直接将 `x_train` 和 `y_train` 数组传递给它。（如果数据集很大，比如 ImageNet，我们将使用 `.flow_from_directory()` 直接从硬盘中流式传输批次数据，正如我们在前面的例子中看到的那样）。当我们训练模型时，不是将原始静态数组传递给 `model.fit()` 函数，而是传递数据生成器对象。即使完全单独应用数据增强，也能将基线模型的测试准确率提升至非常可观的 81%。最后，我们组合最终的模型。我们使用自始至终包含批量归一化的架构，但遵循 CNN 的经验最佳实践，我们将 Dropout 仅限于最终的密集层，因为在空间卷积层早期丢弃特征通常会导致欠拟合。我们使用 `ImageDataGenerator` 进行即时增强来训练这个强大的架构，并应用 `LearningRateScheduler` 回调来慢慢降低学习率。结合所有这些最先进的技术——批量归一化、局部 Dropout、数据增强和学习率衰减——产生了我们迄今为止最好的结果，将验证准确率推高至 86%。这证明了这些正则化和优化策略的叠加威力。我们将在这里休息一下，直到下午 4:00，回来后，我们将讨论对抗性训练，看看如何彻底破坏我们刚刚花了这么多精力完善的模型。

  - [16:00:00 - 16:03:04] **Adversarial Training and Robustness**

    The goal of adversarial training is to dramatically improve the robustness of your model. When we have a classifier, what exactly do we mean by a "robust classifier"? We mean that the classifier head is robust if, given a normal input image $x$ and a small, bounded perturbation $x'$ that is close to $x$ in distance, the model's output for $x'$ remains essentially the same as its output for $x$. You can ask a similar question for regression models: if you make a tiny change to the input, does the output change drastically, or only by a proportionally small amount? To truly test robustness, we try to deliberately break the model. In general, you might want your model to just be robust to random Gaussian noise, but we are looking to add noise in a much more drastic and targeted way. We are adding noise that is explicitly trained to maximize the error and break your model. This is the core concept of finding adversarial examples. In normal model training, you adjust the network's parameters $\theta$ to *minimize* the loss function given the fixed input data. When finding adversarial examples, we flip this completely: we keep the model parameters $\theta$ fixed, and instead, we try to *maximize* the loss function by optimizing over the input space itself. Suppose you have an image $x$. I want to find another input $z$ that lies within some small distance $\delta$ of $x$, but crucially, $z$ maximizes the model's loss. That resulting image $z$ is our adversarial example, $x'$. It is the absolute worst possible image within that small radius $\delta$—"worst" meaning it maximizes the loss and causes the most incorrect prediction. You are searching for the worst possible small noise vector to add to your image. We will look at a demo of how to construct this in code later.

    对抗性训练的目标是显着提高模型的稳健性。当我们有一个分类器时，“稳健的分类器”到底是什么意思呢？我们的意思是，给定一个正常的输入图像 $x$ 和一个在距离上接近 $x$ 的小的有界扰动 $x'$，如果分类器头部是稳健的，那么模型对 $x'$ 的输出基本上与对 $x$ 的输出保持相同。您可以针对回归模型提出类似的问题：如果您对输入进行微小的改变，输出是会发生剧烈变化，还是仅发生按比例的小幅变化？为了真正测试稳健性，我们试图故意破坏模型。通常，您可能只希望您的模型对随机高斯噪声具有鲁棒性，但我们希望以更激烈和更有针对性的方式添加噪声。我们添加的噪声经过明确训练，以最大化误差并破坏您的模型。这是寻找对抗性示例的核心概念。在正常的模型训练中，您调整网络参数 $\theta$ 以**最小化**给定固定输入数据的损失函数。在寻找对抗性样本时，我们完全颠倒了这一点：我们保持模型参数 $\theta$ 固定，相反，我们试图通过在输入空间本身上进行优化来**最大化**损失函数。假设您有一张图像 $x$。我想找到另一个输入 $z$，它位于距离 $x$ 某个很小的距离 $\delta$ 内，但关键是，$z$ 使模型的损失最大化。生成的图像 $z$ 就是我们的对抗性样本 $x'$。它是该小半径 $\delta$ 内可能出现的最糟糕的图像——“最糟糕”意味着它最大化了损失并导致最不正确的预测。您正在寻找可能的最糟糕的小噪声向量以添加到您的图像中。稍后我们将看一个如何在代码中构造此向量的演示。

  - [16:03:04 - 16:10:49] **Generating Adversarial Examples using Gradient Tape**

    To actually find this worst-case perturbation, we are going to perform gradient ascent in the input space. Let's look at the demo to get a concrete feeling for what this means. We will take the CIFAR-10 model we previously trained using data augmentation—a model we consider quite good, achieving 84% test accuracy. First, we need a small technical fix: during initial training, we used arguments that implicitly handled the softmax activation, so we must explicitly reintroduce the softmax layer at the end of the loaded model for this analysis. Let's pick a random sample from the dataset. I have an image of a bird (an ostrich), and our trained model correctly predicts it is a bird with very high confidence. Now, let's try to break the model. To generate the adversarial example, we specify our loss function, which is cross-entropy. Interestingly, it is often practically helpful to start by adding a tiny bit of random noise to the original image before applying the targeted adversarial attack. We do that first. Next, we use TensorFlow's `GradientTape`. By default, automatic differentiation looks at the parameter space. However, by using the command `tape.watch(blurred_image)`, we explicitly tell the tape to compute gradients with respect to the input image space, not the weights. We compute the model's prediction on the input image, calculate the loss, and then ask the tape for the gradient of that loss with respect to the image pixels. If we were doing strict gradient ascent, we would simply take the perturbed image and add a small step size (epsilon, like 0.01) multiplied by this calculated gradient. Note that we *add* the gradient because we are doing gradient *ascent* to maximize the loss. After updating the image pixels, we must apply a clipping function (`clip_by_value`) to ensure all pixel values remain strictly bounded between 0 and 1, keeping the image valid. When we plot this newly perturbed adversarial image side-by-side with the original, to our human eyes, it still clearly looks like a bird. But when we feed this new adversarial example back into our supposedly good model, the model confidently predicts it is a frog. The model is highly susceptible to this targeted attack.

    为了实际找到这种最坏情况的扰动，我们将在输入空间中执行梯度上升。让我们看看演示以具体了解这意味着什么。我们将采用我们之前使用数据增强训练的 CIFAR-10 模型——我们认为这是一个相当好的模型，达到了 84% 的测试准确率。首先，我们需要一个小的技术修复：在初始训练期间，我们使用了隐式处理 softmax 激活的参数，因此我们必须在加载的模型末尾明确重新引入 softmax 层以进行此分析。让我们从数据集中随机选取一个样本。我有一张鸟（鸵鸟）的图像，我们训练好的模型以非常高的置信度正确预测它是一只鸟。现在，让我们尝试破坏模型。为了生成对抗样本，我们指定我们的损失函数，即交叉熵。有趣的是，在应用有针对性的对抗攻击之前，先向原始图像添加一点随机噪声通常在实践中很有帮助。我们首先这样做。接下来，我们使用 TensorFlow 的 `GradientTape`。默认情况下，自动微分着眼于参数空间。但是，通过使用命令 `tape.watch(blurred_image)`，我们明确告诉 tape 计算关于输入图像空间而不是权重的梯度。我们计算模型在输入图像上的预测，计算损失，然后向 tape 请求该损失相对于图像像素的梯度。如果我们进行严格的梯度上升，我们只需获取扰动图像并添加一个乘以这个计算出的梯度的小步长（epsilon，例如 0.01）。请注意，我们**添加**梯度是因为我们正在进行梯度**上升**以最大化损失。更新图像像素后，我们必须应用裁剪函数 (`clip_by_value`) 以确保所有像素值严格保持在 0 和 1 之间，从而保持图像有效。当我们将这个新扰动的对抗性图像与原始图像并排绘制时，在我们人类看来，它显然仍然像一只鸟。但是，当我们将这个新的对抗样本送回我们所谓的好模型时，模型自信地预测它是一只青蛙。该模型极易受到这种针对性攻击的影响。

  - [16:10:49 - 16:12:43] **The Fast Gradient Sign Method (FGSM)**

    I need to explain one specific detail I glossed over: the `sign(gradient)` function. In the seminal papers defining adversarial training, instead of just using the raw gradient vector for the ascent step, they propose using the mathematical *sign* of the gradient. Why do we do this? There are practical and theoretical reasons. Practically, it works remarkably well and is computationally faster. The gradient is a vector of real numbers. Taking the sign of that vector converts it into a vector containing only +1 or -1 for each coordinate, indicating solely the direction of the steepest ascent, ignoring the magnitude. You can think of this conceptually: I don't want to spend too much time carefully calibrating the perfect continuous gradient ascent step. I want to move as fast as possible to the extremes of the allowed perturbation boundary. If the gradient says "move slightly positive," I immediately push that pixel entirely to the maximum allowed positive perturbation. Theoretically, doing this is equivalent to trying to maximize the loss subject to an $L_\infty$ (L-infinity) norm constraint on the perturbation, rather than an $L_2$ norm constraint. An $L_\infty$ constraint strictly limits the maximum absolute amount of noise you can add to *any single pixel*, guaranteeing a uniform maximum noise bound across the entire image. If you used raw gradients (an $L_2$ approach), some pixels might receive massive, visually obvious perturbations while others receive none. By using the sign of the gradient, we force small, uniform noise across all pixels. Empirically, the human visual system is very good at ignoring small, uniform changes across an entire image. Thus, using the Fast Gradient Sign Method (FGSM) generates adversarial examples that successfully fool the model while remaining almost completely imperceptible to the human eye.

    我需要解释一个我刚才一笔带过的具体细节：`sign(gradient)` 函数。在定义对抗性训练的开创性论文中，他们建议使用梯度的数学**符号**，而不是仅仅使用原始梯度向量进行上升步骤。我们为什么要这样做？这有实践和理论上的原因。实际上，它的效果非常好，而且计算速度更快。梯度是一个实数向量。取该向量的符号会将其转换为一个仅包含每个坐标 +1 或 -1 的向量，仅指示最陡峭的上升方向，而忽略大小。您可以从概念上这样想：我不想花太多时间仔细校准完美的连续梯度上升步长。我希望尽快移动到允许的扰动边界的极端。如果梯度说“稍微向正移动”，我会立即将该像素完全推向允许的最大正扰动。理论上，这样做等同于试图在对扰动施加 $L_\infty$（L-无穷大）范数约束的情况下最大化损失，而不是 $L_2$ 范数约束。$L_\infty$ 约束严格限制了您可以添加到**任何单个像素**的最大绝对噪声量，保证整个图像具有统一的最大噪声边界。如果您使用原始梯度（一种 $L_2$ 方法），某些像素可能会受到大量、视觉上明显的扰动，而其他像素则完全没有。通过使用梯度的符号，我们在所有像素上施加了较小的、均匀的噪声。根据经验，人类视觉系统非常擅长忽略整个图像中微小、均匀的变化。因此，使用快速梯度符号方法 (FGSM) 生成的对抗样本成功欺骗了模型，同时人眼几乎完全察觉不到。

  - [16:12:43 - 16:26:08] **Adversarial Training Theory and Objective**

    Since our model is clearly susceptible to these attacks, how do we defend against them? The primary goal of adversarial training is to build a defense mechanism directly into the optimization process. Instead of the usual objective where we minimize the loss on the original data samples $x_i$, we fundamentally change the objective function: we want to train the model parameters $\theta$ to minimize the loss evaluated on the *adversarial examples*, $z_i$. The formal objective is a min-max problem: $\min_{\theta} \frac{1}{N} \sum_{i} \max_{z_i : ||z_i - x_i|| \le \delta} L(f(z_i; \theta), y_i)$. For every single data point $x_i$, we first run the inner maximization loop to find its worst-case adversarial perturbation $z_i$ within radius $\delta$. Then, we run the outer minimization loop to update the model weights $\theta$ so that the model correctly classifies that adversarial example $z_i$. You can view this as an extreme, targeted form of data augmentation: instead of adding random noise, we add the absolutely worst possible noise, and force the model to learn to ignore it. In practice, you perform these steps concurrently; you find the adversarial batch, take a gradient descent step on the weights, find the next adversarial batch, and so on. Let's analyze a simple 1D scalar model to see why this works. Imagine a model predicting $\hat{y} = \theta x$, and a single data point where $x=1$ and the true label $y=0$. We use a specialized loss function that is 0 if the prediction is between -1 and 1, and rises quadratically outside that band. With standard gradient descent starting from $\theta > 1$, the model will converge exactly to $\theta = 1$, yielding zero loss. However, this is brittle. If a tiny adversarial perturbation pushes $x$ slightly above 1, the prediction $\theta x$ exceeds 1, and the loss shoots up. Now, if we apply the adversarial training objective, we evaluate the loss at the worst-case perturbed point, which is $z = 1 + \delta$. The loss is now evaluated on $\theta(1+\delta)$. To achieve zero adversarial loss, we require the prediction at this worst-case point to be less than or equal to 1. This mathematically forces the learned parameter $\theta$ to be bounded: $\theta \le \frac{1}{1+\delta}$. By applying adversarial training with a noise radius $\delta$, we explicitly force the model weights to shrink closer to zero, creating a robust buffer zone that absorbs the adversarial perturbations without changing the classification.

    既然我们的模型显然容易受到这些攻击，我们该如何防御它们呢？对抗性训练的主要目标是将防御机制直接建立到优化过程中。与我们通常在原始数据样本 $x_i$ 上最小化损失的目标不同，我们从根本上改变了目标函数：我们希望训练模型参数 $\theta$，以最小化在**对抗样本** $z_i$ 上评估的损失。形式上的目标是一个最小-最大问题：$\min_{\theta} \frac{1}{N} \sum_{i} \max_{z_i : ||z_i - x_i|| \le \delta} L(f(z_i; \theta), y_i)$。对于每一个数据点 $x_i$，我们首先运行内部最大化循环，在半径 $\delta$ 内找到其最坏情况的对抗扰动 $z_i$。然后，我们运行外部最小化循环来更新模型权重 $\theta$，以便模型正确地对该对抗样本 $z_i$ 进行分类。您可以将其视为一种极端的、有针对性的数据增强形式：我们不再添加随机噪声，而是添加绝对最糟糕的噪声，并迫使模型学会忽略它。在实践中，您同时执行这些步骤；您找到对抗批次，在权重上采取梯度下降步长，找到下一个对抗批次，依此类推。让我们分析一个简单的 1D 标量模型来看看它为什么有效。想象一个预测 $\hat{y} = \theta x$ 的模型，以及一个单一的数据点，其中 $x=1$，真实标签 $y=0$。我们使用一种特殊的损失函数，如果预测在 -1 和 1 之间则为 0，并且在该频带之外呈二次上升。使用从 $\theta > 1$ 开始的标准梯度下降，模型将准确收敛到 $\theta = 1$，产生零损失。然而，这是脆弱的。如果微小的对抗性扰动将 $x$ 推至略高于 1，则预测 $\theta x$ 会超过 1，损失会急剧上升。现在，如果我们应用对抗性训练目标，我们将在最坏情况的扰动点 $z = 1 + \delta$ 评估损失。现在对 $\theta(1+\delta)$ 评估损失。为了实现零对抗损失，我们要求在这个最坏情况点的预测小于或等于 1。这在数学上迫使学习参数 $\theta$ 受到约束：$\theta \le \frac{1}{1+\delta}$。通过应用半径为 $\delta$ 噪声的对抗训练，我们明确地迫使模型权重收缩到更接近于零的值，从而创建一个稳健的缓冲区，该缓冲区吸收了对抗扰动而不改变分类。

  - [16:26:08 - 16:34:40] **Implementation using the Adversarial Robustness Toolbox**

    To implement this concurrent gradient ascent (for generating attacks) and gradient descent (for training) in Keras, we do not need to write the complex loops manually. We leverage the Adversarial Robustness Toolbox (ART). First, we define our standard Keras model, but importantly, we must wrap it using ART's model wrapper functionality. We pass the model and the `clip_values` (defining the valid image pixel range, typically 0 to 1) to the wrapper. Next, we specify the attack generation algorithm; we instantiate the `FastGradientMethod` from ART, linking it to the wrapped model and specifying the perturbation magnitude, `eps`. Then, we set up the `AdversarialTrainer`. We provide it with the wrapped model, our chosen FGSM attack method, and an important parameter called `ratio`. This `ratio` determines the proportion of adversarial examples versus clean examples in each training batch. Setting `ratio=1.0` means the model trains *exclusively* on adversarial examples, which is extreme but instructive for the demo. In a real-world scenario, you would likely use a smaller ratio (like 0.5) to maintain performance on clean data. The `AdversarialTrainer` handles the complex inner min-max loop entirely behind the scenes, automatically generating the worst-case examples and adjusting the model's loss function to perform adversarial minimization. We then call the `fit` method on the trainer object instead of the base model. After training completes, we test this newly hardened model by feeding it the exact same adversarial image (the "frog") that successfully fooled our previous network. The adversarially trained model easily survives the attack, correctly maintaining its prediction that the image is a bird with high probability. While you can experiment at home with larger perturbations to eventually break even this hardened model, it is demonstrably far more robust. One final practical remark: generating adversarial attacks for every single batch is computationally very expensive. You should only employ adversarial training if your specific application demands high security against malicious inputs, such as in autonomous driving or facial recognition systems. Otherwise, the computational cost may outweigh the benefits. That concludes our lecture. Please remember there is a practice quiz on Canvas to help you prepare for the upcoming test. See you next week.

    为了在 Keras 中实现这种并发梯度上升（用于生成攻击）和梯度下降（用于训练），我们不需要手动编写复杂的循环。我们利用对抗鲁棒性工具箱 (Adversarial Robustness Toolbox, ART)。首先，我们定义标准的 Keras 模型，但重要的是，我们必须使用 ART 的模型包装器功能对其进行包装。我们将模型和 `clip_values`（定义有效的图像像素范围，通常为 0 到 1）传递给包装器。接下来，我们指定攻击生成算法；我们实例化 ART 中的 `FastGradientMethod`，将其链接到包装的模型并指定扰动大小 `eps`。然后，我们设置 `AdversarialTrainer`。我们向它提供包装的模型、我们选择的 FGSM 攻击方法，以及一个称为 `ratio` 的重要参数。这个 `ratio` 决定了每个训练批次中对抗性样本与干净样本的比例。设置 `ratio=1.0` 意味着模型**完全**在对抗样本上训练，这很极端，但对于演示很有指导意义。在现实场景中，您可能会使用较小的比例（例如 0.5）以保持在干净数据上的性能。`AdversarialTrainer` 完全在幕后处理复杂的内部最小-最大循环，自动生成最坏情况的示例并调整模型的损失函数以执行对抗最小化。然后，我们在训练器对象上而不是基线模型上调用 `fit` 方法。训练完成后，我们通过向它输入完全相同的对抗图像（“青蛙”）来测试这个新强化的模型，该图像曾成功骗过我们之前的网络。经过对抗训练的模型轻松挺过了攻击，正确地保持了它高概率认为图像是一只鸟的预测。虽然您可以在家中尝试更大的扰动以最终甚至破坏这个强化的模型，但事实证明它要稳健得多。最后一个实用的评论：为每个批次生成对抗性攻击在计算上非常昂贵。只有当您的特定应用程序需要对恶意输入具有高安全性时（例如在自动驾驶或面部识别系统中），您才应该采用对抗性训练。否则，计算成本可能会超过收益。我们的讲座到此结束。请记住 Canvas 上有一个练习测验，可以帮助您为即将到来的测试做准备。下周见。
## Week 8
  - [14:00:00 - 14:12:43] **Introduction and Overview of Unsupervised Learning**

    Welcome to Lecture 8 of DSA 5204, Deep Learning and Applications. I am Dr. Low Yi Rui. Before we dive into today's material, I want to quickly remind everyone about the upcoming in-class test on the 4th of April. Please ensure you have Examsoft installed on your personal laptops—take note that iPads may not work for this, so you really need a proper laptop, and make sure it is fully charged before coming to class. The test format will be multiple-choice, multiple-response, or fill-in-the-integer questions, very much like the practice quiz you can find on Canvas. It is an open-book exam, but only hardcopies are allowed. No electronic devices are permitted other than the laptop you are using to take the test. The syllabus will cover everything up to and including today's lecture, Lecture 8. So, the test will take place on the 4th of April from 2:00 PM to 3:00 PM right here in LT26.

    Now, let us review what we have covered so far in the course. Up to this point, we have introduced several basic neural network architectures and discussed many techniques to improve their performance. However, our discussion has been heavily focused on supervised learning. In supervised learning, the goal is straightforward: given a set of inputs and their corresponding labels, such as pictures of cats and dogs explicitly labeled as "cat" or "dog," we want to learn a predictive model that approximates the true underlying function. We want our learned function to map the input data to the correct output label. Today, we are going to venture out of this familiar setting and step into the realms of semi-supervised and unsupervised learning. In unsupervised learning, we are dealing with situations where we simply do not have any labels. We only have the input data. For example, you might just have a massive collection of cat and dog photos, but nobody has gone through to tag which is which. Without these labels, our goal shifts. Instead of predicting a specific target, we are trying to uncover task-agnostic patterns hidden within the structure of the data itself.

  - [14:12:43 - 14:16:44] **Dimensionality Reduction and the Manifold Assumption**

    In unsupervised learning, all we have is a collection of images—or any other type of data—without any labels. Because there is no specific task like classification or regression yet, you are simply trying to learn interesting patterns from the data agnostic of any task. So, what are some examples of goals in unsupervised learning? One major example is dimensionality reduction. Imagine a dataset where points are plotted in a three-dimensional space, and let's say they represent two different classes, conceptually appearing as two separate cylinders. If your only goal is to tell these two groups apart, you do not actually need all three dimensions. You can flatten or project this data down from three dimensions to two, and eventually down to just a single dimension. By simply looking at that single, well-chosen dimension, you retain enough information to distinguish the two classes. This idea of dimensionality reduction is incredibly powerful.

    This leads us to a concept we will mention frequently: the manifold assumption. The manifold assumption basically states that high-dimensional data in the real world essentially lies on a low-dimensional curve or surface, which we call a manifold. Consider the task of recognizing a human face. A raw image consists of thousands or millions of pixels, representing a very high-dimensional space. But you have to ask yourself, how many fundamental degrees of freedom are there really in a human face? It is not millions. It is defined by a limited set of variables: the size of the eyes, the color of the eyes, the width of the nose, the spacing between the facial features, and so on. What we are saying is that there are only a limited number of essential variables that dictate the variations in faces, compared to the massive dimensionality of the raw pixel space. Therefore, faces effectively lie on a low-dimensional manifold embedded within that high-dimensional pixel space. If we can successfully reduce the dimensions and uncover this specific manifold, we will have learned something highly useful about the data structure.

    These concepts are highly interconnected. For example, if you are trying to build generative models—models designed to generate brand new, realistic faces—dimensionality reduction is a crucial ingredient. Since we established that faces are governed by a limited set of continuous variables lying on a low-dimensional manifold, if you manage to accurately learn this manifold representation, you can generate entirely new faces simply by taking random samples from within this low-dimensional space. Each random sample represents a specific combination of eye size, nose shape, etc., which the model then translates back into an image. In stark contrast, if you were to just randomly generate pixel values in the original high-dimensional space, the result would just look like television static; it would never spontaneously look like a human face. Hence, this idea of manifold learning is a cornerstone of modern unsupervised learning and generative tasks.

    在无监督学习中，我们所拥有的只是图像集合——或任何其他类型的数据——没有任何标签。因为目前还没有像分类或回归这样的具体任务，你只是试图从数据中学习一些与具体任务无关的有趣模式。那么，无监督学习目标的一些例子是什么呢？一个主要的例子是降维。想象一个数据集，其中的点绘制在三维空间中，假设它们代表两个不同的类别，在概念上表现为两个独立的圆柱体。如果你的唯一目标是区分这两个群体，你实际上不需要全部三个维度。你可以将这些数据从三维压扁或投影到二维，最终只需降到一个单一的维度。只要观察那个单一且选择恰当的维度，你就保留了足够的信息来区分这两个类别。这种降维的想法非常强大。

    这引出了我们将经常提到的一个概念：流形假设。流形假设基本上指出，现实世界中的高维数据本质上位于一个低维曲线或表面上，我们称之为流形。考虑识别人脸的任务。一张原始图像由数千或数百万个像素组成，代表一个非常高维的空间。但你必须问自己，人脸真正有多少个基本的自由度？绝对不是几百万个。它是由一组有限的变量决定的：眼睛的大小、眼睛的颜色、鼻子的宽度、面部特征之间的间距等等。我们要说的是，与巨大的原始像素空间维度相比，决定人脸变化的本质变量只有那么多。因此，人脸实际上位于嵌入在那个高维像素空间内的一个低维流形上。如果我们能够成功地降低维度并揭示这个特定的流形，我们就学到了关于数据结构的非常有用的一些东西。

    这些概念是高度相互关联的。例如，如果你试图构建生成模型——旨在生成全新的、逼真人脸的模型——降维是一个至关重要的组成部分。既然我们已经确定人脸受一组位于低维流形上的有限连续变量控制，那么如果你设法准确地学习到这个流形表示，你只需在这个低维空间内进行随机采样，就能生成全新的人脸。每次随机采样代表眼睛大小、鼻子形状等的一种特定组合，然后模型将其转换回图像。形成鲜明对比的是，如果你仅仅在原始的高维空间中随机生成像素值，结果只会像电视机的雪花噪点；它永远不会自发地看起来像一张人脸。因此，这种流形学习的思想是现代无监督学习和生成任务的基石。

  - [14:16:44 - 14:18:40] **Clustering and Density Estimation**

    Some other common tasks in unsupervised learning include clustering. We won't dwell too much on clustering in this specific course, but the basic idea is to discover how many distinct natural groupings or types of data exist within your dataset. Clustering operates on the intuitive assumption that data points which are geometrically close to each other in the feature space are likely similar and probably belong to the same class. Furthermore, we don't just rely on simple distance metrics; we also look at density measures. The idea is that meaningful clusters of data are typically separated by regions of relatively low data density.

    Another prime example of unsupervised learning is density estimation. In a density estimation problem, there is a true, underlying probability distribution—let's call it the blue curve—from which your data was generated, and your goal is to predict or approximate this curve. However, you don't get to see the true distribution; all you have access to are discrete instances drawn from it, which are the raw data points you observe. From these observed data points alone, you want to try to recover the shape of that underlying density function, perhaps resulting in an estimated curve, like a dotted line that loosely fits the distribution of your points. This process is what we refer to as density estimation. In many ways, density estimation serves as a foundational component for generative models. Conceptually, once you have successfully learned the underlying density function of your data, you could theoretically sample from this learned function to generate new, synthetic data points. However, in practice, they are not exactly the same thing. There are significant subtleties in implementation. For instance, not all learned density functions are mathematically easy to sample from computationally. Furthermore, for a good generative model, you don't just want a rigid representation; you want to ensure there is adequate variance and "creativity" in what is generated, rather than just memorizing and spitting back the exact training data.

    无监督学习中的一些其他常见任务包括聚类。我们在这门特定的课程中不会过多地探讨聚类，但其基本思想是发现数据集中存在多少个截然不同的自然分组或数据类型。聚类的运作基于一个直观的假设：在特征空间中几何距离相互靠近的数据点很可能是相似的，并且可能属于同一个类别。此外，我们不仅依赖于简单的距离度量；我们还会观察密度度量。其思想是，有意义的数据簇通常会被数据密度相对较低的区域分隔开来。

    无监督学习的另一个主要例子是密度估计。在密度估计问题中，存在一个真实的、潜在的概率分布——我们姑且称之为蓝色曲线——你的数据就是从这个分布中生成的，而你的目标是预测或近似这条曲线。然而，你无法看到真实的分布；你所能获取的只是从中提取的离散实例，即你观察到的原始数据点。仅凭这些观察到的数据点，你想尝试恢复那个潜在密度函数的形状，可能会得出一个估计曲线，就像一条松散拟合你数据点分布的虚线。这个过程就是我们所说的密度估计。在很多方面，密度估计可以作为生成模型的基础组件。从概念上讲，一旦你成功学习了数据的潜在密度函数，理论上你就可以从这个学到的函数中采样，以生成新的合成数据点。然而，在实践中，它们并不完全是一回事。在实现中存在着显著的微妙之处。例如，并非所有学到的密度函数在数学上都很容易进行计算采样。此外，对于一个优秀的生成模型，你不仅需要一个死板的表示；你还需要确保在生成的内容中有足够的方差和“创造力”，而不是仅仅记住并吐出完全一样的训练数据。

  - [14:18:40 - 14:21:03] **Reviewing Principal Component Analysis (PCA)**

    Let's do a quick review of dimensionality reduction, specifically focusing on what is perhaps the most basic yet most popular tool for this: Principal Component Analysis, or PCA. I'll just go through this briefly to ensure everyone is on the same page, in case you are not entirely familiar with it. Consider an example dataset where we are looking at features of shoes. Imagine we plot the size of a shoe, where the x-axis represents the US shoe size scale, and the y-axis represents the European shoe size scale. When you plot this, it technically looks like two-dimensional data. The points don't form a perfect single line because maybe there's some slight rounding error when converting between the two sizing systems. But fundamentally, conceptually, this data should really just be one-dimensional; it's just "shoe size."

    What you want to learn from this data is the single primary dimension that best describes it. The direction that visually makes the most sense as a line passing through the center of these points turns out to also be the direction of maximum variance in the data. I am not going to rigorously prove this in this course, but mathematically, the direction of maximum variance turns out to perfectly align with the direction that yields the minimal reconstruction error. Basically, if you project all your 2D data points onto this single best-fit line, you can calculate the error—the distance lost during the projection. As a mathematical fact, these two directions are absolutely identical: the direction that minimizes the reconstruction loss is exactly the same as the direction that maximizes the data's variance.

    So, this is the essence of Principal Component Analysis. You find this first principal direction—the direction of maximum variance. Naturally, in a 2D case, you also have a second principal direction, which is perfectly orthogonal to the first, and it corresponds to the direction of minimum variance. The overarching goal of the PCA algorithm is to take your input data and break it down into these different orthogonal directions, which we call principal components or principal directions. These components are strictly ordered according to how much variance they contain, meaning you arrange them from the highest variance component down to the lowest variance component.

    让我们快速回顾一下降维，特别关注一下这方面也许是最基本但最受欢迎的工具：主成分分析，或称 PCA。我将简要地过一遍这个内容，以防大家对它不够熟悉，确保我们的进度一致。考虑一个样本数据集，我们正在观察鞋子的特征。想象我们绘制鞋子的尺码，其中 x 轴代表美国鞋码标准，y 轴代表欧洲鞋码标准。当你把它们画出来时，它在技术上看起来像二维数据。这些点没有形成一条完美的直线，因为在两种尺码系统之间转换时可能存在一些轻微的舍入误差。但从根本上、概念上讲，这个数据实际上应该只是一维的；它仅仅就是“鞋码”。

    你想从这些数据中学习到的是最能描述它的单一主要维度。作为穿过这些点中心的一条线，视觉上最合理的那个方向，恰好也是数据中方差最大的方向。我不会在这门课上严格证明这一点，但在数学上，最大方差的方向与产生最小重构误差的方向是完全一致的。基本上，如果你把你所有的二维数据点投影到这条最佳拟合的单一直线上，你可以计算误差——即投影过程中丢失的距离。作为一个数学事实，这两个方向是绝对相同的：使重构损失最小化的方向，正是使数据方差最大化的方向。

    所以，这就是主成分分析的本质。你找到这第一个主方向——最大方差的方向。自然地，在二维情况下，你也有第二个主方向，它与第一个方向完全正交，对应于最小方差的方向。PCA 算法的总体目标是获取你的输入数据，并将其分解为这些不同的正交方向，我们称之为主成分或主方向。这些成分严格按照它们所包含的方差大小进行排序，这意味着你将它们从最高方差成分一路排列到最低方差成分。

  - [14:21:03 - 14:26:01] **The Mechanics of the PCA Algorithm and Compression**

    In the PCA algorithm, all you start with is the input. This is unsupervised, so there is no output label table. All your data is simply contained within an input matrix X, which has N rows (representing your N samples) and d columns (representing your d features). The first step is to compute the covariance matrix, denoted as S, which is equal to 1/N times X transpose times X. Once we have the covariance matrix S, we perform an eigen decomposition on it. Let's denote the eigen decomposition of the covariance matrix S as U Λ U^T.

    In this decomposition, the columns of the matrix U are the eigenvectors of the covariance matrix. We specifically call these eigenvectors the principal components of our data. The matrix in the middle, Λ, is a diagonal matrix. Let's call its diagonal entries λ_1, λ_2, all the way up to λ_d. These lambda values are known as the explained variances, and we will clearly see what that practically means in a moment. Conventionally, whenever we perform PCA, we dictate that these λ_i values are arranged in descending order. Because these λ_i are the eigenvalues corresponding to the eigenvectors of the covariance matrix, and because the covariance matrix S is symmetric, it guarantees that these eigenvalues are real numbers, which allows us to meaningfully sort them in descending order. Furthermore, since the covariance matrix is positive semi-definite, these eigenvalues are also guaranteed to be non-negative. So, the standard PCA convention is that both the eigenvectors and their corresponding eigenvalues are always sorted in descending order of the eigenvalues.

    With this setup, we can compute what we call the PC scores, or the dimensionally reduced version of our data. Basically, you compute a transformation Z, where Z equals X multiplied by U. And if you have Z and you want to go backward, you take Z and multiply it by U transpose to recover the original X. That is the fundamental relationship between the latent representation Z and the original data X. This recovery works beautifully because the matrix U is an orthogonal matrix. We know this because the covariance matrix has very nice mathematical properties—it's symmetric and positive semi-definite—so we can assume that its eigenvectors form an orthonormal basis.

    Now, how exactly do we use PCA to compress data or reduce its dimensionality? Here is how we do it in practice. You take the full eigenvector matrix U, but instead of using all its columns, you only retain the first m columns—the m eigenvectors corresponding to the largest eigenvalues. Let's call this truncated matrix U_m. We then take our original data X and multiply it by this matrix U_m. This operation yields a new matrix Z_m, which has dimensions N by m, where m is strictly less than the original dimension d. This resulting matrix Z_m is essentially your m-dimensional compressed representation of the original data.

    Whenever you perform compression, the immediate follow-up question is: how do we reconstruct the data, and crucially, how much information was lost in the process? To attempt to recover our original X, we take our compressed data Z_m and multiply it by U_m transpose. Recall that previously, with the full uncompressed Z, we multiplied by the full U transpose to get a perfect recovery. Now, we compute Z_m times U_m transpose, and this returns a reconstructed matrix of size N by d, which we will call X_m. The vital question we need to answer next is: what is the mathematical error of this reconstruction? What exactly is the difference between the original data matrix X and our reconstructed version X_m? We will derive this error explicitly now.

    在 PCA 算法中，你一开始拥有的只有输入。这是无监督的，所以没有输出标签表。你所有的数据都简单地包含在一个输入矩阵 X 中，它有 N 行（代表你的 N 个样本）和 d 列（代表你的 d 个特征）。第一步是计算协方差矩阵，记为 S，它等于 1/N 乘以 X 的转置乘以 X。一旦我们有了协方差矩阵 S，我们就对它进行特征分解。我们将协方差矩阵 S 的特征分解记为 U Λ U^T。

    在这个分解中，矩阵 U 的列是协方差矩阵的特征向量。我们特别将这些特征向量称为数据的主成分。中间的矩阵 Λ 是一个对角矩阵。我们将其对角线元素称为 λ_1、λ_2，一直到 λ_d。这些 lambda 值被称为解释方差，我们稍后会清楚地看到它在实际中意味着什么。按照惯例，每当我们执行 PCA 时，我们都规定这些 λ_i 值按降序排列。因为这些 λ_i 是对应于协方差矩阵特征向量的特征值，并且因为协方差矩阵 S 是对称的，它保证了这些特征值是实数，这允许我们有意义地将它们按降序排序。此外，由于协方差矩阵是半正定的，这些特征值也保证是非负的。所以，标准的 PCA 惯例是，特征向量及其对应的特征值总是按照特征值的降序排列。

    有了这个设定，我们就可以计算所谓的 PC 得分，或者说是我们数据的降维版本。基本上，你计算一个变换 Z，其中 Z 等于 X 乘以 U。如果你有了 Z 并且想反向推导，你用 Z 乘以 U 的转置就能恢复原始的 X。这就是潜在表示 Z 和原始数据 X 之间的基本关系。这种恢复之所以完美有效，是因为矩阵 U 是一个正交矩阵。我们知道这一点是因为协方差矩阵具有非常好的数学性质——它是对称且半正定的——所以我们可以假设其特征向量形成一个标准正交基。

    现在，我们究竟如何使用 PCA 来压缩数据或降低其维度呢？在实践中我们是这样做的。你取完整的特征向量矩阵 U，但你不再使用它的所有列，而是只保留前 m 列——即对应于最大特征值的 m 个特征向量。我们把这个截断的矩阵称为 U_m。然后，我们用原始数据 X 乘以这个矩阵 U_m。这个操作产生了一个新的矩阵 Z_m，它的维度是 N 乘 m，其中 m 严格小于原始维度 d。这个结果矩阵 Z_m 本质上就是原始数据的 m 维压缩表示。

    每当你执行压缩时，紧接着的后续问题是：我们如何重建数据？而且至关重要的是，在这个过程中丢失了多少信息？为了尝试恢复我们原始的 X，我们将压缩后的数据 Z_m 乘以 U_m 的转置。回想一下，之前对于完整未压缩的 Z，我们乘以完整的 U 转置以获得完美的恢复。现在，我们计算 Z_m 乘以 U_m 转置，这返回一个尺寸为 N 乘 d 的重构矩阵，我们称之为 X_m。我们接下来需要回答的关键问题是：这种重构的数学误差是多少？原始数据矩阵 X 和我们的重构版本 X_m 之间到底有什么区别？我们现在将显式地推导这个误差。

  - [14:26:01 - 14:44:07] **Deriving the Reconstruction Error in PCA**

    Let's formally write down the setting to find the reconstruction error for PCA. We have our data matrix X, an N by d matrix. The covariance matrix S is d by d. We diagonalize it so S = U Λ U^T. U is a matrix where each column corresponds to an eigenvector, and we are only interested in looking at the first m columns. We call this matrix U_m, and the eigenvectors u_j are chosen to be an orthonormal basis. We compress our data by multiplying X with U_m to get Z_m, an N by m matrix. To reconstruct the data, we calculate X_m = Z_m U_m^T, which expands to X_m = X U_m U_m^T. The core question is: what is the difference between X_m and X?

    Let's introduce some notation. Let x^(i) be the i-th data point, meaning the i-th row of the matrix X. Because the eigenvectors u_j form a complete orthonormal basis for the d-dimensional space, we can express the original vector x^(i) as a linear combination of these basis vectors: x^(i) = Σ_{j=1}^{d} (x^(i) dot u_j) u_j. Following this logic, we can also write out the formula for x_m^(i), which is the i-th row of the reconstructed matrix X_m. Since the reconstruction relies exclusively on the matrix U_m, it only uses the first m eigenvectors. You can verify through matrix multiplication that x_m^(i) is simply the truncated sum: x_m^(i) = Σ_{j=1}^{m} (x^(i) dot u_j) u_j.

    Now we have the mathematical expressions for both the original data point x^(i) and its reconstructed version x_m^(i). I want to compare these two to figure out the exact difference, so let's use the squared L2 norm to measure the error on a single point. We want to evaluate || x^(i) - x_m^(i) ||^2. Because we expressed both vectors using the exact same orthonormal basis u_j, computing the difference is exceptionally clean. The original x^(i) is a sum from 1 to d, and x_m^(i) is a sum from 1 to m. When you subtract them, the first m terms cancel out entirely, and you are left strictly with the tail end of the sum: x^(i) - x_m^(i) = Σ_{j=m+1}^{d} (x^(i) dot u_j) u_j. Since the basis vectors u_j are orthonormal, taking the squared L2 norm of this residual vector is simply equivalent to summing up the squares of its coefficients. Therefore, the squared error for this single data point is Σ_{j=m+1}^{d} (x^(i) dot u_j)^2.

    But we need the average error over all N samples in our dataset. To find the total average reconstruction error, we sum this individual error over all points i from 1 to N, and multiply by 1/N. So the average error is 1/N Σ_{i=1}^{N} Σ_{j=m+1}^{d} (x^(i) dot u_j)^2. Now, I am going to do two things to simplify this expression. First, I will rewrite the squared dot product (x^(i) dot u_j)^2 as (u_j^T x^(i))(x^(i)^T u_j). Both of these are just different, valid ways of writing the dot product. Second, I will swap the order of the summations. I'll pull the sum over j to the outside, and push the sum over i to the inside. Since u_j does not depend on the index i, we can pull it outside the inner sum. The equation becomes: Σ_{j=m+1}^{d} u_j^T [ 1/N Σ_{i=1}^{N} x^(i) x^(i)^T ] u_j.

    I hope you recognize the expression inside the square brackets. If you don't immediately see it, you can take it as a fact and verify it later: the term [ 1/N Σ_{i=1}^{N} x^(i) x^(i)^T ] is exactly equal to the covariance matrix S. As an aside, this specific way of writing the covariance matrix as a sum of outer products is extremely useful to know in practice. If you are dealing with a massive dataset where storing the entire covariance matrix in memory is prohibitively expensive, you don't have to load it all at once. You can store your data on a disk, stream in one data point x^(i) at a time, compute its outer product x^(i) x^(i)^T, add it to a running accumulator, and then discard x^(i) from memory before moving to the next point. This streaming algorithm is a highly efficient way to compute the covariance matrix for big data.

    Anyway, applying this identity, our average reconstruction error simplifies beautifully to: Σ_{j=m+1}^{d} u_j^T S u_j. Now, recall the fundamental definition of our vector u_j: it is an eigenvector of the covariance matrix S with the corresponding eigenvalue λ_j. This means that S u_j is identically equal to λ_j u_j. Substituting this in, the expression simplifies further to Σ_{j=m+1}^{d} λ_j (u_j^T u_j). And finally, because our eigenvectors are orthonormal, the dot product u_j^T u_j is simply equal to 1. The entire complex expression collapses down to a remarkably simple final result: the average reconstruction error is exactly Σ_{j=m+1}^{d} λ_j.

    让我们正式写下寻找 PCA 重构误差的设定。我们有数据矩阵 X，一个 N 乘 d 的矩阵。协方差矩阵 S 是 d 乘 d 的。我们将它对角化，所以 S = U Λ U^T。U 是一个矩阵，其每一列对应一个特征向量，我们只对前 m 列感兴趣。我们称这个矩阵为 U_m，且特征向量 u_j 被选为标准正交基。我们通过将 X 乘以 U_m 来压缩数据以获得 Z_m，这是一个 N 乘 m 的矩阵。为了重构数据，我们计算 X_m = Z_m U_m^T，它展开后就是 X_m = X U_m U_m^T。核心问题是：X_m 和 X 之间的差异是什么？

    让我们引入一些符号。设 x^(i) 为第 i 个数据点，即矩阵 X 的第 i 行。因为特征向量 u_j 构成了 d 维空间的完整标准正交基，我们可以将原始向量 x^(i) 表示为这些基向量的线性组合：x^(i) = Σ_{j=1}^{d} (x^(i) 与 u_j 的点积) u_j。遵循这个逻辑，我们也可以写出 x_m^(i) 的公式，它是重构矩阵 X_m 的第 i 行。既然重构完全依赖于矩阵 U_m，它就只使用了前 m 个特征向量。你可以通过矩阵乘法验证 x_m^(i) 就是截断求和：x_m^(i) = Σ_{j=1}^{m} (x^(i) 与 u_j 的点积) u_j。

    现在我们有了原始数据点 x^(i) 及其重构版本 x_m^(i) 的数学表达式。我想比较这两个点以弄清楚确切的差异，所以让我们使用平方 L2 范数来衡量单个点的误差。我们想要评估 || x^(i) - x_m^(i) ||^2。因为我们使用完全相同的标准正交基 u_j 来表示这两个向量，计算差异就变得极其简洁。原始的 x^(i) 是从 1 到 d 的求和，而 x_m^(i) 是从 1 到 m 的求和。当你将它们相减时，前 m 项完全抵消了，你严格地只剩下求和的尾部：x^(i) - x_m^(i) = Σ_{j=m+1}^{d} (x^(i) 与 u_j 的点积) u_j。既然基向量 u_j 是标准正交的，取这个残差向量的平方 L2 范数简单地等同于将它的系数的平方和相加。因此，这单个数据点的平方误差是 Σ_{j=m+1}^{d} (x^(i) 与 u_j 的点积)^2。

    但是我们需要数据集中所有 N 个样本的平均误差。为了找到总的平均重构误差，我们将这个个体误差在所有从 1 到 N 的点 i 上求和，并乘以 1/N。所以平均误差是 1/N Σ_{i=1}^{N} Σ_{j=m+1}^{d} (x^(i) 与 u_j 的点积)^2。现在，我将做两件事来简化这个表达式。首先，我将平方点积 (x^(i) 与 u_j 的点积)^2 重写为 (u_j^T x^(i))(x^(i)^T u_j)。这两个都只是书写点积的不同且有效的方式。其次，我将交换求和的顺序。我把关于 j 的求和移到外面，把关于 i 的求和推到里面。因为 u_j 不依赖于索引 i，我们可以把它提到内部求和的外面。等式变成：Σ_{j=m+1}^{d} u_j^T [ 1/N Σ_{i=1}^{N} x^(i) x^(i)^T ] u_j。

    我希望你们能认出方括号内的表达式。如果你没有立刻看出来，你可以把它当作一个事实并在之后验证：这个项 [ 1/N Σ_{i=1}^{N} x^(i) x^(i)^T ] 完全等于协方差矩阵 S。顺便说一句，这种将协方差矩阵写成外积之和的特定方式在实践中非常有用。如果你正在处理一个海量数据集，将整个协方差矩阵存储在内存中的成本高得令人望而却步，你不必一次性加载所有数据。你可以将数据存储在磁盘上，一次读入一个数据点 x^(i)，计算它的外积 x^(i) x^(i)^T，把它加到一个运行累加器中，然后在移动到下一个点之前将 x^(i) 从内存中丢弃。对于大数据来说，这种流式算法是计算协方差矩阵的一种高效方式。

    无论如何，应用这个恒等式后，我们的平均重构误差优美地简化为：Σ_{j=m+1}^{d} u_j^T S u_j。现在，回想一下我们向量 u_j 的基本定义：它是协方差矩阵 S 的一个特征向量，对应的特征值是 λ_j。这意味着 S u_j 恒等于 λ_j u_j。将其代入，表达式进一步简化为 Σ_{j=m+1}^{d} λ_j (u_j^T u_j)。最后，因为我们的特征向量是标准正交的，点积 u_j^T u_j 仅仅等于 1。整个复杂的表达式坍缩为一个非常简单的最终结果：平均重构误差完全就是 Σ_{j=m+1}^{d} λ_j。

  - [14:44:07 - 14:47:26] **Visualizing PCA Error and Optimality**

    What this conclusion means is that when we compress our data down to m dimensions, the reconstruction error can be neatly expressed as the sum of the last d minus m eigenvalues, which we also call the explained variances. Because we deliberately order these eigenvalues in descending order from largest to smallest, we are mathematically guaranteeing that we are only summing up the d minus m smallest possible values for our error. The variance associated with the first m largest eigenvalues is robustly retained in your compressed data; you only lose the information associated with the smaller ones. You can actually plot a curve of these sorted eigenvalues, and if you draw a line cutting the dimension from d down to m, the area or sum of the tail end of that curve perfectly represents your reconstruction error.

    We have successfully proven what the error is when you use PCA. But a valid question remains: is this approach actually the optimal way to reduce dimensionality? I am just going to give you some facts here; you might have seen the rigorous proofs in other math courses, but we won't prove it here. There is a fundamental mathematical theorem that tells us that if you compress data from X to X_m, and you are strictly only allowed to use linear operations—meaning you can only multiply X by a matrix of at most rank m—then the method that minimizes the reconstruction error in terms of the Frobenius norm of the matrix is definitively the PCA algorithm. Basically, you can take it as an established fact that PCA is the absolute optimal linear algorithm available to reduce dimension while maintaining as low a reconstruction error as mathematically possible.

    这个结论意味着，当我们将数据压缩到 m 维时，重构误差可以清晰地表示为最后 d 减 m 个特征值（我们也称之为解释方差）的总和。因为我们有意地将这些特征值从大到小按降序排列，我们在数学上保证了我们的误差仅仅是在求和 d 减 m 个尽可能小的值。与前 m 个最大特征值相关的方差被稳健地保留在你的压缩数据中；你失去的仅仅是与较小特征值相关的信息。你实际上可以绘制出这些排序后的特征值的曲线，如果你画一条线把维度从 d 截断到 m，那条曲线尾部的面积或总和就完美地代表了你的重构误差。

    我们已经成功证明了使用 PCA 时的误差是多少。但一个有效的问题依然存在：这种方法实际上是降维的最佳途径吗？我在这里只告诉你们一些事实；你们可能在其他数学课程中见过严谨的证明，但我们不会在这里证明它。有一个基础的数学定理告诉我们，如果你将数据从 X 压缩到 X_m，并且你严格只被允许使用线性操作——这意味着你只能将 X 乘以一个秩最多为 m 的矩阵——那么在最小化矩阵的 Frobenius 范数重构误差方面，PCA 算法绝对是使误差最小化的方法。基本上，你可以把它当作一个既定事实，即 PCA 是目前可用的绝对最佳的线性算法，它能在降低维度的同时，保持数学上尽可能低的重构误差。

  - [14:47:26 - 14:51:49] **Moving Beyond Linearity: Autoencoders**

    So, PCA is the most efficient linear algorithm to compress data. If PCA works well for your specific problem, then that is perfectly fine. However, its fundamental limitation is precisely that: you are strictly restricted to using linear functions for both the encoding (compression) and decoding (reconstruction) steps. If a linear transformation simply is not good enough to capture the complexities of your data, you need to look beyond it. This naturally leads us to autoencoders. You can think of autoencoders as the nonlinear counterpart to PCA. It follows the exact same philosophy of having a compression step and a reconstruction step.

    In PCA, our compression or "encoder" step is multiplying X by the matrix U_m, which maps the data from d dimensions down to m dimensions. The decoding step is taking that latent representation and multiplying it by U_m transpose to map it back up from m dimensions to d dimensions. If we want a nonlinear counterpart, we just replace these matrices with nonlinear functions. We introduce an encoder function f that maps X from d dimensions to m dimensions, and a decoder function g that maps the data back from m dimensions to d dimensions. A very natural way to obtain these complex nonlinear functions is to use fully connected neural networks. This is an extremely straightforward application because when you build a neural network, you have absolute control over the architecture and can easily specify the number of nodes in the output layer of each network to match your required dimensions. You just need to ensure that the encoder network f has an output dimension of m, and the decoder network g has an output dimension of d.

    In this context, the compressed m-dimensional space you are mapping into is called the latent space, and the actual output vector of the encoder f is called the latent variable or latent state. So now we have two neural networks, f and g. How do we train them? Just like PCA, we want to minimize the reconstruction error. We do this by passing our original data X through the encoder to get the latent variable f(X), and then passing that latent variable through the decoder to get the reconstructed version, which is mathematically g(f(X)). We then compare the original data X with the reconstructed data g(f(X)) using a standard loss function, such as the mean squared error loss. We then utilize backpropagation to train the weights of both neural networks f and g to aggressively minimize this loss. What is fascinating here is that even though we fundamentally do not have any human-provided labels in unsupervised learning, we have cleverly twisted the problem into something that operates exactly like a supervised learning framework, where the input data X itself serves as the target label we are trying to predict.

    因此，PCA 是压缩数据最有效率的线性算法。如果 PCA 在你的特定问题上效果很好，那完全没问题。然而，它的根本限制也恰恰在于此：在编码（压缩）和解码（重构）步骤中，你都被严格限制只能使用线性函数。如果线性变换完全不足以捕捉数据的复杂性，你就需要把目光投向线性之外。这自然地引出了自编码器。你可以把自编码器看作是 PCA 的非线性对应物。它遵循完全相同的哲学，即包含一个压缩步骤和一个重构步骤。

    在 PCA 中，我们的压缩或“编码器”步骤是将 X 乘以矩阵 U_m，这将数据从 d 维映射到 m 维。解码步骤是提取该潜在表示并将其乘以 U_m 转置，以将其从 m 维映射回 d 维。如果我们想要一个非线性的对应物，我们只需将这些矩阵替换为非线性函数。我们引入一个编码器函数 f，它将 X 从 d 维映射到 m 维；引入一个解码器函数 g，它将数据从 m 维映射回 d 维。获得这些复杂非线性函数的一种非常自然的方法是使用全连接神经网络。这是一个极其直接的应用，因为当你构建一个神经网络时，你对架构拥有绝对的控制权，并且可以轻松地指定每个网络输出层中的节点数量以匹配你所需的维度。你只需要确保编码器网络 f 的输出维度为 m，解码器网络 g 的输出维度为 d。

    在这个背景下，你映射到的压缩 m 维空间被称为潜在空间，而编码器 f 的实际输出向量被称为潜变量或潜在状态。所以现在我们有两个神经网络，f 和 g。我们如何训练它们呢？就像 PCA 一样，我们希望最小化重构误差。我们通过将原始数据 X 输入编码器以获得潜变量 f(X)，然后将该潜变量输入解码器以获得重构版本来实现这一点，在数学上这就是 g(f(X))。然后，我们使用标准损失函数（例如均方误差损失）来比较原始数据 X 和重构数据 g(f(X))。接着，我们利用反向传播来训练两个神经网络 f 和 g 的权重，以积极地最小化这个损失。这里令人着迷的是，尽管在无监督学习中我们从根本上没有任何人工提供的标签，但我们巧妙地将问题扭转成了一个运作起来完全像监督学习框架的形式，其中输入数据 X 本身就充当了我们试图预测的目标标签。

  - [14:51:49 - 14:54:57] **Types of Autoencoders: Undercomplete and Overcomplete**

    Let's talk a little bit about the different variants of autoencoders. The most intuitive and standard version is the undercomplete autoencoder. This is exactly what we have been discussing, where the latent dimension m is strictly smaller than the original data dimension d. Undercomplete autoencoders are very natural because you can directly think of them as an algorithm explicitly designed to compress data or reduce dimensionality. The core philosophy here is that if you genuinely believe your high-dimensional data actually lies on a much smaller, low-dimensional manifold, you build an undercomplete autoencoder to find it. I should briefly mention why learning this latent space is so valuable in practice. There is a well-known phenomenon in high-dimensional spaces where it becomes notoriously difficult to find meaningful mathematical relationships between points that are actually related conceptually. If you take the dot product between two related high-dimensional vectors, it is statistically unlikely you will get a high value; they are unlikely to be well-aligned. However, if you successfully reduce the data down to a well-learned latent dimension, the meaningful correlations between the data points often become much stronger and more mathematically obvious. An undercomplete autoencoder achieves exactly this goal.

    While undercomplete autoencoders are the most usual type, there are specific niche applications where you deliberately choose to build what we call an overcomplete autoencoder. In an overcomplete autoencoder, the latent dimension m is actually designed to be larger than the original dimension d. If you just set m greater than d and train it normally, the network might just trivially copy the input to the output without learning anything useful. Therefore, whenever you use an overcomplete autoencoder, you must enforce a strict regularization penalty during training to force the network to achieve specific goals rather than mere memorization. For instance, if the regularization term you apply to the overcomplete autoencoder is the L1 norm penalty, the model is known as a sparse autoencoder. By using L1 regularization, you are forcing the network to maintain a sparse activation in the latent layer, which effectively makes the network perform a form of feature selection—learning a very specific, sparse dictionary of features that describe the data.

    Another sophisticated example is the contractive autoencoder. In this variant, you apply a completely different mathematical penalty—specifically, a penalty based on the Frobenius norm of the gradient of the encoder function with respect to the input. Adding this gradient penalty forces the learned function to be highly robust and far less sensitive to small, incremental changes or noise in the input data. We actually saw a similar concept earlier in the course when we discussed that explicitly adding noise to the weights or inputs of a neural network acts as a form of gradient regularization.

    让我们来谈谈自编码器的不同变体。最直观和标准的版本是不完备自编码器。这正是我们一直在讨论的，即潜在维度 m 严格小于原始数据维度 d。不完备自编码器非常自然，因为你可以直接将它们视为明确设计用于压缩数据或降低维度的算法。这里的核心理念是，如果你真的相信你的高维数据实际上位于一个小得多的低维流形上，你就可以构建一个不完备自编码器来找到它。我应该简要提及为什么在实践中学习这个潜在空间如此有价值。在高维空间中有一个著名的现象，即在概念上实际上相关的点之间，找到有意义的数学关系变得众所周知的困难。如果你取两个相关的高维向量的点积，在统计上你不太可能得到一个高值；它们不太可能很好地对齐。然而，如果你成功地将数据降维到一个学得很好的潜在维度，数据点之间有意义的相关性通常会变得强得多，在数学上也更加明显。不完备自编码器正是实现了这个目标。

    虽然不完备自编码器是最常见的类型，但在某些特定的细分应用中，你会故意选择构建我们所谓的过完备自编码器。在过完备自编码器中，潜在维度 m 实际上被设计为大于原始维度 d。如果你只是设置 m 大于 d 并正常训练它，网络可能只是平凡地将输入复制到输出，而没有学到任何有用的东西。因此，每当你使用过完备自编码器时，你必须在训练期间强制执行严格的正则化惩罚，以迫使网络实现特定的目标，而不是仅仅是死记硬背。例如，如果你应用于过完备自编码器的正则化项是 L1 范数惩罚，该模型被称为稀疏自编码器。通过使用 L1 正则化，你迫使网络在潜在层中保持稀疏激活，这有效地使网络执行了一种形式的特征选择——学习一个非常具体的、描述数据的稀疏特征字典。

    另一个复杂的例子是收缩自编码器。在这个变体中，你应用了一种完全不同的数学惩罚——具体来说，是基于编码器函数相对于输入的梯度的 Frobenius 范数的惩罚。添加这种梯度惩罚迫使学到的函数具有高度的鲁棒性，并且对输入数据中微小的增量变化或噪声远不那么敏感。我们在课程前面其实见过类似的概念，当时我们讨论过显式地将噪声添加到神经网络的权重或输入中可以作为一种梯度正则化的形式。

  - [14:54:57 - 14:56:52] **Denoising Autoencoders and Conclusion**

    Autoencoders do not need to be used purely for dimensionality reduction; they can be cleverly adapted to achieve entirely different tasks. One fantastic example is turning an autoencoder into a powerful denoising algorithm. How do we make an autoencoder denoise data? Instead of simply feeding the original, clean data X into the network and asking it to reconstruct X to minimize the standard loss, we deliberately corrupt the input. We take our original data X, intentionally inject noise to create a perturbed, dirty version of the data, let's call it X_tilde. We then feed this noisy X_tilde into the autoencoder's input layer. However, we do not want the network to reconstruct the noise; we calculate the error loss by comparing the network's final output strictly against the original, uncorrupted clean data X. By forcing the neural network to minimize the discrepancy between the autoencoder's output given a noisy input and the clean target data, the model inherently learns how to successfully identify and strip away the noise from the signal. We will actually be implementing this specific algorithm in our practical session later. Once you complete this training process, your autoencoder functions as an excellent model that has learned to effectively denoise incoming data.

    Are there any questions about this? We have looked at these main variants of autoencoders, but I should mention there are also some other very interesting and useful architectures in practice. For instance, there is something called a sequence-to-sequence autoencoder. In this setup, you are dealing with a sequence of time-series data. Instead of standard feed-forward layers, you utilize a recurrent neural network (RNN) acting as the encoder to compress the sequence down into a latent state, which is then fed into another RNN acting as the decoder to reconstruct the sequence. This is a very interesting approach for temporal data.
