  - [16:23:03 - 16:27:56] **The Encoder: Factorized Gaussian and Log Variance**

    So that elegantly handles the decoder. The next logical thing we must do is specify the architectural model for our approximate encoder, Q_phi(z|x). Normally, for the encoder, you don't really deeply care what the raw mathematical structure of the latent output looks like in isolation, whereas for the decoder, whatever you produce absolutely needs to structurally look like the target image data. But for the latent variables themselves, we don't naturally have a strict physical structure they must conform to. Because of this freedom, we typically do not complicate the distribution structure. It will almost universally just be a simple factorized Gaussian model. By factorized Gaussian, we mean it is a multivariate Gaussian distribution that is strictly axis-aligned. Mathematically, this means that its covariance matrix is constrained to be a purely diagonal matrix; all the off-diagonal covariance terms are forcefully set to zero, implying no linear correlation between the different latent dimensions.

    The primary reason we strictly specify this factorized Gaussian structure for the encoder is because we absolutely need this distribution to possess the property of being computationally easy to reparameterize. We already saw the exact mathematical example of how to reparameterize a Gaussian earlier. So, we are simply going to make use of that exact same trick. What functionally happens is that the specific parameters of this Gaussian distribution—its mean vector and its diagonal covariance elements—will come directly from the outputs of our encoder neural network. This network explicitly takes the data 'x' as its input because the posterior distribution Q must be strictly conditioned on observing 'x'. The neural network acting on the pixels of 'x' crunches the data and spits out the exact mu and sigma parameters defining a specific Gaussian distribution in the latent space.

    Obviously, utilizing a factorized Gaussian perfectly allows us to execute the reparameterization trick very easily. For any specific latent dimension 'j', it is a 1D Gaussian distribution where the mean is mu_j and the standard deviation is sigma_j. So, we can seamlessly rewrite the latent coordinate z_j deterministically as: z_j = mu_j plus sigma_j multiplied by u_j, where u_j is simply a random value drawn freshly from a static standard normal distribution. Let me just explicitly point out one vital technicality regarding the network implementation here. In practice, we design the encoder network to output mu, which is fine, but for the variance component, we specifically configure the neural network to output the natural logarithm of the standard deviation, log(sigma), rather than outputting sigma directly. Why do we explicitly use the log of sigma here? Think about the mathematics of a standard deviation; it represents a physical spread, so it must be strictly a positive number. A neural network outputting raw linear values could easily predict a negative number, which would mathematically crash the Gaussian formula. The clever engineering idea is that I just want the neural network to output any unconstrained real number, and during the forward pass, I will mathematically exponentiate that output to definitively recover my actual standard deviation, sigma. Because the mathematical exponential of any real number is always strictly positive, this guarantees our standard deviation remains mathematically valid regardless of what the network outputs.

    Before we move on to the loss, someone might ask: we saw that the domain of the data strongly influenced the choice of the decoder distribution, driving us to use the Bernoulli model for pixels. But for the encoder, we are saying that we uniformly just choose a factorized Gaussian regardless. Where does the specific nature of the data actually influence the encoder? The influence fundamentally dictates the architectural choice of the neural network you place inside the encoder. For example, since 'x' is spatial image data, you are vastly better off using a Convolutional Neural Network inside the encoder to extract the mu and log-sigma parameters, rather than simple dense layers. The CNN is where you aggressively input your domain knowledge regarding spatial translation invariance. The statistical distribution stays Gaussian, but the feature extraction engine feeding it becomes specialized.

    这样解码器的部分就完美解决了。我们接下来必须要做的合乎逻辑的事情是，为我们的近似编码器 Q_phi(z|x) 指定架构模型。通常，对于编码器，你并不是特别关心孤立的潜在输出的原始数学结构到底是什么样子，而对于解码器来说，无论你产生什么，在结构上都必须看起来像目标图像数据。但对于潜在变量本身，我们本来就没有一个它们必须符合的严格物理结构。因为这种自由度，我们通常不会把分布结构复杂化。它几乎普遍就是一种简单的因子化高斯模型。因子化高斯的意思是，它是一个严格与坐标轴对齐的多元高斯分布。在数学上，这意味着它的协方差矩阵被限制为一个纯对角矩阵；所有非对角线上的协方差项都被强制设为零，这意味着不同的潜在维度之间没有线性相关性。

    我们为编码器严格指定这种因子化高斯结构的主要原因是，我们绝对需要这种分布具备易于计算和重参数化的属性。我们之前已经看到了如何对高斯分布进行重参数化的确切数学示例。所以，我们只是要利用完全相同的技巧。在功能上发生的情况是，这个高斯分布的特定参数——它的均值向量和它的对角协方差元素——将直接来自我们编码器神经网络的输出。这个网络明确地将数据 'x' 作为它的输入，因为后验分布 Q 必须严格以观察到 'x' 为条件。作用于 'x' 像素的神经网络处理数据，并吐出确切的 mu 和 sigma 参数，这些参数定义了潜在空间中特定的高斯分布。

    显然，利用因子化高斯完美地允许我们非常容易地执行重参数化技巧。对于任何特定的潜在维度 'j'，它是一个一维高斯分布，其中均值是 mu_j，标准差是 sigma_j。因此，我们可以无缝地将潜在坐标 z_j 确定性地重写为：z_j = mu_j 加上 sigma_j 乘以 u_j，其中 u_j 仅仅是从静态标准正态分布中新鲜抽取的一个随机值。让我在网络实现方面明确指出一个至关重要的技术细节。在实践中，我们设计编码器网络输出 mu，这没问题，但对于方差部分，我们专门配置神经网络输出标准差的自然对数 log(sigma)，而不是直接输出 sigma。为什么我们在这里明确使用 sigma 的对数呢？想一想标准差的数学性质；它代表物理扩散范围，所以它必须严格是一个正数。输出原始线性值的神经网络很容易预测出一个负数，这在数学上会导致高斯公式崩溃。巧妙的工程理念是，我只是想让神经网络输出任何不受约束的实数，在正向传播期间，我将在数学上对该输出取指数，以确定性地恢复我实际的标准差 sigma。因为任何实数的数学指数总是严格为正，这保证了我们的标准差在数学上保持有效，无论网络输出什么。

    在我们继续讨论损失之前，有人可能会问：我们看到数据的领域强烈影响了解码器分布的选择，驱使我们对像素使用伯努利模型。但对于编码器，我们说无论如何我们都统一选择因子化高斯分布。数据的特定性质实际上在哪里影响编码器呢？这种影响从根本上决定了你放在编码器内部的神经网络的架构选择。例如，因为 'x' 是空间图像数据，所以在编码器内部使用卷积神经网络来提取 mu 和 log-sigma 参数，要比使用简单的密集层好得多。CNN 是你积极输入关于空间平移不变性的领域知识的地方。统计分布保持为高斯分布，但为其提供数据的特征提取引擎变得专业化了。

  - [16:27:56 - 16:42:02] **Simplifying the ELBO Loss and KL Divergence**

    Now that we have firmly established our network distributions, what we must do now is mathematically unify everything to derive the final, computable ELBO loss function. Let me write this out step-by-step so we don't go too fast. Recall the abstract definition of the ELBO loss we derived earlier. I can cleanly rewrite the numerator inside the expectation using Bayes' theorem. The joint probability, p_theta(x,z), expands out to p_theta(x|z) multiplied by the prior p_theta(z). When we substitute that expansion into the ELBO, we suddenly have the logarithm of a product of three distinct distribution terms. Following the fundamental rules of logarithms, this elegantly splits into the sum of three distinct log expectations.

    We just extensively analyzed the first of those three terms, the expectation of log p_theta(x|z). That evaluates exactly to the negative Binary Cross Entropy loss. So that part is securely locked in. We are now mathematically left to figure out the algebraic forms of the remaining two terms: the expectation of log p_theta(z), which evaluates our standard normal prior, and the expectation of negative log Q_phi(z|x), which evaluates our encoder posterior. To compute these, we critically rely on the reparameterization trick we just defined. We substitute 'z' with its deterministic equivalent, mu plus sigma times 'u'. Because we are dealing with factorized Gaussians, the math is tedious but surprisingly straightforward. I won't write out every single algebraic line manually here since the pen is glitching, but I will walk you through the structural logic. It is an excellent homework exercise for you to algebraically derive this yourself to verify.

    When you expand the log of the Gaussian PDF for the prior p_theta(z), you get terms involving z_j squared. Substituting our reparameterization, this expands to (mu_j + sigma_j * u_j)^2. When you distribute that square, you get mu_j^2, a cross term of 2 * mu_j * sigma_j * u_j, and a final term of sigma_j^2 * u_j^2. Now, here is where the magic of the expectation over the standard normal variable 'u' heavily simplifies things. The expectation of the cross term contains the expectation of 'u'. Since 'u' is a standard normal variable, its mathematical mean is strictly zero, so that entire cross term algebraically vanishes to zero. The expectation of the u_j^2 term evaluates exactly to its variance, which is strictly 1. Therefore, the expectation of the prior's exponent term beautifully simplifies to just mu_j^2 plus sigma_j^2.

    Similarly, when you evaluate the log Q_phi term representing the encoder, taking the expectation over 'u' causes the exponent to evaluate to exactly 1. We also aggressively throw away all constant scalar terms, like log(2 * pi), because they are mathematical constants. Their derivative with respect to any parameter is absolute zero, so they have zero impact on backpropagation and gradient descent. After throwing away all the constant garbage and combining the surviving algebraic terms from the prior and the posterior, the remaining components merge mathematically. At the end of the day, when you combine everything, the final negative ELBO loss equation we want to minimize looks like this: You have the Binary Cross Entropy term, plus 0.5 multiplied by the sum over all latent dimensions j of (mu_j^2 + sigma_j^2 - 1 - 2 * log(sigma_j)). The constants are handled slightly differently in the standard implementation, leaving you with the core variables: mu squared, sigma squared, and the log of sigma.

    If you stare at those final aggregated terms, you will realize that mathematically, those remaining terms collectively represent the exact closed-form analytical equation for the KL Divergence between our encoder's Gaussian posterior Q_phi and the standard normal prior P_theta(z). What is this KL divergence term intuitively doing to the network? The BCE term is desperately trying to minimize reconstruction loss, forcing the latent point to be highly precise to recreate the image perfectly. However, the KL divergence term is acting as a powerful mathematical regularizer. It actively penalizes the network if the encoder's output distribution drifts too far away from a smooth, centered, standard normal distribution. It prevents the network from memorizing the data by collapsing the variance to zero and creating those isolated, jagged points we saw in the standard autoencoder. It forces the clusters to expand and overlap smoothly. In fact, in advanced practice, we often inject a custom scalar weight parameter in front of this KL divergence term. If you want more visual variety and smoother interpolations in your generated samples, you can artificially give a bigger weight to the KL divergence term, forcing stricter adherence to the continuous normal prior. You can read the original VAE paper for more profound details on that balancing act.

    既然我们已经牢牢确立了我们的网络分布，我们现在必须要做的就是在数学上统一一切，以推导出最终的、可计算的 ELBO 损失函数。让我一步一步地写出来，这样我们就不会走得太快。回想一下我们之前推导出的 ELBO 损失的抽象定义。我可以使用贝叶斯定理干净利落地重写期望内部的分子。联合概率 p_theta(x,z) 展开为 p_theta(x|z) 乘以先验 p_theta(z)。当我们把这个展开式代入 ELBO 时，我们突然就得到了三个不同分布项乘积的对数。根据对数的基本法则，这优雅地拆分为三个独立对数期望的总和。

    我们刚刚深入分析了这三项中的第一项，即 log p_theta(x|z) 的期望。它的计算结果恰好是负的二元交叉熵损失。所以这部分已经牢牢锁定了。我们现在在数学上需要弄清楚剩下两项的代数形式：评估我们标准正态先验的 log p_theta(z) 的期望，以及评估我们编码器后验的负 log Q_phi(z|x) 的期望。为了计算这些，我们关键要依赖我们刚刚定义的重参数化技巧。我们用其等价的确定性公式 mu 加上 sigma 乘以 'u' 来替换 'z'。因为我们处理的是因子化高斯分布，数学运算很繁琐但出奇地直接。因为笔有点问题，我不会在这里手动写出每一行代数式，但我会带你们理清结构逻辑。对于你们来说，自己进行代数推导来验证这是一个极好的课后练习。

    当你展开先验 p_theta(z) 的高斯 PDF 对数时，你会得到包含 z_j 平方的项。代入我们的重参数化公式，它展开为 (mu_j + sigma_j * u_j)^2。当你把这个平方展开时，你得到 mu_j^2，一个 2 * mu_j * sigma_j * u_j 的交叉项，以及最后一项 sigma_j^2 * u_j^2。现在，这就是关于标准正态变量 'u' 的期望发挥魔力极大地简化事情的地方。交叉项的期望包含了 'u' 的期望。因为 'u' 是一个标准正态变量，它的数学均值严格为零，所以整个交叉项在代数上抵消为零。u_j^2 项的期望的计算结果恰好是它的方差，严格来说就是 1。因此，先验指数项的期望极其漂亮地简化为 mu_j^2 加上 sigma_j^2。

    同样，当你评估代表编码器的 log Q_phi 项时，对 'u' 求期望会导致指数计算结果精确为 1。我们还大刀阔斧地丢弃了所有的常数标量项，比如 log(2 * pi)，因为它们是数学常数。它们关于任何参数的导数绝对为零，所以它们对反向传播和梯度下降没有丝毫影响。在丢弃所有常数垃圾并合并先验和后验中幸存的代数项之后，剩余的组件在数学上合并了。归根结底，当你把所有东西结合起来时，我们想要最小化的最终负 ELBO 损失方程看起来像这样：你有二元交叉熵项，加上 0.5 乘以所有潜在维度 j 上 (mu_j^2 + sigma_j^2 - 1 - 2 * log(sigma_j)) 的总和。常数在标准实现中的处理方式略有不同，留给你的是核心变量：mu 的平方、sigma 的平方以及 sigma 的对数。

    如果你盯着那些最终聚合的项看，你会意识到在数学上，那些剩余的项集体代表了我们编码器的高斯后验 Q_phi 与标准正态先验 P_theta(z) 之间 KL 散度的精确闭式解析方程。直观地看，这个 KL 散度项对网络起什么作用呢？BCE 项拼命试图最小化重建损失，迫使潜在点高度精确以完美重建图像。然而，KL 散度项充当了一个强大的数学正则化器。如果编码器的输出分布偏离平滑、居中的标准正态分布太远，它会积极地惩罚网络。它防止网络通过将方差折叠为零并创建我们在标准自编码器中看到的那些孤立、锯齿状的点来死记硬背数据。它迫使集群扩展并平滑重叠。事实上，在高级实践中，我们经常在这个 KL 散度项前面注入一个自定义的标量权重参数。如果你希望生成的样本具有更多的视觉多样性和更平滑的插值，你可以人为地赋予 KL 散度项更大的权重，强制它更严格地遵守连续的正态先验。你可以阅读原始的 VAE 论文，了解更多关于这种平衡行为的深刻细节。

  - [16:42:02 - 16:53:46] **Code Demo: Implementing the VAE in TensorFlow**

    To quickly summarize before we code: the reason we mandate Variational Autoencoders over standard ones is because VAEs optimize abstract distributions rather than rigid point-to-point mappings. This inherently forces a smoother, more mathematically continuous latent space that perfectly correlates with semantic labels, enabling robust data generation. Let's look at a concrete PyTorch or TensorFlow demo of how to implement this architecture. It is actually surprisingly short. The primary engineering challenge is simply writing out that custom ELBO loss mathematically.

    Let's build the VAE encoder first. We just established we want a factorized Gaussian model Q_phi. Our neural network needs to take an image input 'x', flatten it, and route it through fully connected dense layers. Crucially, the network splits into two distinct output vectors. We will explicitly define variable 'y1' to represent the mean vector, mu, and variable 'y2' to explicitly represent the log of the standard deviation. We mathematically guarantee a positive standard deviation later by taking the exponent of y2. Next, we build a custom sampling layer specifically to execute the reparameterization trick. Inside this layer, we systematically generate a tensor 'u' entirely populated by random samples drawn strictly from a standard normal distribution. We then deterministically calculate our latent coordinate 'z' by implementing the exact formula: z = y1 + exp(y2) * u. To enable backpropagation through the loss, we explicitly configure the encoder model to output a tuple containing the final latent sample 'z', along with the raw 'y1' (mu) and 'y2' (log_sigma) tensors.

    For the generative decoder, P_theta, we construct a sequential network that takes that 2D latent sample 'z' as its strict input. It passes it through dense hidden layers, gradually expanding the dimensionality until the final output layer structurally reshapes it into a 784-dimensional vector, representing the 28 by 28 image pixels. As demanded by our factorized Bernoulli derivation, we strictly apply a sigmoid activation on this final layer to ensure every single element acts as a valid, bounded success probability between 0 and 1. The complete, overarching VAE model is simply initialized by composing the decoder onto the output of the encoder.

    The final critical step is programming the custom loss function to minimize the negative ELBO. We manually code the two distinct mathematical parts. First, the reconstruction loss, which is the standard Binary Cross Entropy between the input image and the decoder's sigmoid output. Second, we code the KL divergence regularization formula utilizing the 'y1' and 'y2' tensors we saved from the encoder. Here is an absolutely vital implementation detail you must catch: by default, deep learning frameworks like TensorFlow calculate the Binary Cross Entropy loss and immediately average the result across all 784 output dimensions to keep the loss scale artificially small. However, our mathematical derivation relied on the exact sum across all pixels. If you let the framework average the BCE term, but you calculate the raw sum for the KL divergence term, your loss magnitudes will be catastrophically mismatched, and the KL divergence will mathematically overpower the reconstruction, destroying the model. To compensate for this hidden framework behavior, you must manually apply that exact same 1/784 scaling factor to your KL divergence term before adding them together, ensuring both terms remain mathematically perfectly balanced as they were in our theoretical derivation. Once you compile the model with this custom `add_loss` method, training is invoked standardly using `model.fit()`.

    Let's immediately look at the visual results of this VAE and contrast them directly with the standard autoencoder from our first demo. First, look at the linear interpolation across the latent space. While the vanilla autoencoder exhibited severe, disjointed jumps between digits, the VAE produces remarkably smoother, continuous morphological changes as it transitions from one digit class to another. Second, looking at the 2D grid plot where we exhaustively sample the latent plane, the patterns are drastically smoother; the harsh empty voids and aggressive artifacting boundaries are largely gone. Finally, look at the scatter plot of all encoded points. While there is still some density overlapping in the exact center, as you expand outwards, you see significantly more clearly defined, unified clusters corresponding accurately to the specific digit labels. More importantly, observe the overall geometric shape of the clusters. In the standard autoencoder, the clusters were chaotic, weirdly spiked, and completely unpredictable. Here, due directly to the strict KL divergence penalty heavily enforcing the standard normal prior, the overall global distribution is constrained into a much tighter, more regular spherical shape. The VAE successfully solved our generative representation problem. If you want to experiment further, I highly encourage you to swap these simple dense layers for proper Convolutional Neural Networks and observe the massive leap in generation quality.

    在我们在代码中实现之前，先快速总结一下：我们之所以强制使用变分自编码器而不是标准自编码器，是因为 VAE 优化的是抽象分布，而不是僵硬的点对点映射。这从本质上迫使产生一个更平滑、在数学上更连续的潜在空间，它与语义标签完美相关，从而实现了稳健的数据生成。让我们来看一个具体的 PyTorch 或 TensorFlow 演示，看看如何实现这种架构。它实际上出奇地简短。主要的工程挑战仅仅是用数学方法写出那个自定义的 ELBO 损失。

    让我们先构建 VAE 编码器。我们刚刚确定了我们想要一个因子化的高斯模型 Q_phi。我们的神经网络需要接收图像输入 'x'，将其展平，并将其通过全连接的密集层。至关重要的是，网络分裂为两个截然不同的输出向量。我们将明确定义变量 'y1' 代表均值向量 mu，并明确定义变量 'y2' 代表标准差的对数。稍后我们将通过对 y2 取指数在数学上保证一个正的标准差。接下来，我们构建一个自定义的采样层，专门用来执行重参数化技巧。在这个层内部，我们系统地生成一个张量 'u'，它完全由严格从标准正态分布中抽取的随机样本填充。然后，我们通过实现确切的公式来确定性地计算我们的潜在坐标 'z'：z = y1 + exp(y2) * u。为了能够通过损失进行反向传播，我们明确配置编码器模型输出一个元组，其中包含最终的潜在样本 'z'，以及原始的 'y1' (mu) 和 'y2' (log_sigma) 张量。

    对于生成解码器 P_theta，我们构建了一个序列网络，将那个 2D 潜在样本 'z' 作为其严格的输入。它将其传递过密集的隐藏层，逐渐扩展维度，直到最终的输出层在结构上将其重塑为 784 维向量，代表 28 x 28 的图像像素。正如我们因子化伯努利推导所要求的那样，我们严格在最后一层应用 sigmoid 激活，以确保每一个元素都充当 0 和 1 之间有效的、有界的成功概率。完整的、全局的 VAE 模型只需通过将解码器组合到编码器的输出来初始化。

    最后一个关键步骤是编写自定义损失函数以最小化负 ELBO。我们手动编写这两个截然不同的数学部分。首先是重建损失，它是输入图像和解码器 sigmoid 输出之间的标准二元交叉熵。其次，我们利用从编码器保存的 'y1' 和 'y2' 张量编写 KL 散度正则化公式。这里有一个绝对至关重要的实现细节你必须抓住：默认情况下，像 TensorFlow 这样的深度学习框架在计算二元交叉熵损失时，会立即将结果在所有 784 个输出维度上取平均值，以人为地保持损失规模较小。然而，我们的数学推导依赖于所有像素的精确总和。如果你让框架对 BCE 项取平均值，但你计算了 KL 散度项的原始总和，你的损失量级将会灾难性地不匹配，KL 散度将在数学上压倒重建，从而摧毁模型。为了补偿这种隐藏的框架行为，你必须在将它们加在一起之前，手动将完全相同的 1/784 缩放因子应用于你的 KL 散度项，确保这两项在数学上保持完美的平衡，就像它们在我们的理论推导中一样。一旦你用这个自定义的 `add_loss` 方法编译了模型，就可以使用 `model.fit()` 标准地调用训练。

    让我们立即看看这个 VAE 的视觉结果，并将它们直接与我们第一个演示中的标准自编码器进行对比。首先，看看整个潜在空间的线性插值。虽然普通的自编码器在数字之间表现出严重、脱节的跳跃，但 VAE 在从一个数字类别过渡到另一个数字类别时，产生了明显更平滑、连续的形态变化。其次，看看我们穷举采样潜在平面的二维网格图，图案要平滑得多；刺眼的空隙和强烈的伪影边界基本上消失了。最后，看看所有编码点的散点图。虽然在正中心仍有一些密度重叠，但随着你向外扩展，你会看到明显更清晰、统一的集群，它们与特定的数字标签准确对应。更重要的是，观察集群的整体几何形状。在标准自编码器中，集群是混乱的、怪异地呈尖刺状，并且完全不可预测。在这里，直接归因于强烈执行标准正态先验的严格 KL 散度惩罚，整体全局分布被限制在一个更紧密、更规则的球形形状中。VAE 成功地解决了我们的生成表示问题。如果你想进一步实验，我强烈鼓励你将这些简单的密集层换成合适的卷积神经网络，并观察生成质量的巨大飞跃。

## Week 10
  - [14:00:00 - 14:12:00] **Review of VAEs and Introduction to GANs**

    Welcome back, everyone. Let's go ahead and get started with Lecture 10. Last time, we introduced a very important class of generative models known as Variational Autoencoders, or VAEs. We spent a lot of time discussing the bottleneck architecture and how we can use it to force the model to learn a smooth latent space. The two absolutely crucial ideas you should have taken away from last week are the concept of latent variables and the reparameterization trick. Remember, to make the whole system differentiable so we could train it with backpropagation, we had to decouple the randomness. We did this by expressing our target variable x as a deterministic function x = g(u, θ), where the randomness was isolated in an auxiliary variable u sampled from a simple static prior, u ~ p_0(u). This allowed us to optimize complex distributions effectively.

    Today, we are going to discuss another wildly popular class of generative models that shares some of these foundational ideas—specifically the use of latent variables to generate data—but it approaches the optimization problem from a completely different, almost combative philosophical angle. We are going to dive into Generative Adversarial Networks, universally known as GANs. If VAEs are about rigorous statistical approximation and maximizing lower bounds, GANs are about setting up a high-stakes game between two competing neural networks.

    欢迎大家回来。我们现在开始第10讲。上节课，我们介绍了一类非常重要的生成模型，即变分自编码器（VAEs）。我们花了很多时间讨论瓶颈架构，以及如何利用它来迫使模型学习一个平滑的潜空间。大家上周必须掌握的两个绝对核心的思想是潜变量的概念和重参数化技巧。请记住，为了使整个系统可微以便我们能用反向传播进行训练，我们必须将随机性解耦。我们通过将目标变量x表示为确定性函数 x = g(u, θ) 来实现这一点，其中随机性被隔离在一个从简单静态先验中采样的辅助变量u中，即 u ~ p_0(u)。这使我们能够有效地优化复杂的分布。

    今天，我们将讨论另一类极其受欢迎的生成模型，它与VAE共享一些基础思想——特别是使用潜变量来生成数据——但它从一个完全不同的、甚至可以说是对抗性的哲学角度来处理优化问题。我们将深入探讨生成对抗网络，大家普遍称之为GANs。如果说VAE的核心在于严谨的统计近似和最大化下界，那么GAN的核心则是建立两个相互竞争的神经网络之间的高风险博弈。

  - [14:12:00 - 14:25:00] **Recalling Adversarial Training**

    To truly understand the motivation behind GANs, I need you to cast your minds back a few weeks to Lecture 7, where we discussed adversarial training. Do you remember when we talked about how a carefully crafted, imperceptible noise vector could completely fool a highly accurate image classifier? We looked at examples where a model confidently classified an image of a dog, but after adding a tiny, mathematically optimized layer of noise, the model suddenly thought it was an ostrich. We formalized this by trying to find an adversarial example x' that maximizes the loss function L(f_hat(z), y) subject to the perturbation z being within some tiny distance δ from the original image x.

    To defend against this, we introduced adversarial training using the Fast Gradient Sign Method, or FGSM. The core idea there was fundamentally game-theoretic: we introduced an adversary into the training loop. We formulated it as a min-max optimization problem. The adversary was actively trying to maximize the network's loss by finding the worst-case perturbations, and simultaneously, our training algorithm was updating the model parameters θ to minimize that maximized loss. We essentially used the adversary to aggressively challenge the training process, operating under the assumption that if our training can win against this adversary, our model will be robust enough to beat any real-world adversaries. Now, what if we take this exact game-theoretic idea of an adversary and apply it not to classification defense, but to the generation of entirely new data?

    为了真正理解GAN背后的动机，我需要大家回想一下几周前的第7讲，当时我们讨论了对抗训练。还记得我们讲过，一个精心构造的、肉眼无法察觉的噪声向量是如何彻底欺骗一个高精度的图像分类器的吗？我们看了一些例子，模型自信地将一张图像分类为狗，但在添加了一层微小的、经过数学优化的噪声后，模型突然认为它是一只鸵鸟。我们将此形式化为寻找一个对抗样本x'，使其最大化损失函数 L(f_hat(z), y)，前提是扰动z与原图像x的距离在一个极小的范围δ内。

    为了防范这种情况，我们引入了使用快速梯度符号法（FGSM）的对抗训练。这里的核心思想从根本上说是博弈论的：我们在训练循环中引入了一个对手。我们将其表述为一个极大极小（min-max）优化问题。对手积极地试图通过寻找最坏情况的扰动来最大化网络的损失，与此同时，我们的训练算法正在更新模型参数θ以最小化这个被最大化的损失。我们本质上是利用对手来主动挑战训练过程，基于这样一种假设：如果我们的训练能战胜这个对手，我们的模型就会足够健壮，从而击败任何现实世界中的对手。现在，如果我们把这种博弈论中的对手概念，不用于分类防御，而是用于生成全新的数据，会怎么样呢？

  - [14:25:00 - 14:40:00] **The GAN Architecture and Min-Max Game**

    This brings us to the dual-network architecture of a GAN. Instead of one network trying to do everything, we build two separate neural networks that compete against each other: the Generator and the Discriminator. The Generator's job is to take a random noise vector—our latent variable z, which is usually sampled from a simple prior like a standard normal distribution, z ~ N(0, I_n)—and map it to the data space to generate fake samples. Let's denote the Generator as a differentiable function G_θ(z) that maps from R^n to R^d. On the other side of the ring, we have the Discriminator, denoted as D_φ(x), which maps an image in R^d to a single scalar value between 0 and 1. The Discriminator acts as our adversary. It is a binary classifier whose sole job is to look at a sample and detect whether it is real (coming from the true training data p*) or fake (coming from the Generator).

    Let's formalize the mathematics of this training problem. The Discriminator D_φ wants to minimize its classification error, which means it wants to minimize the standard binary cross-entropy loss. Recall that if the true label y is 1 for a real sample, the cross-entropy loss is -log D_φ(x). If y is 0 for a generated fake sample, the loss is -log(1 - D_φ(G_θ(z))). Since we generally frame this as the Discriminator trying to maximize its success rate, the total objective function it wants to maximize is L(θ, φ) = E_{x~p*}[log D_φ(x)] + E_{z~p_0}[log(1 - D_φ(G_θ(z)))]. We assume it is fed an equal mix of real and fake data.

    The Generator, G_θ, has the exact opposite goal. It wants to completely fool the Discriminator. Therefore, its objective is to minimize this exact same value L(θ, φ). This sets up a classic saddle-point formulation, an elegant zero-sum game between the two networks. The overarching mathematical problem can be written simply as: min_θ max_φ L(θ, φ). The Discriminator pushes the value up, and the Generator pushes it down.

    I see a hand raised in the front. Yes?

    *Student:* "Professor, if it's a zero-sum game, does that mean one network eventually destroys the other and training stops?"

    *Professor:* That is a very perceptive question. In an ideal theoretical world, one doesn't "destroy" the other. Instead, they reach a stalemate. In game theory, we call this a Nash Equilibrium, which is exactly what we will discuss next. It is the point where neither network can improve its position given the other network's current state. But as we will see later, reaching this equilibrium in practice using gradient descent is incredibly notoriously difficult and often leads to the training collapsing rather than cleanly stabilizing.

    这就引出了GAN的双网络架构。我们不再用一个网络试图做所有事情，而是构建两个相互竞争的独立神经网络：生成器（Generator）和判别器（Discriminator）。生成器的任务是接收一个随机噪声向量——也就是我们的潜变量z，它通常从一个简单的先验分布（如标准正态分布 z ~ N(0, I_n)）中采样——并将其映射到数据空间以生成伪造样本。我们把生成器记为一个从R^n映射到R^d的可微函数 G_θ(z)。在擂台的另一边，我们有判别器，记为 D_φ(x)，它将R^d中的图像映射为0到1之间的单一标量值。判别器充当我们的对手。它是一个二分类器，其唯一的工作就是观察一个样本并检测它是真实的（来自真实训练数据p*）还是伪造的（来自生成器）。

    让我们把这个训练问题的数学表达形式化。判别器 D_φ 希望最小化其分类误差，这意味着它想要最小化标准的二元交叉熵损失。回想一下，如果真实样本的真实标签y为1，则交叉熵损失为 -log D_φ(x)。如果生成的伪造样本y为0，则损失为 -log(1 - D_φ(G_θ(z)))。由于我们通常将其构建为判别器试图最大化其成功率，因此它想要最大化的总目标函数是 L(θ, φ) = E_{x~p*}[log D_φ(x)] + E_{z~p_0}[log(1 - D_φ(G_θ(z)))]。我们假设它接收到的是等量混合的真实和伪造数据。

    生成器 G_θ 的目标则完全相反。它想要彻底欺骗判别器。因此，它的目标是最小化这个完全相同的值 L(θ, φ)。这就建立了一个经典的鞍点公式，也就是两个网络之间优雅的零和博弈。这个首要的数学问题可以简单地写成：min_θ max_φ L(θ, φ)。判别器把这个值往上推，而生成器把它往下压。

    我看到前排有同学举手。请问？

    *学生*：“教授，如果这是一个零和博弈，这是否意味着一个网络最终会摧毁另一个网络，然后训练就停止了？”

    *教授*：“这是一个非常敏锐的问题。在一个理想的理论世界中，一个并没有‘摧毁’另一个。相反，它们会达到一种僵局。在博弈论中，我们称之为纳什均衡（Nash Equilibrium），这正是我们接下来要讨论的。在这一点上，给定另一个网络当前的状态，任何一个网络都无法改善其自身的位置。但是正如我们稍后将看到的，在实践中使用梯度下降来达到这种平衡是非常出了名的困难，并且通常会导致训练崩溃，而不是干净利落地稳定下来。”

  - [14:40:00 - 14:55:00] **GANs, Nash Equilibrium, and JS Divergence**

    So, what exactly does this Nash Equilibrium look like for our GAN? If interpreted strictly as a two-player zero-sum game, the equilibrium is reached when the distribution of the generated images, p_G, perfectly matches the true data distribution, p*. When the fake data perfectly overlaps with the real data, what can the Discriminator do? It is left completely blind. The best it can do is randomly guess, meaning D_φ(x) equals 1/2 for every single image it receives. Neither player has an incentive to change their strategy because the Generator can't get any more "real," and the Discriminator can't find any separating boundary.

    We can actually link this min-max game directly back to the concept of statistical divergence that we discussed last week with VAEs. Let's forget about the neural network parameters θ and φ for a moment and just look at the raw functions. If we fix the Generator G, we can mathematically solve for the absolutely optimal Discriminator, D*. Using some basic calculus of variations, it turns out the optimal discriminator is D*_G(x) = p*(x) / (p*(x) + p_G(x)). If you plug this optimal D back into our overarching min-max objective, the math collapses beautifully.

    It reveals that optimizing the Generator against a perfect Discriminator is mathematically identically equivalent to: min_G D_JS(p_G || p*) - 2 log 2. We are simply minimizing the Jensen-Shannon (JS) divergence between the generated distribution and the real distribution. Why do we care about the JS divergence? Last week we used the Kullback-Leibler (KL) divergence, D_KL(p||q), but I explicitly warned you that KL divergence is not a true distance metric because it is highly asymmetric—the distance from p to q is not the same as q to p. The Jensen-Shannon divergence fixes this. It is defined as the average of the KL divergence from p to the midpoint (p+q)/2, and the KL divergence from q to the midpoint. Because of this, JS divergence is completely symmetric and is a much better behaved metric on the space of probability distributions. So, fundamentally, a basic GAN is just a machine explicitly designed to minimize the JS distance between fake data and real data.

    那么，对于我们的GAN来说，这种纳什均衡究竟是什么样子的呢？如果严格解释为一个双人零和博弈，当生成的图像分布 p_G 完美地匹配真实数据分布 p* 时，就达到了均衡。当假数据与真实数据完美重叠时，判别器能做什么？它变得完全像瞎子一样。它能做到的最好程度就是随机猜测，这意味着对于它接收到的每一张图像，D_φ(x) 都等于 1/2。由于生成器无法变得更“真实”，而判别器也找不到任何分离边界，因此没有玩家有动机改变他们的策略。

    实际上，我们可以将这个极大极小博弈直接联系回我们上周在讨论VAE时提到的统计散度概念。让我们暂时忘记神经网络的参数θ和φ，只看原始函数。如果我们固定生成器G，我们可以通过数学方法求解出绝对最优的判别器D*。使用一些基础的变分法，结果表明最优判别器为 D*_G(x) = p*(x) / (p*(x) + p_G(x))。如果你将这个最优的D代回我们首要的极大极小目标中，数学公式会非常漂亮地化简。

    它揭示了针对完美判别器优化生成器，在数学上完全等价于：min_G D_JS(p_G || p*) - 2 log 2。我们只是在最小化生成分布和真实分布之间的Jensen-Shannon (JS)散度。为什么我们要关注JS散度？上周我们使用了Kullback-Leibler (KL)散度 D_KL(p||q)，但我明确警告过你们，KL散度不是一个真正的距离度量，因为它是高度不对称的——从p到q的距离不等于从q到p的距离。Jensen-Shannon散度修复了这个问题。它被定义为从p到中点 (p+q)/2 的KL散度，以及从q到中点的KL散度的平均值。正因为如此，JS散度是完全对称的，并且是在概率分布空间上表现好得多的度量标准。所以，从根本上说，一个基本的GAN就是一台被明确设计用来最小化假数据和真实数据之间JS距离的机器。

  - [14:55:00 - 15:10:00] **The Basic GAN Training Algorithm**

    Okay, the theoretical math is elegant, but how do we actually write code to train this? We absolutely cannot solve the min-max problem analytically for deep neural networks. Instead, we must rely on our trusty tool: Stochastic Gradient Descent, specifically, alternating mini-batch gradient descent. Because we have two competing networks, we cannot update them simultaneously with a single loss gradient. We must take turns.

    The basic training loop looks like this. First, we enter a loop to train the Discriminator for 'k' steps. We sample a mini-batch of 'm' random noise vectors z_i from our prior p_0, and we use the current Generator to turn those into fake images. Simultaneously, we sample 'm' real data images x_i from our dataset. We compute the discriminator loss on this combined batch and update the discriminator's parameters φ using gradient ascent, because it wants to maximize the objective. The update rule is φ <- φ + ε_1 ∇_φ (1/m) Σ [log D_φ(x_i) + log(1 - D_φ(G_θ(z_i)))].

    Once the Discriminator has had its 'k' steps and is hopefully somewhat strong, we freeze its weights and switch to training the Generator for one step. We sample a fresh batch of 'm' noise vectors. We pass them through the Generator and then through the frozen Discriminator. We update the Generator's parameters θ using gradient descent to minimize the objective: θ <- θ - ε_2 ∇_θ (1/m) Σ log(1 - D_φ(G_θ(z_i))). We alternate this process over and over: train D a bit, train G a bit, train D a bit, train G a bit. A common question I get here is: "Do we set k=1 and train them equally?" The answer is that typically, you want the Discriminator to be slightly ahead of the Generator so it provides a clean, accurate gradient signal. So in practice, 'k' is often set to something like 3 or 5, meaning we train the Discriminator multiple times for every single Generator update. However, keeping this delicate balance from collapsing is extremely tough, which leads to issues like vanishing gradients and mode collapse. We will tackle those exact pathologies right after a short break.

    好了，理论数学很优雅，但我们实际上如何编写代码来训练它呢？对于深度神经网络，我们绝对无法解析地求解极大极小问题。相反，我们必须依靠我们可靠的工具：随机梯度下降，具体来说，是交替小批量梯度下降。因为我们有两个相互竞争的网络，我们不能用单一的损失梯度同时更新它们。我们必须轮流进行。

    基本的训练循环是这样的。首先，我们进入一个循环，训练判别器 'k' 步。我们从先验 p_0 中采样一个小批量（包含 'm' 个随机噪声向量 z_i），然后使用当前的生成器将它们变成假图像。同时，我们从数据集中采样 'm' 张真实数据图像 x_i。我们在这个组合批次上计算判别器损失，并使用梯度上升来更新判别器的参数 φ，因为它想要最大化目标。更新规则是 φ <- φ + ε_1 ∇_φ (1/m) Σ [log D_φ(x_i) + log(1 - D_φ(G_θ(z_i)))]。

    一旦判别器完成了它的 'k' 步并且希望变得相对强大了一些，我们就冻结它的权重，转而训练生成器一步。我们采样一批全新的 'm' 个噪声向量。我们将它们通过生成器，然后再通过被冻结的判别器。我们使用梯度下降更新生成器的参数 θ 以最小化目标：θ <- θ - ε_2 ∇_θ (1/m) Σ log(1 - D_φ(G_θ(z_i)))。我们不断交替这个过程：训练D一会，训练G一会，训练D一会，训练G一会。我在这里经常收到的一个常见问题是：“我们是否设置 k=1 并且平等地训练它们？” 答案是，通常你希望判别器稍微领先于生成器，以便它提供清晰、准确的梯度信号。所以在实践中，'k' 经常被设置为类似 3 或 5，这意味着对于生成器的每一次更新，我们都会训练判别器多次。然而，保持这种微妙的平衡不崩溃是极其困难的，这会导致诸如梯度消失和模式崩溃等问题。我们将在短暂休息后，马上解决这些具体的病态问题。

  - [15:10:00 - 15:20:00] **Problem I: The Vanishing Gradient Problem & The Heuristic Fix**

    Alright, let's settle back down. Before the break, we laid out the elegant theoretical foundation of GANs, showing how minimizing the JS divergence emerges from a min-max game. So, theoretically, we just minimize this divergence and we are done. Sounds perfectly easy, right? Well, in practice, if you just code up that basic formulation and hit train, you will almost certainly fail. Training a standard GAN is notoriously difficult and unstable. There are three primary problems you will encounter, and we are going to walk through each one. Problem number one is the Vanishing Gradient problem. Look at the loss function for the generator. It wants to minimize the expected value of log(1 - D_φ(G_θ(z))). Let's look at the gradient of this loss with respect to the generator's parameters, θ.

    Early in training, your generator is completely untrained; it is producing absolute garbage—just static noise. The discriminator, on the other hand, is usually a pretty capable CNN. When tasked with distinguishing real, crisp training images from the generator's static noise, the discriminator easily learns to tell them apart with nearly 100% confidence very quickly. It rejects the fakes effortlessly, meaning D_φ(G_θ(z)) is approximately 0. Now, let's look at the math. If the discriminator outputs a sigmoid function, denoted as σ(a), the derivative of log(1 - σ(a)) with respect to 'a' is simply -σ(a). By the chain rule, the gradient of our generator's loss with respect to θ is proportional to the expected value of D_φ(G_θ(z)). If the discriminator is too good, D_φ is essentially 0. Therefore, the gradient vanishes. It becomes 0. The generator receives absolutely no learning signal because the discriminator is too perfect. It gets stuck and learns nothing.

    To solve this, researchers introduced a practical heuristic. Instead of telling the generator to minimize the probability of the discriminator being correct—which is minimizing log(1 - D_φ)—we change the instruction. We instruct the generator to maximize the probability of the discriminator being wrong. Mathematically, we replace the objective with minimizing -log(D_φ(G_θ(z))). If you look at the graphs of these two functions, log(1 - x) is incredibly flat when x is near 0, giving no gradient. But -log(x) drops sharply towards positive infinity as x approaches 0. This means that when the generator is performing poorly and D_φ is near 0, the -log(x) function provides a massive, steep gradient. This heuristic breaks the zero-sum game theory strictly speaking, but it provides the strong early signal the generator desperately needs to start learning, so we adopt it in standard practice.

    好了，大家安静一下。在休息之前，我们阐述了GAN优雅的理论基础，展示了最小化JS散度是如何从一个极大极小博弈中产生的。所以，从理论上讲，我们只需要最小化这个散度就大功告成了。听起来很容易，对吧？然而在实践中，如果你仅仅把那个基本公式写成代码并开始训练，你几乎肯定会失败。训练一个标准的GAN是出了名的困难和不稳定。你会遇到三个主要问题，我们将逐一进行探讨。问题一：梯度消失（Vanishing Gradient）问题。看看生成器的损失函数。它想要最小化 log(1 - D_φ(G_θ(z))) 的期望值。让我们看看这个损失关于生成器参数θ的梯度。

    在训练早期，你的生成器完全没有经过训练；它产生的是绝对的垃圾——纯粹的静态噪声。另一方面，判别器通常是一个相当有能力的CNN。当任务是区分真实的、清晰的训练图像与生成器的静态噪声时，判别器很容易、很快就能以近乎100%的置信度学会区分它们。它毫不费力地拒绝了伪造品，这意味着 D_φ(G_θ(z)) 约等于0。现在，让我们看看数学。如果判别器输出一个sigmoid函数，记为σ(a)，那么 log(1 - σ(a)) 对 'a' 的导数简单来说就是 -σ(a)。根据链式法则，我们的生成器损失对θ的梯度，正比于 D_φ(G_θ(z)) 的期望值。如果判别器太优秀了，D_φ本质上就是0。因此，梯度消失了。它变成了0。生成器收不到任何学习信号，因为判别器太完美了。它被卡住了，什么也学不到。

    为了解决这个问题，研究人员引入了一个实用的启发式方法。我们不再指示生成器去最小化判别器正确的概率——即最小化 log(1 - D_φ)——而是改变了指令。我们指示生成器最大化判别器犯错的概率。在数学上，我们将目标替换为最小化 -log(D_φ(G_θ(z)))。如果你看看这两个函数的图像，当x接近0时，log(1 - x) 非常平坦，没有梯度。但是当x接近0时，-log(x) 会急剧下降向正无穷大。这意味着当生成器表现不佳且 D_φ 接近0时，-log(x) 函数会提供一个巨大、陡峭的梯度。严格来说，这个启发式方法打破了零和博弈理论，但它提供了生成器开始学习所急需的强烈早期信号，所以我们在标准实践中采用了它。

  - [15:20:00 - 15:30:00] **Problem II & III: Non-convergence and Mode Collapse**

    Problem number two is Non-convergence. Remember, the optimization algorithms we use, like Gradient Descent, are fundamentally designed to find local minima. They simply follow the slope downwards. They are absolutely not designed to solve min-max saddle point problems. A classic mathematical example to visualize this is trying to solve min_x max_y L(x,y) = xy. The optimal solution is clearly at the origin (0,0). However, if you apply alternating gradient descent to this—updating x to minimize, then y to maximize—the system will not converge to the origin. Instead, the parameters will orbit around the origin in a continuous circle endlessly. GAN training in high-dimensional spaces often exhibits these exact same frustrating, infinite oscillations where the Generator and Discriminator just chase each other in circles without ever stabilizing.

    Problem number three is arguably the most famous GAN failure state: Mode Collapse. To understand mode collapse, think about what happens when the Generator discovers one single, specific image—maybe a somewhat blurry but passable image of a dog—that happens to successfully fool the Discriminator. Because the Generator's only goal is to maximize the Discriminator's error, it realizes it has found a winning lottery ticket. Instead of taking the difficult path of learning the highly diverse distribution of all possible dogs, the Generator lazily collapses its output. It starts producing that exact same passable dog image for every single random noise vector z you feed it.

    When this happens, the generated samples lose all variance; the model collapses to a single "mode" of the data distribution. Look at the slide showing the evolution of training over 25k steps. Initially, the output is diverse, but by step 15k, every single generated patch looks virtually identical. To combat mode collapse, researchers developed a technique called Mini-batch GAN. The core idea is that we don't just feed individual images to the Discriminator; we feed it an entire mini-batch at once and force it to compute batch-level statistics, like the variance or pairwise L2 norms between the generated images. If the Generator is suffering from mode collapse, the variance of that fake batch will be incredibly low. The Discriminator easily detects this abnormally low variance, realizes it is looking at a batch of fakes, and rejects them all. This creates a strong penalty, forcing the Generator to produce diverse samples to maintain a natural batch variance.

    问题二是未收敛（Non-convergence）。请记住，我们使用的优化算法（如梯度下降）从根本上讲是为了寻找局部极小值而设计的。它们只是顺着斜坡向下走。它们绝对不是为解决极大极小鞍点问题而设计的。一个经典的数学例子是试图求解 min_x max_y L(x,y) = xy。最优解显然在原点(0,0)。然而，如果你对此应用交替梯度下降——更新x以最小化，然后更新y以最大化——系统将不会收敛到原点。相反，参数将无休止地绕着原点连续画圈。高维空间中的GAN训练经常表现出完全相同的令人沮丧的无限震荡，生成器和判别器只是在兜圈子互相追逐，而永远无法稳定下来。

    问题三可以说是最著名的GAN失败状态：模式崩溃（Mode Collapse）。为了理解模式崩溃，想一想当生成器发现了一张特定的图像——也许是一张有些模糊但还能勉强过关的狗的图像——碰巧成功欺骗了判别器时会发生什么。因为生成器的唯一目标是最大化判别器的误差，它意识到自己找到了一张中奖彩票。生成器没有选择学习所有可能狗的高度多样化分布这条艰难的道路，而是偷懒地让其输出崩溃了。对于你输入的每一个随机噪声向量z，它都开始产生完全相同的、勉强过关的狗的图像。

    当这种情况发生时，生成的样本失去了所有的方差；模型崩溃到数据分布的单一“模式”（mode）。看看幻灯片上展示的25k步训练的演变过程。最初，输出是多样化的，但到了15k步，每一个生成的图像块看起来几乎都一模一样。为了对抗模式崩溃，研究人员开发了一种称为Mini-batch GAN的技术。核心思想是，我们不只是将单张图像输入给判别器；我们一次将整个小批量输入给它，并强制它计算批次级别的统计数据，比如生成的图像之间的方差或成对的L2范数。如果生成器遭受模式崩溃，那么那个假批次的方差将会非常低。判别器很容易检测到这种异常低的方差，意识到它正在看一批假货，并全部拒绝。这就产生了一个强烈的惩罚，迫使生成器产生多样化的样本，以保持自然的批次内方差。

  - [15:30:00 - 15:45:00] **The Flaw of KL and JS Divergences in High Dimensions**

    So, we have these practical heuristics to patch up vanishing gradients and mode collapse, but let's take a step back and ask a deeper theoretical question. Why do these problems—especially the vanishing gradient—happen in the first place? Let us return to the viewpoint of training GANs as minimizing "distances" between distributions. We said earlier that optimizing a standard GAN is equivalent to minimizing the Jensen-Shannon divergence: min_G D_JS(p_G || p*). The fundamental mathematical flaw lies in how KL divergence, and by extension JS divergence, behaves when distributions do not perfectly overlap.

    In deep learning, we are working with high-dimensional data like images. According to the Manifold Hypothesis we discussed last week, true image data p* sits on a very low-dimensional manifold embedded within that massive high-dimensional pixel space. Similarly, our generated distribution p_G starts off as a completely different, random low-dimensional manifold. In an overwhelmingly large space, the probability that these two infinitely thin manifolds perfectly intersect and overlap is essentially zero. They are what we call "singular" distributions.

    Let's look at the mathematical example on the slide. If you have two singular distributions—say, one is a Dirac delta exactly at x=1, and the other is a Dirac delta at x=-1. If you try to compute the KL divergence, you have to divide p(x) by q(x). At x=1, q(x) is exactly 0. You end up dividing by zero, so the KL divergence explodes to positive infinity. It is completely undefined. What about the JS divergence? Because the distributions do not overlap anywhere, the formula for the JS divergence completely simplifies. It mathematically evaluates to a constant value of exactly log 2. It doesn't matter if your generated images are somewhat close to a real face, or if they look like absolute television static. As long as they don't perfectly overlap with the real data manifold, the JS divergence is stuck at a flat constant ceiling of log 2.

    那么，我们有这些实用的启发式方法来修补梯度消失和模式崩溃，但让我们退一步，问一个更深层次的理论问题。为什么一开始会发生这些问题——特别是梯度消失？让我们回到将训练GAN视为最小化分布之间“距离”的观点。我们之前说过，优化标准GAN等价于最小化Jensen-Shannon散度：min_G D_JS(p_G || p*)。根本的数学缺陷在于，当分布不能完美重叠时，KL散度以及延伸而来的JS散度是如何表现的。

    在深度学习中，我们处理的是像图像这样的高维数据。根据我们上周讨论的流形假设（Manifold Hypothesis），真实的图像数据 p* 位于嵌入在那个巨大的高维像素空间中的一个非常低维的流形上。类似地，我们的生成分布 p_G 最初也是一个完全不同的、随机的低维流形。在一个无比巨大的空间中，这两个无限薄的流形完美相交并重叠的概率基本上为零。它们就是我们所说的“奇异”（singular）分布。

    看看幻灯片上的数学例子。如果你有两个奇异分布——比如说，一个是在 x=1 处的狄拉克δ函数，另一个是在 x=-1 处的狄拉克δ函数。如果你试图计算KL散度，你必须用 p(x) 除以 q(x)。在 x=1 处，q(x) 正好为0。你最终会除以零，所以KL散度爆炸到正无穷大。它是完全未定义的。那么JS散度呢？因为分布在任何地方都不重叠，JS散度的公式被彻底简化了。它在数学上计算出一个精确的常数值：log 2。不管你生成的图像是稍微接近真实人脸，还是看起来像绝对的电视雪花噪点，这都无所谓。只要它们不与真实数据流形完美重叠，JS散度就会卡在 log 2 这样一个平坦的常数天花板上。

  - [15:45:00 - 15:55:00] **The Parallel Lines Example & Earth Mover's Distance**

    Let's solidify this flaw with a brilliant geometric example. Consider a 2D space where our latent variable z is drawn from a standard normal distribution. Let the true distribution p* be a vertical line sitting exactly at x=0. So the data points are (0, z). Now, let our generator model, p_θ, produce a parallel line sitting at x=θ, giving points (θ, z). Our entire training goal is to use gradient descent to update θ and push our generated line until it overlaps with the true line at 0.

    If we compute the KL divergence, D_KL(p_θ || p*), it evaluates to positive infinity everywhere except exactly when θ=0. If we use the standard GAN metric, the JS divergence, D_JS(p_θ || p*), it evaluates to exactly log 2 for any non-zero value of θ, and drops abruptly to 0 only when θ=0. Look at that function. It is a completely flat horizontal line at log 2, with a sudden discontinuity at the origin. If you apply gradient descent to this, what is the gradient? The derivative of a flat line is exactly zero. There is absolutely no gradient. The generator receives zero information telling it *which direction* it needs to move θ to get closer to the true line. This perfectly explains the vanishing gradient problem geometrically.

    The natural question is, can we replace the JS divergence with a better measure of distance so that we don't have this discontinuity issue? The answer is yes, and we look to optimal transport theory for it. We introduce the Wasserstein distance, affectionately known as the Earth Mover's Distance (EMD). Imagine our two distributions, the real line and the fake line, as physical piles of dirt. The Earth Mover's Distance simply measures the minimum mechanical cost required to transport the dirt from one pile and physically reshape it into the other pile. The cost is calculated as the mass multiplied by the Euclidean distance it was moved.

    If we calculate the Wasserstein distance between our two parallel lines, (0, z) and (θ, z), the answer is beautifully simple: it is exactly the absolute value of θ, |θ|. Unlike the JS divergence, this is a true, continuous metric. Even when the lines are completely disjoint and singular, the Wasserstein distance provides a smooth, continuous curve. More importantly, the mapping is differentiable almost everywhere. If you take the derivative of |θ|, it yields a constant, non-zero gradient (either +1 or -1) pointing directly towards the true distribution. Gradient descent works flawlessly here! This realization fundamentally revolutionized GAN training, leading to the Wasserstein GAN, which transformed an unstable heuristic game into a rigorous, robust distance-minimization process.

    让我们用一个绝妙的几何例子来巩固（理解）这个缺陷。考虑一个2D空间，我们的潜变量z从标准正态分布中抽取。假设真实分布 p* 是精确位于 x=0 处的一条垂直线。所以数据点是 (0, z)。现在，假设我们的生成器模型 p_θ 产生一条位于 x=θ 处的平行线，给出数据点 (θ, z)。我们整个训练目标就是使用梯度下降来更新θ，推动我们生成的线，直到它与0处的真实线重叠。

    如果我们计算KL散度，D_KL(p_θ || p*)，除了在θ=0的时候，它在其他任何地方都计算为正无穷大。如果我们使用标准的GAN度量标准，即JS散度，D_JS(p_θ || p*)，对于任何非零的θ值，它的计算结果都精确地等于 log 2，并且只有当θ=0时才突然降到0。看看那个函数。它是一条在 log 2 处的完全平坦的水平线，在原点处有一个突然的不连续点。如果你对此应用梯度下降，梯度是多少？平坦直线的导数正好为零。绝对没有梯度。生成器收到零信息来告诉它需要向 *哪个方向* 移动θ才能更接近真实的线。这从几何上完美地解释了梯度消失问题。

    自然的问题是，我们能不能用一个更好的距离度量来代替JS散度，这样我们就不存在这种不连续的问题了？答案是肯定的，我们从最优传输理论（optimal transport theory）中寻找答案。我们引入了瓦瑟斯坦距离（Wasserstein distance），它被亲切地称为推土机距离（Earth Mover's Distance, EMD）。把我们的两个分布，真线和假线，想象成物理上的土堆。推土机距离简单地测量了将泥土从一堆运走并物理地重塑成另一堆所需的最小机械成本。成本的计算方法是质量乘以其移动的欧几里得距离。

    如果我们计算我们的两条平行线 (0, z) 和 (θ, z) 之间的瓦瑟斯坦距离，答案异常简单：它恰好是θ的绝对值，|θ|。与JS散度不同，这是一个真正的、连续的度量。即使这些线完全脱节和奇异，瓦瑟斯坦距离也能提供一条平滑连续的曲线。更重要的是，这个映射几乎处处可微。如果你对 |θ| 求导，它会产生一个恒定的、非零的梯度（+1或-1），直接指向真实的分布。梯度下降在这里完美运作！这个认识从根本上彻底改变了GAN的训练，导致了Wasserstein GAN的诞生，它将一个不稳定的启发式博弈转变为一个严格的、稳健的距离最小化过程。

  - [15:55:00 - 16:10:00] **Kantorovich-Rubinstein Duality and The Critic**

    So, we established right before the break that the Earth Mover's Distance, or Wasserstein distance, solves our vanishing gradient problem by giving us a smooth, continuous metric. But there is a massive catch. If you look at the formal definition of the Wasserstein distance, it requires us to find the infimum—the absolute minimum cost—over all possible joint distributions gamma that move the mass from p_theta to p*. Searching through every mathematically possible transport plan in a high-dimensional space is computationally impossible for a neural network. We seem to be stuck again.

    But mathematics gives us a miraculous escape route called the Kantorovich-Rubinstein Duality. I am not going to ask you to prove this duality on the exam, but you need to understand its implication. It essentially flips the problem upside down. It proves that instead of trying to find the optimal physical transport plan for the dirt, we can equivalently rewrite the Wasserstein distance as the supremum—the maximum—over a specific class of scoring functions. Specifically, we want to find a function f that maximizes the expected score on real data minus the expected score on fake data.

    However, there is one strict mathematical condition: this scoring function f must be 1-Lipschitz continuous. What does 1-Lipschitz mean in plain English? It simply means the slope of the function can never exceed 1 anywhere. It prevents the function from spiking too dramatically or growing too fast. To implement this in our GAN framework, we use a neural network for this function f. Notice that I am no longer calling it a Discriminator. It no longer outputs a probability between 0 and 1, and we remove the sigmoid activation at the end. It outputs a raw, unbounded real number. Because its job is just to score realism, we now call it the Critic. To enforce the 1-Lipschitz constraint, the original WGAN paper simply clipped the weights of the Critic network so they couldn't get too large. Later papers improved this by adding a gradient penalty to the loss function. The training algorithm looks remarkably similar to a standard GAN, but the loss is much cleaner: train the Critic to output high scores for real images and low scores for fake images, and train the Generator to produce images that force the Critic to output high scores.

    因此，我们在休息前确定了推土机距离（即瓦瑟斯坦距离）通过为我们提供一个平滑连续的度量，解决了梯度消失的问题。但这有一个巨大的陷阱。如果你看看瓦瑟斯坦距离的正式定义，它要求我们在所有可能的联合分布gamma中寻找下确界——即绝对的最低成本，这些分布负责将质量从 p_theta 移动到 p*。在高维空间中搜索每一个数学上可能的传输方案，对于神经网络来说在计算上是不可能的。我们似乎又被困住了。

    但是，数学为我们提供了一条奇迹般的逃生路线，称为Kantorovich-Rubinstein对偶性。我不会要求你们在考试中证明这种对偶性，但你们需要理解它的含义。它本质上将问题颠倒了过来。它证明了，我们实际上可以将瓦瑟斯坦距离等价地重写为一类特定评分函数上的上确界（即最大值），而不是试图寻找搬运泥土的最优物理传输方案。具体来说，我们想要找到一个函数f，它能最大化真实数据上的期望得分减去假数据上的期望得分。

    然而，有一个严格的数学条件：这个评分函数f必须是 1-Lipschitz 连续的。用通俗的话来说，1-Lipschitz 是什么意思？它只是意味着该函数的斜率在任何地方都不能超过1。它防止函数出现过于剧烈的峰值或增长过快。为了在我们的GAN框架中实现这一点，我们使用一个神经网络来表示这个函数f。请注意，我不再称它为判别器（Discriminator）。它不再输出0到1之间的概率，并且我们去掉了最后的sigmoid激活函数。它输出的是一个原始的、无界的实数。因为它的工作仅仅是对真实程度进行评分，我们现在称它为评论家（Critic）。为了强制执行 1-Lipschitz 约束，最初的WGAN论文只是简单地裁剪了评论家网络的权重，使其不能变得过大。后来的论文通过在损失函数中添加梯度惩罚（gradient penalty）改进了这一点。这个训练算法看起来与标准的GAN非常相似，但损失要干净得多：训练评论家为真实图像输出高分，为假图像输出低分；同时训练生成器去产生能迫使评论家输出高分的图像。

  - [16:10:00 - 16:25:00] **Neural Style Transfer: Separating Content and Style**

    Let us shift gears for the final part of our lecture today. So far, we've spent a lot of time discussing how to generate completely new images from scratch out of latent noise, using VAEs and GANs. Now, I want to talk about modifying existing images. We are going to look at a highly popular and visually fascinating technique called Neural Style Transfer.

    The fundamental task of Neural Style Transfer is this: you are given two inputs. First, a content image, let's denote it as x_c. This could be a photograph you took with your phone of the Singapore skyline or the buildings along a river in Tuebingen. Second, you are given a style image, x_s, which is usually a famous painting, like Vincent van Gogh's Starry Night. Your objective is to command a neural network to generate a third, completely new image that perfectly preserves the structural content of your photograph, but seamlessly adopts the artistic style, colors, textures, and brushstrokes of the painting.

    To achieve this seemingly magical blending, we need a robust mathematical way to separate the "content" of an image from its "style." How exactly do we decouple those things? We rely heavily on the hierarchical representations that are naturally formed inside a pre-trained Convolutional Neural Network. Typically, researchers use an established architecture like VGG19, which has already been exhaustively trained on a massive dataset like ImageNet. As we established several weeks ago when we first studied CNNs, the hierarchical nature of these networks means that different layers represent completely different types of features.

    If you look at the early layers, like Convolution 1, the filters act as simple edge and blob detectors. They just look at raw pixels. Moving deeper, the middle layers, like Convolution 3, start combining those edges to pick up complex, repeating textures. Finally, the deep layers, like Convolution 5, have discarded the exact, pixel-level spatial details entirely. Instead, they capture high-level object parts and abstract structural content, like recognizing the presence of a building or a dog, regardless of its exact texture. We are going to directly exploit this specific architectural hierarchy to build our loss functions.

    让我们在今天课程的最后一部分换个话题。到目前为止，我们花了很多时间讨论如何使用VAE和GAN，从潜噪声中从头开始生成全新的图像。现在，我想谈谈如何修改现有的图像。我们将探讨一种非常受欢迎且在视觉上引人入胜的技术，称为神经风格迁移（Neural Style Transfer）。

    神经风格迁移的基本任务是这样的：你得到两个输入。首先，一张内容图像，我们将其记为 x_c。这可能是你用手机拍摄的新加坡天际线，或者是图宾根河畔建筑的照片。其次，你得到一张风格图像，x_s，通常是一幅名画，比如文森特·梵高的《星月夜》。你的目标是指示神经网络生成第三张全新的图像，它能完美地保留你照片的结构内容，但无缝地采用画作的艺术风格、色彩、纹理和笔触。

    为了实现这种看似神奇的融合，我们需要一种强大的数学方法，将图像的“内容”与其“风格”分离开来。我们究竟该如何解耦这些东西呢？我们在很大程度上依赖于预训练卷积神经网络（CNN）内部自然形成的层次化表示。通常，研究人员会使用一种成熟的架构，比如VGG19，它已经在像ImageNet这样的大型数据集上进行了极其充分的预训练。正如我们几周前首次学习CNN时所确立的那样，这些网络的层次化特性意味着不同的层代表了完全不同类型的特征。

    如果你观察早期的层，比如卷积1层，滤波器充当的是简单的边缘和斑点检测器。它们只观察原始像素。更深入地看，中间的层，比如卷积3层，开始组合这些边缘以提取复杂的、重复的纹理。最后，深层的层，比如卷积5层，已经完全丢弃了精确的像素级空间细节。相反，它们捕捉高级的对象部位和抽象的结构内容，比如识别出一栋建筑或一只狗的存在，而不在乎其具体的纹理。我们将直接利用这种特定的架构层次来构建我们的损失函数。

  - [16:25:00 - 16:40:00] **The Math of NST: Content Loss and Gram Matrices**

    Let's define the actual loss functions that will drive this generation. First, let's look at the Content Loss, which is quite intuitive. We take our original photograph, our content image x_c, and we pass it through our frozen, pre-trained CNN. We extract the output feature maps at one of the deeper layers—let's call it layer l, perhaps Conv 5. We then do the exact same thing for the image we are trying to generate, x. The Content Loss is simply defined as the squared Euclidean distance—the standard L2 loss—between the deep feature maps of the generated image and the deep feature maps of the content image. By minimizing this specific L2 loss, we forcefully command the generated image to have the exact same high-level structural layout and object placement as the photograph, even if the actual colors or textures change wildly.

    Now, defining the Style Loss mathematically is significantly more intricate. Artistic style is fundamentally independent of exact spatial location. A Van Gogh painting doesn't just have swirls in the top left corner; the swirling style is a global property of the entire canvas. It is about the recurring textures and how different colors and strokes correlate with one another. To capture this spatially-independent correlation, we compute something called the Gram matrix.

    For a given layer l in the CNN, we take the feature maps and completely flatten their spatial dimensions (height and width) so we are just left with a list of feature channels. We then compute the dot product of all these channels with themselves. Mathematically, the Gram matrix G equals X multiplied by X transpose. What does this matrix actually mean? Think of it this way: if channel 1 detects the color blue, and channel 2 detects a swirling brushstroke, the dot product between them in the Gram matrix will be extremely large if those two features frequently light up in the exact same spatial locations across the image. The Gram matrix is a beautiful statistical summary of feature co-occurrence. To compute the final Style Loss, we extract the Gram matrices from multiple different layers—early, middle, and deep—for both the style painting x_s and our generated image x. The total Style Loss is simply the sum of the squared Euclidean distances between these Gram matrices across all those chosen layers.

    让我们来定义驱动这种生成的实际损失函数。首先，让我们看看内容损失（Content Loss），这是相当直观的。我们把原始照片，即我们的内容图像 x_c，输入到我们冻结的、预训练好的CNN中。我们在其中一个较深的层（我们称之为层 l，也许是Conv 5）提取输出特征图。然后，我们对试图生成的图像 x 执行完全相同的操作。内容损失简单地定义为生成的图像的深层特征图与内容图像的深层特征图之间的平方欧几里得距离——即标准的L2损失。通过最小化这个特定的L2损失，我们强行命令生成的图像拥有与照片完全相同的高级结构布局和对象位置，即使实际的颜色或纹理发生了剧烈的变化。

    现在，在数学上定义风格损失（Style Loss）要复杂得多。艺术风格从根本上是独立于精确的空间位置的。一幅梵高的画不仅仅是在左上角有漩涡；漩涡风格是整个画布的全局属性。它关乎于反复出现的纹理，以及不同颜色和笔触之间是如何相互关联的。为了捕捉这种与空间无关的相关性，我们计算一种称为格拉姆矩阵（Gram matrix）的东西。

    对于CNN中给定的层 l，我们获取特征图，并完全展平它们的空间维度（高度和宽度），这样我们就只剩下一个特征通道的列表。然后我们计算所有这些通道与它们自身的点积。在数学上，格拉姆矩阵 G 等于 X 乘以 X 的转置。这个矩阵实际上意味着什么呢？可以这样想：如果通道1检测蓝色，通道2检测漩涡状笔触，那么如果在整个图像中，这两个特征经常在完全相同的空间位置同时被激活，它们在格拉姆矩阵中的点积就会非常大。格拉姆矩阵是特征共现的一个极好的统计摘要。为了计算最终的风格损失，我们从风格画作 x_s 和生成图像 x 的多个不同层（早期、中期和深层）中提取格拉姆矩阵。总风格损失就是这些在所有选定层上的格拉姆矩阵之间的平方欧几里得距离之和。

  - [16:40:00 - 16:55:00] **Optimizing Pixels and Lecture Summary**

    So we have our Content Loss and our Style Loss. Our final optimization objective is beautifully simple: we want to find a generated image x that minimizes a weighted sum of the content loss and the style loss. We write this as minimize alpha * L_c + beta * L_s. The hyperparameter weights alpha and beta allow you, as the artist, to manually control how strongly you want to preserve the structural content versus how aggressively you want to apply the painting's style.

    But how do we actually optimize this? I often get a question here, so let me anticipate it. "Professor, we are using a VGG network. Are we training the VGG weights using this loss?" The answer is absolutely not. This is the big twist of Neural Style Transfer. The weights of the pre-trained CNN remain completely frozen throughout the entire process. They solely act as fixed feature extractors. Instead, we compute the gradient of this total loss with respect to the *input pixels* of the generated image x itself. We start by initializing x as pure random white noise. Then, we use standard gradient descent to iteratively update and mold those pixel values, step by step, until the combined loss function is minimized. We are optimizing the data, not the model.

    Let's summarize everything we have covered today, as it has been a dense lecture. We started by exploring Generative Adversarial Networks. We learned that GANs represent a massive shift in generative modeling, framing the generation of data as a zero-sum min-max game between an adversarial Discriminator and a Generator. We analyzed the theoretical ideal of minimizing the Jensen-Shannon divergence, but rigorously exposed why that metric fails catastrophically for disjoint distributions in high dimensions, leading to vanishing gradients. This necessitated the jump to optimal transport theory and the Wasserstein distance, forcing us to use a Lipschitz-constrained Critic to provide smooth, continuous gradients.

    Finally, we wrapped up by looking at Neural Style Transfer, which elegantly combines the hierarchical feature extraction power of CNNs with gradient-based optimization in the pixel space to separate and recombine content and style. Both of these topics today highlight the incredible flexibility of deep learning: we are no longer just fitting curves; we are orchestrating complex, competing systems and manipulating deep latent representations to create art and generate reality. That concludes our lecture for today. I encourage you to play with the provided Python notebooks to generate your own adversarial attacks and style transfers. I will see you all next week!

    所以我们有了内容损失和风格损失。我们最终的优化目标非常简单优美：我们想要找到一个生成的图像 x，它能最小化内容损失和风格损失的加权和。我们将其写为最小化 alpha * L_c + beta * L_s。超参数权重 alpha 和 beta 允许你作为艺术家，手动控制你希望多强烈地保留结构内容，以及你希望多激进地应用画作的风格。

    但是我们实际上如何优化它呢？我在这里经常被问到一个问题，所以让我先预判一下。“教授，我们使用的是VGG网络。我们是在用这个损失来训练VGG的权重吗？”答案是绝对不。这是神经风格迁移的重大转折。在整个过程中，预训练CNN的权重保持完全冻结。它们仅仅充当固定的特征提取器。相反，我们计算总损失相对于生成的图像 x 本身的*输入像素*的梯度。我们一开始将 x 初始化为纯粹的随机白噪声。然后，我们使用标准的梯度下降来迭代更新和塑造这些像素值，一步一步，直到组合损失函数被最小化。我们是在优化数据，而不是优化模型。

    让我们总结一下今天涵盖的所有内容，因为这是一节内容密集的课。我们首先探索了生成对抗网络。我们了解到，GAN代表了生成模型的一次巨大转变，它将数据的生成构建为对抗性判别器和生成器之间的零和极大极小博弈。我们分析了最小化Jensen-Shannon散度的理论理想情况，但也严谨地揭示了为什么该度量标准对于高维空间中不相交的分布会遭遇灾难性的失败，从而导致梯度消失。这就迫使我们转向最优传输理论和瓦瑟斯坦距离，迫使我们使用受Lipschitz约束的评论家来提供平滑、连续的梯度。

    最后，我们以神经风格迁移作为结束，它优雅地将CNN的层次化特征提取能力与像素空间中的基于梯度的优化结合起来，从而分离和重新组合内容与风格。今天这两个主题都突显了深度学习令人难以置信的灵活性：我们不再仅仅是拟合曲线；我们正在协调复杂的竞争系统，并操纵深层的潜表示来创造艺术和生成现实。今天的课就到这里。我鼓励大家玩一下提供的Python notebook，来生成你们自己的对抗攻击和风格迁移。我们下周见！
## Week 11
  - [14:00 - 14:05] **Course Logistics and Introduction to Sequence-to-Sequence Modeling**

    Welcome back to Lecture 11 of DSA 5204. I am Dr. Low Yi Rui. Before we dive into the technical material today, a quick administrative reminder that your project submissions are coming up, so please ensure your groups are finalizing your code and reports. Today, we are transitioning to a fascinating new topic: Sequence-to-Sequence modeling. Let's do a quick callback to Lecture 5. In Lecture 5, we introduced Recurrent Neural Networks, or RNNs, to handle sequence data. We modeled the hidden state `h_t` as `σ_r(W h_{t-1} + U x_t + b)`, and the output `y_hat_t` as `σ_o(V h_t + c)`. That standard RNN architecture is powerful, but it inherently assumes a fixed, one-to-one alignment between the input and the output. It expects the input sequence and output sequence to be exactly the same length. However, in the real world, input and output sequence lengths often differ wildly. Consider an input sequence `x = (x_1, x_2, ..., x_T)` and a target output sequence `y = (y_1, y_2, ..., y_{T'})`, where `T` and `T'` are not equal. A classic application of this is machine translation; for example, translating an English sentence into a French sentence, which almost never has the same number of words. Another great example is text summarization, where your input is a massive, multi-page document, and your output is a concise one-paragraph summary. A standard RNN cannot do this natively.  

    欢迎回到 DSA 5204 的第 11 讲。我是 Low Yi Rui 博士。在我们今天深入探讨技术内容之前，先做一个简短的行政提醒：你们的项目提交即将截止，所以请确保你们的小组正在完善代码和报告。今天，我们将过渡到一个引人入胜的新主题：序列到序列（Sequence-to-Sequence）建模。让我们快速回顾一下第 5 讲。在第 5 讲中，我们引入了循环神经网络（RNN）来处理序列数据。我们将隐藏状态 `h_t` 建模为 `σ_r(W h_{t-1} + U x_t + b)`，将输出 `y_hat_t` 建模为 `σ_o(V h_t + c)`。这种标准的 RNN 架构非常强大，但它天生假设输入和输出之间存在固定的、一对一的对齐关系。它期望输入序列和输出序列的长度完全相同。然而在现实世界中，输入和输出序列的长度往往差异巨大。考虑一个输入序列 `x = (x_1, x_2, ..., x_T)` 和一个目标输出序列 `y = (y_1, y_2, ..., y_{T'})`，其中 `T` 和 `T'` 并不相等。一个经典的应用程序是机器翻译；例如，将英语句子翻译成法语，两者的单词数量几乎从不相同。另一个很好的例子是文本摘要，你的输入是一份长篇的多页文档，而你的输出是一段简明的摘要。标准的 RNN 原生是无法做到这一点的。  

  - [14:05 - 14:14] **The Encoder-Decoder Architecture and Context Vectors**

    To solve this mismatch in lengths, we need to completely decouple the input and output sequences. We achieve this using the Encoder-Decoder architecture, which forms a beautiful two-stage dynamical system. In the first stage, the Encoder's dynamics compress the entire input sequence into a single, dense mathematical representation, which we call the state or the context vector. In the second stage, the Decoder's dynamics take that representation state and generate a brand new sequence conditioned entirely upon it. Let's zoom in on the Encoder first. The Encoder processes the input sequence `x = (x_1, ..., x_T)` using an RNN, but crucially, it produces absolutely no outputs during this phase. At each time step `t`, it simply updates its hidden state `h_t = f(x_t, h_{t-1})`, where `x_t` is the input token or embedding. We also introduce special boundary tokens to help the model understand the sequence limits: `<bos>` or `<sos>` for the beginning/start of the sequence, and `<eos>` for the end of the sequence. Once the Encoder sees that `<eos>` token, it produces its final hidden state. This brings us to the context vector `c`, which is designed to summarize the entire input sequence mathematically as `c = q(h_1, ..., h_T)`. For our baseline model, the absolute simplest choice for this function `q` is to just use the very last hidden state, setting `c = h_T`.  

    为了解决这种长度不匹配的问题，我们需要将输入和输出序列完全解耦。我们通过使用编码器-解码器（Encoder-Decoder）架构来实现这一点，它构成了一个优美的两阶段动力系统。在第一阶段，编码器的动力学机制将整个输入序列压缩成一个单一的、密集的数学表示，我们称之为状态或上下文向量。在第二阶段，解码器的动力学机制接收该表示状态，并完全以其为条件生成一个全新的序列。让我们先放大看看编码器。编码器使用 RNN 处理输入序列 `x = (x_1, ..., x_T)`，但关键是，它在此阶段绝对不产生任何输出。在每个时间步 `t`，它只是简单地更新其隐藏状态 `h_t = f(x_t, h_{t-1})`，其中 `x_t` 是输入标记或词嵌入。我们还引入了特殊的边界标记来帮助模型理解序列的界限：`<bos>` 或 `<sos>` 表示序列的开始/起始，`<eos>` 表示序列的结束。一旦编码器看到 `<eos>` 标记，它就会生成其最终的隐藏状态。这就引出了上下文向量 `c`，它旨在通过数学方式总结整个输入序列，即 `c = q(h_1, ..., h_T)`。对于我们的基线模型来说，这个函数 `q` 最绝对简单的选择就是只使用最后一个隐藏状态，设定 `c = h_T`。  

  - [14:14 - 14:22] **The RNN Decoder and Autoregressive Generation**

    Now that we have our context vector `c` encapsulating the entire input sentence, we pass it over to the Decoder. The Decoder is also an RNN, but its job is to generate the output sequence autoregressively, meaning sequentially step-by-step. The hidden state of the Decoder, let's denote it as `s_t`, is initialized with our context vector, so `s_0 = c`. From there, it updates using the function `s_t = g(y_hat_{t-1}, s_{t-1}, c)`. Notice that the update depends on the previously generated token, `y_hat_{t-1}` (which starts as `<bos>`), the previous hidden state `s_{t-1}`, and the context vector `c`. The actual output token `y_hat_t` is then sampled from a probability distribution conditioned on the current state, `P(y_hat_t | s_t)`. Operationally, the output layer for `y_hat_t` is simply a softmax regression applied to the hidden state `s_t`, producing a probability vector whose length is exactly equal to your target vocabulary size, V. Fundamentally, this makes the decoder a conditional language model that is tightly conditioned on the encoded representation. Probabilistically speaking, our overarching goal is to model the joint conditional probability of the entire output sequence given the input, which mathematically factorizes via the chain rule into the product from `t=1` to `T'` of `P(y_t | y_{<t}, x)`. The decoder explicitly approximates this complex distribution using its hidden states.  

    现在我们已经有了封装了整个输入句子的上下文向量 `c`，我们将其传递给解码器。解码器也是一个 RNN，但它的工作是自回归地生成输出序列，这意味着是逐步顺序生成的。解码器的隐藏状态（我们将其记为 `s_t`）使用我们的上下文向量进行初始化，所以 `s_0 = c`。从那里开始，它使用函数 `s_t = g(y_hat_{t-1}, s_{t-1}, c)` 进行更新。请注意，该更新取决于先前生成的标记 `y_hat_{t-1}`（从 `<bos>` 开始）、先前的隐藏状态 `s_{t-1}` 以及上下文向量 `c`。然后，实际的输出标记 `y_hat_t` 是从以当前状态为条件的概率分布 `P(y_hat_t | s_t)` 中采样得出的。在操作上，`y_hat_t` 的输出层仅仅是应用于隐藏状态 `s_t` 的 softmax 回归，产生一个长度完全等于目标词汇表大小 V 的概率向量。从根本上说，这使得解码器成为一个严格以编码表示为条件的条件语言模型。从概率的角度来讲，我们的首要目标是模拟在给定输入的情况下整个输出序列的联合条件概率，这在数学上通过链式法则分解为从 `t=1` 到 `T'` 的 `P(y_t | y_{<t}, x)` 的乘积。解码器明确地使用其隐藏状态来近似这个复杂的分布。  

  - [14:22 - 14:29] **Training Challenges, Teacher Forcing, and Scheduled Sampling**

    To train this seq2seq autoencoder, we naturally want to minimize the negative log-likelihood, which translates to minimizing the sum over time steps of `-log P(y_t | y_{<t}, x)`. However, training autoregressive models directly is notoriously difficult. During normal generation inference, the decoder relies entirely on its own previous prediction, calculating `y_hat_{t-1} = argmax P(y | s_{t-1})`, and feeding that back into itself. The problem is, if the untrained model makes a bad mistake at step 1, it feeds that garbage prediction into step 2, causing compounding errors that propagate through time and destroy the gradients. To stabilize this, we use a vital technique called "Teacher Forcing". During training, instead of feeding the model its own unstable predictions, we explicitly feed it the absolute ground truth token from the target sequence, delayed by one time step `y_{t-1}`. So the update becomes `s_t = g(y_{t-1}, s_{t-1}, c)`. This cleverly converts sequence generation into a massive collection of parallel, conditional next-token prediction problems, allowing for significantly faster and more stable convergence. I see a great question in the PollEV chat: "Won't the model panic during testing when the ground truth teacher is suddenly gone?" Excellent insight. Yes, that exact phenomenon is known as "exposure bias," because the model becomes too reliant on the teacher and performs poorly during real-world inference. A popular midway solution to combat this is "scheduled sampling," where during training, you occasionally and increasingly feed the decoder its own predictions instead of the ground truth labels, weaning it off the teacher slowly.  

    为了训练这个 seq2seq 自编码器，我们自然希望最小化负对数似然，这转化为最小化时间步长上 `-log P(y_t | y_{<t}, x)` 的总和。然而，直接训练自回归模型是出了名的困难。在正常的生成推理过程中，解码器完全依赖于自己先前的预测，计算 `y_hat_{t-1} = argmax P(y | s_{t-1})`，并将其反馈给自己。问题在于，如果未经训练的模型在第 1 步犯了一个严重的错误，它就会将这个垃圾预测输入到第 2 步，导致复合误差随着时间传播并破坏梯度。为了稳定这一点，我们使用一种称为“教师强制（Teacher Forcing）”的关键技术。在训练期间，我们不是向模型输入其自身不稳定的预测，而是明确地向其输入来自目标序列的绝对真实（ground truth）标记，延迟一个时间步 `y_{t-1}`。因此，更新变为 `s_t = g(y_{t-1}, s_{t-1}, c)`。这巧妙地将序列生成转换为大量并行的、有条件的下一个标记预测问题集合，从而实现显著更快、更稳定的收敛。我在 PollEV 聊天中看到了一个很好的问题：“在测试期间，当作为教师的真实数据突然消失时，模型不会恐慌吗？” 非常好的洞察力。是的，这种确切的现象被称为“暴露偏差（exposure bias）”，因为模型变得过于依赖教师，在现实世界的推理中表现不佳。对抗这种现象的一个流行的折中解决方案是“计划采样（scheduled sampling）”，即在训练期间，你偶尔且越来越多地向解码器输入它自己的预测而不是真实标签，让它慢慢戒掉对教师的依赖。  

  - [14:29 - 14:38] **Decoding Strategies: Greedy Search vs. Beam Search**

    Finally, let's discuss how we actually extract the best sentence during inference, a process known as decoding. We formally view decoding as a search problem. We want to generate the specific sequence `y_hat` that mathematically maximizes the conditional probability `P(y | x)`, which is the product of all those step-wise probabilities. Since our vocabulary size is `V` and the sequence length is `T'`, there are `V^{T'}` possible sequences. Doing an exhaustive search to check every single combination guarantees finding the optimal sequence, but the exponential computational cost makes it completely impossible for long sequences. Up to now, we've implicitly used "Greedy Search," where at each time step `t`, we simply choose the single most probable token `argmax P(y | y_{<t}, x)`. The computational cost is a very cheap `O(T'V)`. However, taking the locally optimal step does not guarantee a globally optimal sequence, often leading to suboptimal translations. To vastly improve search quality without exploding the cost, we use "Beam Search". Beam Search maintains the top `k` candidate partial sequences at any given time, where `k` is the beam width. At each step, we expand all `k` sequences with all `V` possible vocabulary tokens, generating `kV` new candidate sequences. We score them all, and strictly prune them back down to the top `k` sequences to carry forward. The cost is `O(kVT')`, which scales linearly and is highly efficient. One final trick: when scoring these candidates, we typically divide the log-probability sum by a length penalty factor, `L^alpha` (normally alpha=0.75), to prevent the algorithm from unfairly penalizing and pruning away longer, valid sequences.  

    最后，让我们讨论一下在推理过程中如何实际提取最佳句子，这个过程被称为解码。我们正式将解码视为一个搜索问题。我们希望生成特定的序列 `y_hat`，在数学上最大化条件概率 `P(y | x)`，这就是所有那些逐步概率的乘积。由于我们的词汇表大小为 `V`，序列长度为 `T'`，因此存在 `V^{T'}` 种可能的序列。进行穷举搜索以检查每一种组合可以保证找到最佳序列，但对于长序列来说，指数级的计算成本使其完全不可能实现。到目前为止，我们隐含地使用了“贪婪搜索（Greedy Search）”，即在每个时间步 `t`，我们简单地选择单一的、最可能的标记 `argmax P(y | y_{<t}, x)`。其计算成本是极其低廉的 `O(T'V)`。然而，采取局部最优步骤并不能保证全局最优序列，这通常会导致次优的翻译结果。为了在不使成本爆炸的情况下大幅提高搜索质量，我们使用“束搜索（Beam Search）”。束搜索在任何给定时间都维护着前 `k` 个候选的部分序列，其中 `k` 是束宽（beam width）。在每一步中，我们用所有 `V` 个可能的词汇标记来扩展所有 `k` 个序列，生成 `kV` 个新的候选序列。我们对它们进行全部评分，并严格将它们修剪回前 `k` 个序列以继续推进。其成本是 `O(kVT')`，呈线性扩展并且效率极高。最后一个技巧：在对这些候选序列进行评分时，我们通常将对数概率总和除以一个长度惩罚因子 `L^alpha`（通常 alpha=0.75），以防止算法不公平地惩罚并修剪掉较长的、有效的序列。  

  - [14:38 - 14:48] **The Information Bottleneck and Average Pooling**

    Let's pick up where we left off. We just saw how the context vector c summarizes the entire input sequence. But we must ask a critical question: is one single context vector really enough? In our baseline model, we simply set c equal to h_T, the very last hidden state of the encoder. Think about what this implies mathematically and structurally. You are forcing the network to compress the semantic meaning, the syntax, and the nuance of a potentially extremely long sentence—say, a 50-word paragraph—into one single, fixed-size vector. This creates a massive information bottleneck. As we discussed back in Lecture 5, standard RNNs notoriously struggle with long-range dependencies because gradients vanish over time. If the first word of the sentence is crucial for translation, its signal is severely diluted by the time the encoder reaches the final word. A student just asked in the chat, "Can we just make the hidden state vector much larger?" It's a fair thought, but simply increasing the dimensionality of h_T leads to an explosion in parameters and massive overfitting; it doesn't solve the structural issue of forgetting early tokens. To mitigate this bottleneck, an early architectural tweak was to use Average Pooling. Instead of exclusively relying on the final state h_T, what if we define c as the arithmetic mean of all the encoder hidden states? Mathematically, c = (1/T) * Σ h_i from i=1 to T. This gives the decoder a more balanced summary of the entire sentence, overcoming the strict long-range limitations of relying solely on the final state. However, average pooling introduces a fatal flaw of its own: it applies the exact same, uniform mathematical weight (1/T) to every single input token. If we are translating "Ich habe einen apfel gegessen" to "I ate an apple," when the decoder is about to generate the English word "apple," it really should only be focusing heavily on the German word "apfel." It shouldn't care equally about "Ich" or "habe." Average pooling completely destroys this context-specificity.

    让我们接着刚才的讲。我们刚刚看到了上下文向量 c 是如何总结整个输入序列的。但我们必须问一个关键问题：仅仅一个上下文向量真的够吗？在我们的基线模型中，我们简单地将 c 设置为等于 h_T，即编码器的最后一个隐藏状态。想想这在数学和结构上意味着什么。你正在迫使网络将一个可能极长的句子（比如说一段 50 个词的段落）的语义、语法和细微差别压缩成一个单一的、固定大小的向量。这造成了巨大的信息瓶颈。正如我们在第 5 讲中讨论的那样，标准的 RNN 在处理长程依赖关系时出了名地困难，因为梯度会随着时间消失。如果句子的第一个词对翻译至关重要，那么当编码器到达最后一个词时，它的信号已经被严重稀释了。刚才有同学在聊天室里问：“我们能不能直接把隐藏状态向量变大一点？” 这个想法有道理，但是简单地增加 h_T 的维度会导致参数爆炸和严重的过拟合；它并没有解决遗忘早期标记的结构性问题。为了缓解这个瓶颈，早期的一个架构调整是使用平均池化（Average Pooling）。与其完全依赖最终状态 h_T，如果我们将 c 定义为所有编码器隐藏状态的算术平均值会怎样？在数学上，c = (1/T) * Σ h_i，其中 i 从 1 到 T。这给了解码器一个更均衡的整个句子的总结，克服了完全依赖最终状态的严格长程限制。然而，平均池化引入了它自己的一个致命缺陷：它对每一个输入标记应用完全相同、均匀的数学权重 (1/T)。如果我们将“Ich habe einen apfel gegessen”翻译成“I ate an apple”，当解码器准备生成英语单词“apple”时，它真的应该只重度关注德语单词“apfel”。它不应该同等地关心“Ich”或“habe”。平均池化完全破坏了这种上下文特异性。

  - [14:48 - 14:56] **The Breakthrough: Attention as Adaptive Pooling**

    This realization that different output tokens need to focus on different specific input tokens led to one of the most profound breakthroughs in modern deep learning: the Attention Mechanism. If average pooling assigns a static, uniform weight to every hidden state, Attention replaces it with Adaptive Pooling. Instead of one fixed context vector c for the entire decoding process, we compute a dynamically changing context vector c_t for every single decoding time step t. Mathematically, c_t = Σ w_{t,i} * h_i. Here, w_{t,i} represents the specific attention weight that the decoder at time t assigns to the encoder hidden state at position i. The beauty of this is that the decoder dynamically adapts these weights on the fly. How do we ensure these weights behave properly? We mandate that the weights w_{t,i} form a valid probability distribution—they must be positive and sum to exactly 1. We achieve this by applying a Softmax function over a set of raw, unnormalized alignment scores, e_{t,i}. The formula is w_{t,i} = exp(e_{t,i}) / Σ_j exp(e_{t,j}). These alignment scores, e_{t,i}, are the beating heart of the mechanism. They mathematically measure how relevant the encoder's hidden state h_i is to the decoder's current state s_{t-1}. There are several ways to compute this relevance score. The simplest is Dot Product Attention, where e_{t,i} = s_{t-1}^T * h_i. This essentially measures cosine similarity. If the vectors are highly aligned in the high-dimensional space, the dot product is large, the softmax assigns a high probability, and the decoder pays intense "attention" to that specific input word. If the dot product is insufficient, we can use General Bilinear Attention, adding a learnable weight matrix W to get e_{t,i} = s_{t-1}^T * W * h_i. Or, we can use Additive Attention, popularized by Bahdanau, which uses a small feed-forward network to compute the score: e_{t,i} = v^T * tanh(W_s * s_{t-1} + W_h * h_i).

    不同的输出标记需要关注不同的特定输入标记，这一认识导致了现代深度学习中最深刻的突破之一：注意力机制（Attention Mechanism）。如果平均池化给每个隐藏状态分配一个静态的、均匀的权重，那么注意力机制就用自适应池化（Adaptive Pooling）取代了它。对于整个解码过程，我们不再使用一个固定的上下文向量 c，而是为每一个解码时间步 t 计算一个动态变化的上下文向量 c_t。在数学上，c_t = Σ w_{t,i} * h_i。这里，w_{t,i} 代表解码器在时间 t 分配给位置 i 处的编码器隐藏状态的特定注意力权重。这种方法的美妙之处在于解码器可以动态地实时调整这些权重。我们如何确保这些权重表现正常？我们强制要求权重 w_{t,i} 形成一个有效的概率分布——它们必须是正数，并且总和恰好为 1。我们通过在一组原始的、未归一化的对齐分数 e_{t,i} 上应用 Softmax 函数来实现这一点。公式是 w_{t,i} = exp(e_{t,i}) / Σ_j exp(e_{t,j})。这些对齐分数 e_{t,i} 是该机制跳动的心脏。它们在数学上衡量了编码器的隐藏状态 h_i 与解码器的当前状态 s_{t-1} 有多大的相关性。有几种计算这种相关性分数的方法。最简单的是点积注意力（Dot Product Attention），其中 e_{t,i} = s_{t-1}^T * h_i。这本质上衡量的是余弦相似度。如果向量在高维空间中高度对齐，则点积很大，softmax 会分配一个高概率，解码器就会对那个特定的输入单词付出强烈的“注意力”。如果点积不够，我们可以使用广义双线性注意力（General Bilinear Attention），添加一个可学习的权重矩阵 W，得到 e_{t,i} = s_{t-1}^T * W * h_i。或者，我们可以使用由 Bahdanau 推广的加性注意力（Additive Attention），它使用一个小型的前馈网络来计算分数：e_{t,i} = v^T * tanh(W_s * s_{t-1} + W_h * h_i)。

  - [14:56 - 15:05] **Formalizing Attention: The Query-Key-Value Framework**

    To truly understand attention and prepare ourselves for the architectures we will discuss next week, we must generalize this concept into the Query-Key-Value framework. This abstraction originally comes from database retrieval systems. Imagine you go to a library database. You type in a search 'Query'. The database compares your query against the 'Keys' (the titles or tags of the books). When a key closely matches your query, the database returns the corresponding 'Value' (the actual content of the book). We map this exact logic onto our neural network. The 'Query' (q_t) represents the information the decoder is actively seeking at the current time step; it is a linear projection of the decoder's previous hidden state, q_t = W_q * s_{t-1}. The 'Key' (k_i) describes the content available at each input position; it is a projection of the encoder's hidden state, k_i = W_k * h_i. The 'Value' (v_i) represents the actual information we will retrieve from the encoder to build our context vector, v_i = W_v * h_i. So, the alignment score e_{t,i} is computed as a function of the Key and the Query: a(k_i, q_t). Once we pass those scores through the softmax to get our weights w_{t,i}, we compute the final context vector c_t as the weighted sum of the Values: c_t = Σ w_{t,i} * v_i. Why do we introduce these learnable matrices W_q, W_k, and W_v instead of just using the raw hidden states? Because it allows the network to transform the representations into a specialized "attention space." For example, the raw hidden state h_i for the word "apple" might contain its grammatical role, its tense, and its semantic meaning. But for the purpose of matching a query, the Key projection matrix W_k might isolate just the semantic concept of "food item." This separation of concerns—Keys for matching, Values for content extraction—makes the attention mechanism incredibly expressive. Note that if we just set W_q, W_k, and W_v to be the identity matrix, we perfectly recover the basic adaptive attention pooling we just discussed.
