    为了真正理解注意力并为我们下周要讨论的架构做好准备，我们必须将这个概念概括为查询-键-值（Query-Key-Value）框架。这种抽象最初来自于数据库检索系统。想象你去一个图书馆数据库。你输入一个搜索“查询（Query）”。数据库将你的查询与“键（Keys）”（书名或标签）进行比较。当一个键与你的查询紧密匹配时，数据库会返回相应的“值（Value）”（书的实际内容）。我们将这种确切的逻辑映射到我们的神经网络上。“查询”（q_t）代表解码器在当前时间步主动寻找的信息；它是解码器先前隐藏状态的线性投影，q_t = W_q * s_{t-1}。“键”（k_i）描述了每个输入位置可用的内容；它是编码器隐藏状态的投影，k_i = W_k * h_i。“值”（v_i）代表我们将从编码器中检索以构建上下文向量的实际信息，v_i = W_v * h_i。因此，对齐分数 e_{t,i} 计算为键和查询的函数：a(k_i, q_t)。一旦我们通过 softmax 传递这些分数以获得我们的权重 w_{t,i}，我们就计算出最终的上下文向量 c_t，作为值的加权和：c_t = Σ w_{t,i} * v_i。为什么我们引入这些可学习的矩阵 W_q、W_k 和 W_v，而不是仅仅使用原始的隐藏状态？因为它允许网络将表示转换到一个专门的“注意力空间”中。例如，单词“apple”的原始隐藏状态 h_i 可能包含其语法作用、时态和语义。但为了匹配查询，键投影矩阵 W_k 可能仅分离出“食品”的语义概念。这种关注点分离——用于匹配的键，用于内容提取的值——使得注意力机制具有难以置信的表达能力。请注意，如果我们只将 W_q、W_k 和 W_v 设置为单位矩阵，我们就完美地恢复了我们刚刚讨论的基本的自适应注意力池化。

  - [15:05 - 15:15] **Visualizing Alignments and the Transition to Multi-Head Attention**

    One of the most satisfying aspects of attention mechanisms is that they are highly interpretable. By extracting the softmax weights w_{t,i} during inference, we can generate heatmaps to see exactly what the model is "looking at" when it translates. If we plot the English source words on the x-axis and the generated French words on the y-axis, the bright white squares on the heatmap show high attention weights. Usually, you see a bright diagonal line, indicating that the network translates word-by-word sequentially. But beautifully, when translating English adjectives and nouns into French, where the word order is often reversed (like "European Economic Area" to "zone économique européenne"), the attention heatmap actually deviates from the diagonal. The model dynamically looks back and forth across the input sequence, perfectly capturing the non-monotonic grammatical alignment between the two languages.

    But this single attention mechanism has a limitation: it can only compute one weighted combination of values at a time. What if different aspects of the sequence require entirely different attention patterns? For instance, when translating a word, we might need one attention mechanism to focus on finding the subject of the sentence, and another to focus on identifying the tense of the verb. This naturally leads us to Multi-Head Attention. Instead of performing a single attention function, we run H independent attention mechanisms, or "heads", in parallel. Each head 'a' has its own completely independent set of learnable projection matrices: W_q^a, W_k^a, and W_v^a. They independently compute their own context vectors, o_i^a = Σ w_{i,j}^a * v_j^a. Finally, we just concatenate the outputs from all these independent heads into one massive vector, o_i = [o_i^1; o_i^2; ...; o_i^H], and pass it through a feed-forward layer. This allows the model to simultaneously attend to information from different representation subspaces at different positions.

    注意力机制最令人满意的一个方面是它们具有高度的可解释性。通过在推理期间提取 softmax 权重 w_{t,i}，我们可以生成热力图，以准确地看到模型在翻译时“正在看”什么。如果我们将英语源单词画在 x 轴上，将生成的法语单词画在 y 轴上，热力图上明亮的白色方块会显示高注意力权重。通常，你会看到一条明亮的对角线，表明网络是在逐字顺序翻译的。但绝妙的是，当把英语的形容词和名词翻译成法语时，由于词序经常颠倒（比如“European Economic Area”变成“zone économique européenne”），注意力热力图实际上偏离了对角线。模型在输入序列中动态地来回查看，完美地捕捉到了两种语言之间非单调的语法对齐关系。但是这种单一的注意力机制有一个局限性：它一次只能计算一个值的加权组合。如果序列的不同方面需要完全不同的注意力模式该怎么办？例如，在翻译一个词时，我们可能需要一个注意力机制来专注于寻找句子的主语，而另一个专注于识别动词的时态。这自然地引出了多头注意力（Multi-Head Attention）。我们并行运行 H 个独立的注意力机制（或“头”），而不是执行单一的注意力功能。每个头 'a' 都有其自己完全独立的可学习投影矩阵集：W_q^a、W_k^a 和 W_v^a。它们独立地计算自己的上下文向量，o_i^a = Σ w_{i,j}^a * v_j^a。最后，我们只需将所有这些独立头的输出连接成一个庞大的向量，o_i = [o_i^1; o_i^2; ...; o_i^H]，并将其传递给前馈层。这允许模型同时关注来自不同位置的不同表示子空间的信息。

  - [15:15 - 15:22] **Multi-Domain Applications: Translating Images to Text**

    To wrap up this segment before we take our break, I want to emphasize that this attention-based encoder-decoder architecture is not restricted to Natural Language Processing. It is a universal framework for sequence conversion. We can dramatically alter the input domain while keeping the core mechanism intact. A phenomenal example of this is Image Captioning. The goal here is to take a raw photograph as input and output a natural language description of that image. How do we do this? We simply swap out the RNN encoder for a Convolutional Neural Network. The CNN processes the image and extracts a grid of spatial feature maps. We can treat each spatial location in that feature map grid as a "token" in a sequence. The decoder remains a standard RNN language model. When generating the caption word-by-step, the decoder uses the exact same attention mechanism to dynamically compute alignment scores against the spatial grid of CNN features.

    If the model is generating the word "frisbee" in the caption "A woman is throwing a frisbee in a park", we can extract the attention weights and overlay them on the original image. You will literally see a bright highlight over the specific pixels containing the frisbee. The model learns to shift its visual "gaze" around the photograph as it speaks. It is a stunning demonstration of how attention bridges the gap between multimodal domains. We will pause here for 10 minutes. When we come back, we will explore the radical idea of discarding the RNN entirely and relying solely on attention, which leads us to the Transformer architecture.

    在我们休息之前结束这一部分，我想强调的是，这种基于注意力的编码器-解码器架构并不局限于自然语言处理。它是一个用于序列转换的通用框架。我们可以极大地改变输入领域，同时保持核心机制完好无损。这方面一个极好的例子是图像字幕生成（Image Captioning）。其目标是接收一张原始照片作为输入，并输出该图像的自然语言描述。我们如何做到这一点呢？我们只需将 RNN 编码器换成卷积神经网络（CNN）。CNN 处理图像并提取出一个空间特征图网格。我们可以将该特征图网格中的每个空间位置视为序列中的一个“标记（token）”。解码器仍然是一个标准的 RNN 语言模型。在逐字生成字幕时，解码器使用完全相同的注意力机制，动态地计算与 CNN 特征的空间网格的对齐分数。如果在字幕“一位女士在公园里扔飞盘”中，模型正在生成单词“飞盘（frisbee）”，我们可以提取注意力权重并将它们叠加在原始图像上。你会真切地看到在包含飞盘的特定像素上有一个明亮的高光。模型学会在它“说话”时在照片上移动它的视觉“目光”。这是一个令人惊叹的演示，展示了注意力如何弥合多模态领域之间的差距。我们将在这里暂停 10 分钟。当我们回来时，我们将探索一个激进的想法，即完全抛弃 RNN，仅仅依靠注意力，这将引导我们进入 Transformer 架构。

  - [15:22 - 15:31] **The Push to Remove Recurrence: Introducing Self-Attention**

    Welcome back. Before the break, we saw how the attention mechanism beautifully solved the information bottleneck between the encoder and the decoder. However, if we look closely at our encoder, a glaring inefficiency remains. We are still using a Recurrent Neural Network to process the input sequence. As we know, RNNs are strictly sequential. To compute the hidden state `h_4`, you absolutely must wait for the computation of `h_3`, which waits for `h_2`, and so on. This sequential nature completely kills our ability to parallelize computations on modern GPU hardware. So, a radical idea emerges: what if we just remove the recurrence entirely? What if we just use a flat, non-recurrent encoder where we process all the input words simultaneously? Let's try a thought experiment. Suppose our input is "Ich habe einen apfel gegessen." If we just pass each word independently through a simple embedding layer, the word "einen" (meaning "an" or "a") gets a fixed vector representation. But in German, as in many languages, context is everything. Depending on the surrounding grammar, that word could need to be translated as "ein", "einer", or "eines". A non-recurrent, independent encoder completely loses this context-specificity because the words never talk to each other. We need the representation of "einen" to inherently understand that it is sitting next to "apfel". Our solution is to take the brilliant concept of attention and apply it *within* the encoder itself. We allow the input words to pay attention to each other to figure out their own context. This is what we call Self-Attention.

    欢迎回来。在休息之前，我们看到了注意力机制如何优美地解决编码器和解码器之间的信息瓶颈。但是，如果我们仔细观察我们的编码器，就会发现仍然存在一个明显的效率低下问题。我们仍然在使用循环神经网络来处理输入序列。众所周知，RNN 严格来说是顺序的。为了计算隐藏状态 `h_4`，你绝对必须等待 `h_3` 的计算，而 `h_3` 又要等待 `h_2`，依此类推。这种顺序性质完全扼杀了我们在现代 GPU 硬件上并行化计算的能力。因此，一个激进的想法出现了：如果我们完全消除循环会怎样？如果我们只使用一个扁平的、非循环的编码器，同时处理所有输入单词会怎样？让我们来做一个思想实验。假设我们的输入是“Ich habe einen apfel gegessen”。如果我们只是将每个单词独立地通过一个简单的嵌入层，单词“einen”（意思是“an”或“a”）会得到一个固定的向量表示。但是在德语中，就像在许多语言中一样，上下文就是一切。根据周围的语法，这个词可能需要被翻译成“ein”、“einer”或“eines”。一个非循环的、独立的编码器完全失去了这种上下文特异性，因为单词之间从不交流。我们需要“einen”的表示内在理解它紧挨着“apfel”。我们的解决方案是采用注意力的绝妙概念，并将其应用在编码器*内部*。我们允许输入单词相互注意，以弄清楚它们自己的上下文。这就是我们所说的自注意力（Self-Attention）。

  - [15:31 - 15:40] **Deconstructing the Single-Head Self-Attention Block**

    Let's break down exactly how this Self-Attention works mathematically using the Query-Key-Value framework we just established. In the encoder, our initial hidden states, `h_i`, are simply the raw word embeddings of our input sequence. For the word "apple" at position `i`, we compute its Query `q_i = W_q * h_i`, its Key `k_i = W_k * h_i`, and its Value `v_i = W_v * h_i`. Notice the profound shift here: the Queries, Keys, and Values are all derived from the exact same source—the input sequence itself. Hence the name, *Self*-Attention. So, how does the word "apple" update its own meaning based on context? It takes its Query vector `q_i` and computes a dot-product alignment score against the Key vectors of *every single word* in the sentence, including itself. We calculate `e_{ij} = q_i^T * k_j`. We then pass these raw scores through a Softmax function to get our normalized attention weights, `w_{ij}`. Finally, the updated, context-rich representation for the word "apple", which we will call `o_i`, is the weighted sum of all the Value vectors: `o_i = Σ w_{ij} * v_j`. I see a hand up in the third row. Yes? "Does a word pay a lot of attention to itself?" That is a fantastic question. Empirically, yes, it often does. The highest attention weight frequently falls on the word itself because its own identity is usually the most important piece of information, but it seamlessly blends in fractions of meaning from the surrounding adjectives or verbs to refine that identity. This entire operation constitutes a "single-head" Self-Attention Block.

    让我们使用刚刚建立的查询-键-值框架，从数学上准确分解这种自注意力是如何工作的。在编码器中，我们初始的隐藏状态 `h_i` 仅仅是输入序列的原始词嵌入。对于位置 `i` 处的单词“apple”，我们计算它的查询 `q_i = W_q * h_i`，它的键 `k_i = W_k * h_i`，以及它的值 `v_i = W_v * h_i`。注意这里深刻的转变：查询、键和值都是从完全相同的来源（输入序列本身）派生出来的。因此得名，*自*注意力（Self-Attention）。那么，单词“apple”如何根据上下文更新自己的含义呢？它获取其查询向量 `q_i`，并计算与句子中*每一个单词*（包括它自己）的键向量的点积对齐分数。我们计算 `e_{ij} = q_i^T * k_j`。然后，我们将这些原始分数通过 Softmax 函数以获得我们归一化的注意力权重 `w_{ij}`。最后，单词“apple”更新后的、上下文丰富的表示（我们称之为 `o_i`）是所有值向量的加权和：`o_i = Σ w_{ij} * v_j`。我看到第三排有同学举手。请说？“一个词会非常关注它自己吗？” 这是一个极好的问题。从经验来看，是的，通常会。最高的注意力权重经常落在单词本身上，因为它自己的身份通常是最重要的信息，但它会无缝地融入周围形容词或动词的少量含义，以细化该身份。这整个操作构成了一个“单头（single-head）”自注意力块。

  - [15:40 - 15:48] **Scaling Up: Multi-Head Self-Attention and MLPs**

    Just as we saw with the decoder earlier, relying on a single attention head restricts the model to focusing on only one type of relationship per word. But language is vastly more complex than that. A single word might simultaneously act as the direct object of a verb while also being modified by an adjective located three words away. To capture this multi-faceted reality, we expand our architecture to Multi-Head Self-Attention. We instantiate `H` separate, independent self-attention heads running in parallel. Each head, let's denote it with superscript `a`, has its own unique projection matrices `W_q^a`, `W_k^a`, and `W_v^a`. Consequently, each head computes its own independent context vector `o_i^a` for every word. To consolidate this wealth of information, we simply concatenate all these head outputs together into one massive vector: `o_i = [o_i^1; o_i^2; ...; o_i^H]`. But we don't stop there. To further process and integrate this concatenated information, we pass this massive vector through a standard Multi-Layer Perceptron, or MLP, which consists of a couple of fully connected layers. It is crucial to note that the exact same MLP weights are applied independently to every single token's representation across the sequence. This entire pipeline—the parallel multi-head self-attention followed by the shared MLP—forms the foundational Multi-head Self Attention Block. In modern architectures, we aggressively stack these blocks on top of each other. The output representations from block 1 become the inputs `h_i` for block 2, allowing the network to build increasingly profound, abstract levels of contextual understanding with every layer.

    正如我们之前在解码器中看到的那样，依赖单个注意力头会限制模型每个词只能关注一种类型的关系。但语言要比这复杂得多。一个词可能同时充当动词的直接宾语，同时又被位于三个词之外的形容词修饰。为了捕捉这种多面性的现实，我们将架构扩展到多头自注意力（Multi-Head Self-Attention）。我们实例化 `H` 个独立的、并行运行的自注意力头。每个头（我们用上标 `a` 表示）都有其自己独特的投影矩阵 `W_q^a`、`W_k^a` 和 `W_v^a`。因此，每个头为每个词计算其自己独立的上下文向量 `o_i^a`。为了巩固这些丰富的信息，我们简单地将所有这些头的输出连接在一起，形成一个巨大的向量：`o_i = [o_i^1; o_i^2; ...; o_i^H]`。但我们不止于此。为了进一步处理和整合这些连接的信息，我们将这个庞大的向量通过一个标准的多层感知机（MLP），它由几个全连接层组成。至关重要的是要注意，完全相同的 MLP 权重被独立地应用于整个序列中每一个标记的表示。这整个流水线——并行的多头自注意力，随后是共享的 MLP——构成了基础的多头自注意力块。在现代架构中，我们将这些块大量地堆叠在一起。块 1 的输出表示成为块 2 的输入 `h_i`，允许网络通过每一层建立越来越深刻、抽象的上下文理解水平。

  - [15:48 - 15:58] **The Fatal Flaw: Permutation Equivariance**

    Now, we have built a beautiful, highly parallelizable encoder block. But if we deploy it exactly as I just described, it will fail spectacularly at understanding language. Why? I want you to perform a mathematical exercise in your head. Look at the dot-product attention formula: `e_{ij} = q_i^T * k_j`. Now, imagine I take the input sentence and completely scramble the order of the words. I swap the first word with the last word. What happens to the math? The queries and keys simply swap their indices, the softmax weights perfectly swap to follow them, and the final weighted sum `o_i` for that specific word remains identically the same. Mathematically, the Self-Attention block we just defined is a "permutation equivariant" function. It means the output representation of a word is completely, fundamentally invariant to the actual sequential ordering of the other words in the sentence. It treats a sentence simply as a "bag of words." If you feed it "Dog bites man" and "Man bites dog," the core mathematical features extracted for the word "bites" will be indistinguishable. It ignores relative position. An adjacent word is mathematically treated exactly the same as a word three paragraphs away. This is catastrophic for sequence modeling, because in language and time-series data, the order of events is often the most critical piece of information. Since we ruthlessly stripped away the Recurrent Neural Network to gain parallel speed, we have inadvertently lost our entire concept of time and sequence. We must find a way to explicitly inject this lost sense of time back into the model without reintroducing the slow recurrence.

    现在，我们已经构建了一个优美的、高度可并行的编码器块。但如果我们完全按照我刚才描述的那样部署它，它在理解语言方面将一败涂地。为什么？我想让你们在脑海中做一个数学练习。看看点积注意力公式：`e_{ij} = q_i^T * k_j`。现在，想象我把输入句子中的单词顺序完全打乱。我把第一个词和最后一个词互换。数学上会发生什么？查询和键只是交换了它们的索引，softmax 权重完美地交换以跟随它们，而该特定单词的最终加权和 `o_i` 保持完全相同。在数学上，我们刚刚定义的自注意力块是一个“排列等变（permutation equivariant）”函数。这意味着一个词的输出表示完全、根本地独立于句子中其他词的实际顺序排列。它仅仅把句子看作一个“词袋”。如果你输入“Dog bites man（狗咬人）”和“Man bites dog（人咬狗）”，为“bites（咬）”这个词提取的核心数学特征将是无法区分的。它忽略了相对位置。一个相邻的词在数学上的处理方式与三段之外的词完全相同。这对于序列建模来说是灾难性的，因为在语言和时间序列数据中，事件的顺序通常是最关键的信息。既然我们为了获得并行速度而无情地剥离了循环神经网络，我们就在无意中完全失去了对时间和序列的概念。我们必须找到一种方法，将这种失去的时间感明确地重新注入到模型中，而又不重新引入缓慢的循环。

  - [15:58 - 16:08] **The Missing Ingredient: Positional Encoding**

    The elegant solution to this problem is called Positional Encoding. The goal is to design a mathematical vector that encodes the specific spatial location of a token, and we simply add this position vector directly to the word embedding before it even enters the self-attention block. So, Hidden State `h_i` becomes Word Embedding plus Positional Encoding. But what should this positional encoding vector look like? We want it to capture relative distance smoothly. The attention mechanism needs to easily recognize if two words are adjacent or far apart. The original Transformer architecture achieves this by utilizing overlapping sine and cosine wave functions of varying frequencies. For a specific position `t` in the sequence, the positional encoding vector `P_t` is defined such that its elements are `sin(w_l * t)` and `cos(w_l * t)`, where the frequency `w_l` decreases exponentially as you move down the dimensions of the vector.

    Why use sine and cosine waves? It relies on a beautiful property of trigonometry: the double angle formulas. If you want to compute the positional encoding for a future time step, `P_{t+tau}`, you can mathematically express it as a strict linear transformation of the current time step `P_t`, specifically `P_{t+tau} = M_tau * P_t`, where `M_tau` is a rotation matrix that depends exclusively on the relative distance `tau`. This incredible linear relationship allows the self-attention mechanism to effortlessly learn shift-invariant, relative-distance relationships. If the math feels abstract, think of it like a binary counter. When you count from 0 to 7 in binary (000, 001, 010, 011...), the lowest bit flips very rapidly (0, 1, 0, 1). The next bit flips half as fast, and the highest bit flips very slowly. Positional encoding does exactly this, but using continuous, smooth sine waves instead of discrete, jarring binary flips. The first few dimensions of the encoding vector oscillate rapidly to differentiate adjacent words, while the deeper dimensions oscillate slowly to track macroscopic positions within a long document. By adding these continuous "time-stamps" to our word embeddings, our parallel multi-head attention blocks instantly regain a profound understanding of sentence structure and word order.

    这个问题的优雅解决方案被称为位置编码（Positional Encoding）。目标是设计一个数学向量来编码标记的特定空间位置，我们在它进入自注意力块之前，简单地将这个位置向量直接添加到词嵌入中。因此，隐藏状态 `h_i` 变成了词嵌入加上位置编码。但是这个位置编码向量应该是什么样的呢？我们希望它能平滑地捕捉相对距离。注意力机制需要能够轻易识别两个词是相邻还是相距很远。最初的 Transformer 架构通过利用不同频率的重叠正弦和余弦波函数来实现这一点。对于序列中的特定位置 `t`，位置编码向量 `P_t` 的定义是使其元素为 `sin(w_l * t)` 和 `cos(w_l * t)`，其中频率 `w_l` 随着你沿着向量的维度向下移动呈指数级下降。

    为什么要使用正弦和余弦波？它依赖于三角学的一个优美特性：二倍角公式。如果你想计算未来时间步的位置编码 `P_{t+tau}`，你可以在数学上将其表达为当前时间步 `P_t` 的严格线性变换，具体为 `P_{t+tau} = M_tau * P_t`，其中 `M_tau` 是一个完全取决于相对距离 `tau` 的旋转矩阵。这种令人难以置信的线性关系使得自注意力机制能够毫不费力地学习平移不变的相对距离关系。如果觉得这个数学有些抽象，可以把它想象成一个二进制计数器。当你在二进制中从 0 数到 7 时（000, 001, 010, 011...），最低位翻转得非常快（0, 1, 0, 1）。下一位翻转的速度只有它的一半，而最高位翻转得非常慢。位置编码正是这样做的，但使用的是连续、平滑的正弦波，而不是离散、刺耳的二进制翻转。编码向量的前几个维度快速振荡以区分相邻的词，而更深的维度则缓慢振荡以跟踪长文档中的宏观位置。通过将这些连续的“时间戳”添加到我们的词嵌入中，我们并行的多头注意力块瞬间恢复了对句子结构和词序的深刻理解。

  - [16:08 - 16:16] **Adapting Self-Attention for the Decoder: The Masking Problem**

    So, we have completely redesigned the encoder using Multi-Head Self-Attention and Positional Encoding, resulting in a lightning-fast, parallelizable architecture that perfectly understands context. The natural next question is: can we simply copy and paste this exact same self-attention block into the decoder? The answer is a resounding no. Think fundamentally about how the decoder operates. The decoder is inherently autoregressive; it must generate the output sentence sequentially, one word at a time. If we are translating into "I ate an apple," when the decoder is currently trying to generate the word "ate", it should only have access to the previously generated word, "I". However, if we blindly use standard self-attention, the query for "ate" would calculate dot-products with all the keys in the sequence, allowing it to "look ahead" and see the future word "apple" before it has even been generated! This breaks the fundamental laws of causality and autoregressive modeling. To fix this, we must introduce Masked Self-Attention. The mechanism is mathematically elegant. After we compute the raw dot-product alignment scores e_ij, but right before we feed them into the Softmax function, we apply a causal mask. We forcefully set the score e_ij to negative infinity for any token j that comes chronologically after the current token i. Why negative infinity? Because when you pass negative infinity into an exponential Softmax function, it evaluates to exactly zero. Therefore, the attention weights for all future tokens become mathematically zero, completely preventing future information from leaking into the past computations.

    因此，我们使用多头自注意力和位置编码完全重新设计了编码器，产生了一个闪电般快速、可并行化且完美理解上下文的架构。接下来自然会问的一个问题是：我们能简单地将这个完全相同的自注意力块复制并粘贴到解码器中吗？答案是响亮的“不”。从根本上思考一下解码器是如何运作的。解码器本质上是自回归的；它必须按顺序，一个词一个词地生成输出句子。如果我们正在翻译成“I ate an apple”，当解码器当前试图生成单词“ate”时，它应该只能访问先前生成的单词“I”。然而，如果我们盲目地使用标准的自注意力，“ate”的查询将计算与序列中所有键的点积，允许它“向前看”并在未来的单词“apple”产生之前就看到它！这打破了因果关系和自回归建模的基本规律。为了解决这个问题，我们必须引入掩码自注意力（Masked Self-Attention）。其机制在数学上非常优雅。在我们计算出原始的点积对齐分数 e_ij 之后，但在我们将它们输入到 Softmax 函数之前，我们应用一个因果掩码。对于在时间顺序上晚于当前标记 i 的任何标记 j，我们强制将其分数 e_ij 设置为负无穷大。为什么是负无穷大？因为当你将负无穷大传递给指数型的 Softmax 函数时，它的计算结果恰好为零。因此，所有未来标记的注意力权重在数学上都变为零，完全防止了未来信息泄漏到过去的计算中。

  - [16:16 - 16:25] **Training Parallelism vs. Inference Bottlenecks**

    I see a student looking a bit skeptical in the second row. You are probably thinking: "Professor, if the decoder still has to generate words sequentially and mask out the future, didn't we just lose all the parallel computing speed we gained by dropping the RNN?" That is an incredibly sharp observation. This brings us back to the concept of Teacher Forcing we discussed an hour ago. During inference, when the model is deployed in the real world, you are absolutely correct. The decoder is handcuffed by its own sequential nature; it must wait for the output of step t to compute step t+1. Inference in Transformers is inherently sequential and thus computationally bottlenecked. However, during training, the story is entirely different! During training, we already possess the complete ground-truth target sequence. We can feed the entire target sentence into the decoder simultaneously. By applying our causal mask—which practically looks like a lower-triangular matrix of ones and an upper-triangular matrix of negative infinities—we can compute the masked attention scores for every single word in the target sentence at the exact same time using massive, highly optimized matrix multiplications on the GPU. So, we completely eliminate the RNN's sequential bottleneck during training, scaling up to massive datasets, even if we are stuck with sequential generation during inference.

    我看到第二排有位同学看起来有点怀疑。你可能在想：“教授，如果解码器仍然需要按顺序生成单词并掩盖未来，我们难道不是失去了因放弃 RNN 而获得的所有并行计算速度吗？” 这是一个非常敏锐的观察。这就把我们带回到了一个小时前我们讨论过的教师强制（Teacher Forcing）的概念。在推理期间，当模型部署在现实世界中时，你绝对是正确的。解码器被其自身的顺序性质所束缚；它必须等待步骤 t 的输出才能计算步骤 t+1。Transformer 中的推理本质上是顺序的，因此是计算瓶颈。然而，在训练期间，情况就完全不同了！在训练期间，我们已经拥有了完整的真实目标序列。我们可以将整个目标句子同时输入到解码器中。通过应用我们的因果掩码——实际上看起来像一个全为一下三角矩阵和一个全为负无穷大上三角矩阵——我们可以利用 GPU 上大规模、高度优化的矩阵乘法，同时计算目标句子中每一个单词的掩码注意力分数。因此，我们在训练期间完全消除了 RNN 的顺序瓶颈，从而可以扩展到庞大的数据集，即使我们在推理期间受限于顺序生成。

  - [16:25 - 16:34] **The Missing Link: Encoder-Decoder Cross-Attention**

    Okay, so now our decoder understands its own past generation through the Masked Multi-Head Self-Attention block. But wait, it still hasn't looked at the original German input sentence! If it only looks at its own output, it is just hallucinating text, not translating. We need a mechanism to explicitly bridge the gap between the two halves of the network. We achieve this by inserting a second, distinctly different attention block into the decoder, universally known as Cross-Attention or Encoder-Decoder Attention. In the self-attention blocks we built earlier, the Queries, Keys, and Values were all generated from the exact same sequence. In Cross-Attention, we radically split the sources. The Queries (q_t)—which mathematically represent what the decoder is actively looking for at the current step—are derived directly from the output of the decoder's previous layer. However, the Keys (k_i) and Values (v_i)—which represent the raw content and context dictionary—are derived entirely from the final output of the Encoder stack. This is the exact moment where the translation happens. The decoder asks, "I just generated 'I ate', what German concept should I focus on next?" and it calculates the dot-product similarity between its decoder-query and all the encoder-keys to find the answer. This mathematically mirrors the original sequence-to-sequence Bahdanau attention we studied, but now it is distributed across multiple parallel heads.

    好了，现在我们的解码器通过掩码多头自注意力块理解了它自己过去的生成。但是等等，它还没有看过原始的德语输入句子！如果它只看自己的输出，那它只是在产生文本幻觉，而不是在翻译。我们需要一种机制来明确地弥合网络两半之间的差距。我们通过在解码器中插入第二个截然不同的注意力块来实现这一点，通常称为交叉注意力（Cross-Attention）或编码器-解码器注意力。在我们之前构建的自注意力块中，查询、键和值都是从完全相同的序列生成的。在交叉注意力中，我们从根本上拆分了来源。查询（q_t）——在数学上代表解码器在当前步骤积极寻找的内容——直接从解码器前一层的输出派生。然而，键（k_i）和值（v_i）——代表原始内容和上下文字典——完全从编码器堆栈的最终输出派生。这就是翻译发生的准确时刻。解码器问：“我刚刚生成了‘I ate’，接下来我应该关注哪个德语概念？” 然后它计算其解码器查询与所有编码器键之间的点积相似度以找到答案。这在数学上反映了我们研究过的原始序列到序列 Bahdanau 注意力，但现在它分布在多个平行的头中。

  - [16:34 - 16:45] **The Complete Transformer and the Importance of Layer Normalization**

    We can now snap all these modules together to form the complete architecture famously introduced in the 2017 paper "Attention is All You Need" by Vaswani et al. If you look at the full schematic, you will notice that every single attention sub-layer and every feed-forward MLP is strictly enclosed within an "Add & Norm" operation. The "Add" part represents a residual skip connection, exactly like the ones we analyzed in ResNets a few weeks ago. The input to the attention block bypasses the mathematical heavy lifting and is added directly to the output: x_new = Norm(x_old + F(x_old)). As we learned, this creates a pristine, uninterrupted highway for gradients to flow backward through the network, completely preventing the vanishing gradient problem in deep Transformers. The "Norm" part is equally critical, but pay close attention: this is Layer Normalization, NOT Batch Normalization. Why don't we use the Batch Norm we love so much for CNNs? Because text data is highly variable. Sentences in a single batch have vastly different lengths, meaning we pad them with lots of empty zero-tokens. Computing batch statistics across a dimension filled with variable padding introduces massive mathematical instability. Layer Normalization fixes this by ignoring the batch dimension entirely. Instead, it computes the mean and variance across the feature dimension for each specific token independently. Every single word vector is normalized in isolation. As a modern architectural footnote, while the original 2017 paper applied normalization after the addition (Post-norm), almost all state-of-the-art models today, like the GPT series, use Pre-norm: x_new = x_old + F(Norm(x_old)). Pre-norm makes learning the identity function easier and yields significantly more stable gradients during early training phases.

    我们现在可以将所有这些模块拼接在一起，形成 Vaswani 等人在 2017 年的著名论文“Attention is All You Need”中引入的完整架构。如果你查看完整的原理图，你会注意到每一个单一的注意力子层和每一个前馈 MLP 都被严格封装在一个“Add & Norm（相加和归一化）”操作中。“Add”部分代表残差跳跃连接，就像我们几周前在 ResNets 中分析的那样。注意力块的输入绕过了繁重的数学计算，直接添加到输出中：x_new = Norm(x_old + F(x_old))。正如我们所学，这为梯度在网络中向后流动创造了一条原始的、不间断的高速公路，完全防止了深度 Transformer 中的梯度消失问题。“Norm”部分同样关键，但请密切注意：这是层归一化（Layer Normalization），而不是批量归一化（Batch Normalization）。我们为什么不使用我们在 CNN 中非常喜欢的 Batch Norm 呢？因为文本数据的可变性极强。同一个批次中的句子长度差异巨大，这意味着我们会用大量的空零标记来填充它们。在一个充满可变填充的维度上计算批次统计数据会带来巨大的数学不稳定性。层归一化通过完全忽略批次维度来解决这个问题。相反，它独立地计算每个特定标记在特征维度上的均值和方差。每一个词向量都被孤立地归一化。作为一个现代架构的脚注，虽然最初的 2017 年论文在相加之后应用归一化（Post-norm），但今天几乎所有最先进的模型（如 GPT 系列）都使用 Pre-norm（前置归一化）：x_new = x_old + F(Norm(x_old))。Pre-norm 使得学习恒等函数变得更容易，并且在早期训练阶段产生明显更稳定的梯度。

  - [16:45 - 16:55] **Scaled Dot-Product Attention: Saving the Gradients**

    I want to close today's lecture by zooming in on one tiny, easily overlooked mathematical detail that will absolutely ruin your model if you forget it. It's called Scaled Dot-Product Attention. When we compute the raw alignment scores e_ij = q_i^T * k_j, we are taking the dot product of two vectors of dimension d_k. Statistically, if the components of q and k have a mean of 0 and a variance of 1, the resulting dot product will have a mean of 0 but a massively inflated variance of d_k. In modern Transformers, d_k can be 512, 1024, or even higher. This means your dot products will routinely generate extreme numbers, like +400 or -300. Why is this lethal? Remember that these scores are immediately fed into a Softmax function to become probabilities. If you feed extreme numbers into Softmax, the function completely saturates. The largest value gets a probability of essentially 1.0, and every other value is crushed to 0.0. The probability distribution becomes a sharp, jagged cliff. And as we know from calculus, when the Softmax outputs a hard 1.0, the local gradient becomes infinitesimally small. Your model stops learning entirely because the gradients vanish to zero. The brilliant, painfully simple fix is to manually scale down the variance. We divide the raw dot product by the square root of the dimension size: e_ij = (q_i^T * k_j) / sqrt(d_k). By dividing by the square root of d_k, we artificially force the variance of the dot product back down to exactly 1.0, regardless of how wide the network gets. This keeps the values in the sweet, sensitive middle region of the Softmax function, guaranteeing that your gradients flow smoothly and your Transformer actually converges.

    在结束今天的课程之前，我想放大一个微小且容易被忽视的数学细节，如果你忘记了它，绝对会毁了你的模型。它被称为缩放点积注意力（Scaled Dot-Product Attention）。当我们计算原始对齐分数 e_ij = q_i^T * k_j 时，我们是在计算两个维度为 d_k 的向量的点积。从统计学上讲，如果 q 和 k 的分量均值为 0，方差为 1，那么得到的点积的均值将为 0，但方差会极度膨胀到 d_k。在现代 Transformer 中，d_k 可以是 512、1024 甚至更高。这意味着你的点积会经常产生极端的数字，比如 +400 或 -300。为什么这是致命的？记住，这些分数会立即被送入 Softmax 函数以变成概率。如果你把极端的数字输入 Softmax，该函数会完全饱和。最大值的概率基本上是 1.0，而其他所有值都被挤压到 0.0。概率分布变成了一个尖锐的、参差不齐的悬崖。正如我们从微积分中知道的那样，当 Softmax 输出硬性的 1.0 时，局部梯度会变得无限小。你的模型完全停止学习，因为梯度消失为零。绝妙而极其简单的修复方法是手动缩小方差。我们将原始点积除以维度大小的平方根：e_ij = (q_i^T * k_j) / sqrt(d_k)。通过除以 d_k 的平方根，我们人为地迫使点积的方差降回刚好为 1.0，无论网络变得多宽。这使这些值保持在 Softmax 函数甜蜜、敏感的中间区域，保证你的梯度平滑流动，并确保你的 Transformer 真正收敛。

  - [16:55 - 17:08] **Vision Transformers: Treating Images as Words**

    Let's bring everything we've learned full circle. We just spent an hour mathematically proving how powerful the Transformer architecture is for sequential text data. But what about computer vision? For years, Convolutional Neural Networks, the CNNs we studied in Week 4, were the undisputed kings of image processing. The brilliant question asked by Dosovitskiy and his team in their landmark paper "An Image is Worth 16x16 Words" was simple: can we just throw away the CNN entirely and feed an image directly into a standard text Transformer? The answer is yes, and it gave birth to the Vision Transformer, or ViT. The core engineering challenge is that a Transformer expects a 1D sequence of tokens, but an image is a 3D tensor: Height by Width by Channels. If we treated every single pixel as a word, an HD image would create a sequence hundreds of thousands of tokens long, completely blowing up the N^2 memory bottleneck of the self-attention matrix. The elegant solution is to treat patches of the image as words. We carve the input image into a rigid grid of patches, typically 16 by 16 pixels. If the original image is 224 by 224, we end up with exactly 196 patches. We then literally flatten each 16 by 16 by 3 RGB patch into a single 1D vector. We pass each of these flattened vectors through a trainable linear projection matrix, let's call it E, to map them into the constant hidden dimension D used by the Transformer. This simple matrix multiplication creates our "patch embeddings," completely analogous to word embeddings in NLP. We then prepend a special, trainable Class Token, denoted as , to the very front of this sequence. Because self-attention allows all tokens to exchange information globally, by the time this sequence reaches the final layer, that  token has aggressively aggregated a global summary of the entire image, perfectly primed for a final classification MLP. Finally, we add our 1D positional embeddings to the patches, because flattening the image completely destroyed its 2D spatial layout. We just feed this patched sequence straight into a standard Transformer Encoder. No convolutions required.

    让我们把所学的一切融会贯通。我们刚刚花了一个小时从数学上证明了 Transformer 架构对于序列文本数据有多么强大。但是计算机视觉呢？多年来，我们在第 4 周学习的卷积神经网络（CNN）一直是图像处理领域无可争议的王者。Dosovitskiy 和他的团队在他们的里程碑式论文《一张图片等价于 16x16 个单词》中提出了一个绝妙的问题：我们能否完全抛弃 CNN，直接将图像输入到标准的文本 Transformer 中？答案是肯定的，这催生了视觉 Transformer，简称 ViT。核心的工程挑战在于，Transformer 期望接收的是一维的标记序列，但图像是一个三维张量：高度乘以宽度乘以通道数。如果我们把每一个像素都当作一个单词，一张高清图像将产生数十万个标记长的序列，彻底撑爆自注意力矩阵 N 平方的内存瓶颈。优雅的解决方案是将图像的“图块（patches）”视为单词。我们将输入图像切割成严格的图块网格，通常是 16 乘 16 像素。如果原始图像是 224 乘 224，我们最终会得到正好 196 个图块。然后我们真切地将每一个 16 乘 16 乘 3 的 RGB 图块展平为一个单一的一维向量。我们将这些展平的向量分别通过一个可训练的线性投影矩阵（我们称之为 E），将它们映射到 Transformer 使用的恒定隐藏维度 D 中。这个简单的矩阵乘法创建了我们的“图块嵌入（patch embeddings）”，完全类似于 NLP 中的词嵌入。然后我们在整个序列的最前面预先添加一个特殊的可训练分类标记，记为 。因为自注意力允许所有标记在全局交换信息，当这个序列到达最后一层时，那个  标记已经积极地聚合了整个图像的全局摘要，为最终的分类 MLP 做好了完美的准备。最后，我们将一维位置嵌入添加到图块中，因为展平图像彻底破坏了其二维空间布局。我们只需将这个分块的序列直接送入标准的 Transformer 编码器。不需要任何卷积。

  - [17:08 - 17:22] **Inductive Bias and the Pre-Norm Architectural Tweak**

    Before we evaluate how ViT performs, we need to address a critical architectural detail: Pre-norm versus Post-norm. The original Transformer paper used Post-norm, meaning the layer normalization occurred strictly after the residual addition. Mathematically, x_t+1 = Norm(x_t + F_t(x_t)). Post-norm tightly controls the variance of the representations, but it creates notoriously unstable gradients during early training, requiring complex learning rate warmup schedules. Modern architectures, including ViT, universally switch to Pre-norm. Here, normalization happens inside the residual block, before the attention or MLP layers: x_t+1 = x_t + F_t(Norm(x_t)). Pre-norm creates a pure, unnormalized residual highway from the very first layer to the very last, making it vastly easier for the network to learn the identity function and ensuring highly stable gradients. Now, with that sorted, how does ViT actually compare to a CNN like ResNet? We must look at this through the lens of Inductive Bias. CNNs possess a massively strong inductive bias; the convolution operation explicitly assumes that nearby pixels are highly correlated (locality) and that a cat in the top-left corner is the same as a cat in the bottom-right (translation equivariance). CNNs are hard-coded to understand 2D physics. ViT, however, has an incredibly weak inductive bias. Self-attention doesn't know what a 2D grid is. It treats the patches merely as a 1D sequence and has to painfully discover the concept of 2D locality from scratch purely by analyzing the positional embeddings over millions of iterations. What is the consequence of this? If you train ViT on a moderate dataset like ImageNet, which has 1 million images, it actually performs worse than a CNN. It lacks the built-in priors to learn efficiently. But, if you train it on a gargantuan, massive dataset like Google's internal JFT-300M, which has 300 million images, the Vision Transformer crushes the CNN. With enough data, the Transformer's raw, unconstrained capacity allows it to learn spatial relationships that are actually superior to the rigid, hard-coded rules of convolutions. As a fascinating hybrid, researchers later developed the Conformer, which explicitly combines convolution modules to capture short-range local structures with self-attention modules to capture long-range global structures, yielding state-of-the-art results in audio and speech recognition.

    在评估 ViT 的性能之前，我们需要解决一个关键的架构细节：Pre-norm（前置归一化）与 Post-norm（后置归一化）。最初的 Transformer 论文使用 Post-norm，意味着层归一化严格发生在残差相加之后。数学上，x_t+1 = Norm(x_t + F_t(x_t))。Post-norm 严格控制表示的方差，但在早期训练期间会产生出了名的不稳定梯度，需要复杂的学习率预热策略。现代架构，包括 ViT，普遍切换到 Pre-norm。在这里，归一化发生在残差块内部，在注意力或 MLP 层之前：x_t+1 = x_t + F_t(Norm(x_t))。Pre-norm 创造了一条从第一层到最后一层纯粹的、未归一化的残差高速公路，使得网络学习恒等函数变得容易得多，并确保了高度稳定的梯度。现在，解决了这个问题后，ViT 实际上与 ResNet 等 CNN 相比如何？我们必须通过归纳偏置（Inductive Bias）的视角来看待这个问题。CNN 拥有极其强大的归纳偏置；卷积操作明确假设相邻的像素高度相关（局部性），并且左上角的猫与右下角的猫是相同的（平移等变性）。CNN 被硬编码为理解二维物理学。然而，ViT 的归纳偏置极其微弱。自注意力不知道什么是二维网格。它仅仅将图块视为一维序列，并且必须纯粹通过在数百万次迭代中分析位置嵌入，从头开始痛苦地发现二维局部性的概念。这有什么后果？如果你在一个中等规模的数据集（如拥有 100 万张图像的 ImageNet）上训练 ViT，它的表现实际上不如 CNN。它缺乏内置的先验来高效地学习。但是，如果你在一个巨大、海量的数据集（如 Google 内部拥有 3 亿张图像的 JFT-300M）上训练它，视觉 Transformer 就会碾压 CNN。有了足够的数据，Transformer 原始的、不受约束的容量允许它学习到实际上优于卷积那种僵化、硬编码规则的空间关系。作为一种迷人的混合体，研究人员后来开发了 Conformer，它明确地将捕捉短程局部结构的卷积模块与捕捉长程全局结构的自注意力模块结合起来，在音频和语音识别领域取得了最先进的成果。

  - [17:22 - 17:38] **The Tokenizer Paradigm and Pretraining Regimes (BERT, T5, GPT)**

    The realization that Transformers can process text, images via patches, or audio via spectrograms brings us to a profound philosophical shift in deep learning: we should stop thinking of Transformers as text models and start viewing them universally as "token sequence processors." As long as you possess a robust Tokenizer—an algorithm to chop your raw input data into discrete mathematical units—the Transformer can learn its structure. This universal capability triggered the era of Large Scale Pretraining. Instead of training small models on narrow datasets from scratch, we use massive compute clusters to pretrain gargantuan Transformers on the entire internet using self-supervised objectives, and then fine-tune them. There are three dominant regimes for this. First, we have Encoder-only models, famously championed by BERT. BERT uses a masked language modeling objective. We take an input sentence, randomly corrupt or hide 15 percent of the tokens, and force the encoder to predict them using bidirectional context—meaning it looks at both the left and the right surrounding words simultaneously. This creates phenomenally deep representations, making BERT the undisputed champion for understanding tasks like sentiment classification or token labeling. Second, we have Encoder-Decoder models, spearheaded by Google's T5. T5 treats absolutely every NLP problem as a "text-to-text" framing task. Whether you are translating, summarizing, or answering questions, the input is a text string containing the task instructions, and the output is a newly generated text string. It pretrains by corrupting entire spans of text and forcing the decoder to autoregressively reconstruct the missing spans. Finally, we have Decoder-only models, the architecture behind the revolutionary GPT series. GPT strips away the encoder entirely. It relies purely on Masked Self-Attention, meaning it is strictly causal. Every token can only attend to its past. The sole training objective is incredibly simple: predict the next token in the sequence.

    You might ask, "Isn't predicting the next token vastly simpler than BERT's bidirectional masking?" Yes, but that simplicity is its superpower. Because the objective is uniform and open-ended, if you scale a Decoder-only model up to hundreds of billions of parameters, a miraculous phenomenon occurs: In-context Learning. The model becomes so good at modeling the statistical distribution of human language that you no longer need to fine-tune its weights to teach it a new task. You simply prompt the model with an instruction in plain English, provide zero or maybe three examples in the input sequence, and the model instantly adapts to the task merely by continuing to predict the next token. This prompting paradigm has completely reshaped the AI industry.

    认识到 Transformer 可以处理文本、通过图块处理图像或通过频谱图处理音频，为我们在深度学习中带来了一个深刻的哲学转变：我们应该停止将 Transformer 视为文本模型，开始将它们普遍视为“标记序列处理器（token sequence processors）”。只要你拥有一个强大的分词器（Tokenizer）——一种将原始输入数据切分为离散数学单元的算法——Transformer 就能学习其结构。这种通用能力引发了大规模预训练（Large Scale Pretraining）的时代。我们不再从头开始在狭窄的数据集上训练小模型，而是使用庞大的计算集群，利用自监督目标在整个互联网的数据上预训练巨大的 Transformer，然后再对它们进行微调。这主要有三种占主导地位的模式。首先是仅编码器（Encoder-only）模型，以著名的 BERT 为代表。BERT 使用掩码语言建模目标。我们选取一个输入句子，随机破坏或隐藏 15% 的标记，并强迫编码器使用双向上下文（意味着它同时查看左侧和右侧的周围单词）来预测它们。这创造了惊人深度的表示，使 BERT 成为诸如情感分类或标记标注等理解任务的无可争议的冠军。其次是编码器-解码器（Encoder-Decoder）模型，以 Google 的 T5 为先锋。T5 将绝对的每一个 NLP 问题都视为“文本到文本（text-to-text）”框架任务。无论你是在翻译、总结还是回答问题，输入都是包含任务指令的文本字符串，而输出是新生成的文本字符串。它通过破坏整段文本并迫使解码器自回归地重建缺失的片段来进行预训练。最后是仅解码器（Decoder-only）模型，即革命性的 GPT 系列背后的架构。GPT 完全剥离了编码器。它纯粹依赖于掩码自注意力，这意味着它是严格因果的。每一个标记只能关注它的过去。唯一的训练目标极其简单：预测序列中的下一个标记。

    你可能会问：“预测下一个标记难道不比 BERT 的双向掩码简单得多吗？” 是的，但那种简单性正是它的超能力。因为目标是统一和开放式的，如果你将仅解码器模型扩展到数千亿个参数，就会出现一个奇迹般的现象：上下文学习（In-context Learning）。模型在模拟人类语言统计分布方面变得如此出色，以至于你不再需要微调其权重来教它新任务。你只需用纯英语向模型提供指令提示，在输入序列中提供零个或可能是三个例子，模型就会仅仅通过继续预测下一个标记，瞬间适应这项任务。这种提示模式彻底重塑了 AI 行业。

  - [17:38 - 17:50] **Course Wrap-Up: Looking Back and the Path Forward**

    As we arrive at the end of Lecture 11, we are also arriving at the conclusion of our formal curriculum for DSA 5204. I want to take a brief moment to look back at the incredible journey we have taken this semester. We started months ago with the basic mathematics of linear regression and the fundamental realization that deep learning is merely a sophisticated mechanism for extracting feature representations. We moved from shallow networks to Deep Fully Connected Neural Networks, uncovering the curse of dimensionality and the mechanics of Backpropagation. We explored Convolutional Neural Networks, understanding how structural priors like translation invariance allow us to master computer vision. We fought the vanishing gradients of Recurrent Neural Networks with LSTMs, and eventually transcended recurrence entirely today by discovering the parallel power of Self-Attention and Transformers. Along the way, we didn't just build architectures; we learned the rigorous science of training them. We diagnosed overfitting, applied regularization techniques like L2 weight decay and Dropout, stabilized activations with Batch Normalization, and optimized our loss landscapes with Stochastic Gradient Descent and Adam. We even ventured into generative modeling, mathematically dissecting Variational Autoencoders and witnessing how to map hidden, continuous latent spaces.

    This class has been an intensive, overarching introduction to deep learning. But please understand, the field is moving at blistering speed, and we have only scratched the surface. There are vast territories we did not have time to cover in depth, such as the mathematical elegance of Diffusion Models that power modern image generation, the strategic complexities of Deep Reinforcement Learning, or the nuanced data-curation strategies of Contrastive Learning. How do you continue learning? Do not just rely on textbooks. Read the primary papers. Follow the preprints on ArXiv from conferences like NeurIPS, ICLR, and ICML. Look at the open-source PyTorch repositories and run the demonstrations yourself. But most importantly, build something. Do an application project that genuinely interests you, whether it is predicting medical anomalies, generating music, or analyzing financial time-series. The truest understanding comes from getting your hands dirty with the data. I want to acknowledge that parts of this course's material and slides were adapted from the phenomenal "Dive into Deep Learning" textbook by Aston Zhang, Mu Li, and others, which I highly recommend as a continuing resource. It has been an absolute privilege teaching you all this semester. Good luck with your final projects, study hard for the exams, and I look forward to seeing the incredible AI systems you will build in your careers. Thank you, and class dismissed.

    当我们在第 11 讲的尾声时，我们也迎来了 DSA 5204 正式课程的结束。我想花一点时间回顾一下本学期我们所经历的不可思议的旅程。几个月前，我们从线性回归的基础数学开始，并获得了深度学习仅仅是提取特征表示的复杂机制这一基本认识。我们从浅层网络走向深度全连接神经网络，揭示了维度灾难和反向传播的机制。我们探索了卷积神经网络，理解了像平移等变性这样的结构先验如何让我们掌握计算机视觉。我们用 LSTM 对抗了循环神经网络的梯度消失，并最终在今天通过发现自注意力和 Transformer 的并行力量，完全超越了循环。一路走来，我们不仅构建了架构；我们还学习了训练它们的严谨科学。我们诊断了过拟合，应用了如 L2 权重衰减和 Dropout 等正则化技术，用批量归一化（Batch Normalization）稳定了激活值，并用随机梯度下降（SGD）和 Adam 优化了我们的损失地貌。我们甚至涉足了生成建模，在数学上剖析了变分自编码器（VAE），并见证了如何映射隐藏的、连续的潜在空间。

    这门课是对深度学习的一次密集、全面的介绍。但请理解，这个领域正以惊人的速度发展，我们只是触及了皮毛。还有大片领域我们没有时间深入探讨，比如驱动现代图像生成的扩散模型（Diffusion Models）的数学优雅性，深度强化学习（Deep Reinforcement Learning）的战略复杂性，或者对比学习（Contrastive Learning）中微妙的数据整理策略。你们该如何继续学习？不要仅仅依赖教科书。去读原始论文。关注 NeurIPS、ICLR 和 ICML 等会议在 ArXiv 上的预印本。去看看开源的 PyTorch 代码库，自己运行一下演示。但最重要的是，去构建一些东西。做一个你真正感兴趣的应用项目，无论是预测医学异常、生成音乐，还是分析金融时间序列。最真实的理解来自于你亲自处理数据的实践。我想声明，本课程的部分材料和幻灯片改编自 Aston Zhang、Mu Li 等人编写的极好的《动手学深度学习（Dive into Deep Learning）》教科书，我强烈推荐它作为后续资源。这学期能教你们所有人是我绝对的荣幸。祝你们的最终项目好运，为考试努力学习，我期待着在你们的职业生涯中看到你们将构建出的令人惊叹的 AI 系统。谢谢，下课。
***
